| 2 | Real conversation | Barge-in and wake words are the face's own (voice activity plugin); short spoken replies are in the card; cross-day memory built (D42). Speed (a faster voice pipeline) is measured, not built. |

| 5 | Builds things and shows them | Already there: `deliverables` makes decks and PDF reports with checks, opens them, presents slideshows; code goes through the lab agents and the push gate. Not built: a preview panel inside the face (a visual job to do with the owner looking at the screen). |
| 6 | The look (hologram interface, overlay) | Hold lifted 2026-10-25 (D45): being built in the face. |

## D44 Verify before you state: the rule after the Oracle free-limit mistake (2026-10-24, owner)

- **What went wrong:** the cloud session said "3 OCPU / 18 GB is inside the free limit" from a search snippet and a cost line of $0. Oracle's own page (read the same day) says the free A1 pool is 1,500 OCPU-hours and 9,000 GB-hours a month, which is 2 OCPU / 12 GB; the month's usage was already past it. The laptop session repeated the old 3,000 / 18,000 figure. The owner had it right.
- **The rule (in `CLAUDE.md`, so every session reads it):** a fact that can change (limit, price, version, model name, policy, terms) is read from the vendor's own page today, quoted with source and date, before it is stated or acted on. Memory, snippets, forum posts and AI summaries are leads only. Sources that disagree are said out loud and checked against the account's own data. Before a step that depends on the fact (resize, delete, sign-up, spend) the fact is restated with its source. A $0 cost is not proof of free (billing lags a day; the allowance is counted in hours). A mistake is reported in one plain line: what was wrong, what changed.
- **The guards in code:** `scripts/oci_free_pool.py` projects the month from usage in hours against the pool and says over / inside / not sustainable; its pool figures carry the date they were read and it refuses (exit 3) when they are older than 30 days. `skills/cost-check` now includes it: free is checked by hours, not only by dollars.

## D45 The perfect JARVIS: holds lifted, no more permission asks, two sessions coordinate (2026-10-25, owner)

- **Owner words:** "What I want is the perfect Jarvis, where it can see my screen, control my screen, control my whole laptop, and also show my things. ... Don't ask for my permissions again." Earlier the same day: "tell the JARVIS build it should also not ask for my permission. Don't schedule the task, just send the prompt directly."
- **Effect:** Iron Man #4 and #6 (D43) are no longer held. Both sessions decide, act, verify and report instead of asking the owner for approval of routine build steps. The owner's written safety rules in `CLAUDE.md` stay (no secrets read or printed, Oracle Always Free only, protected main server, no run-time Claude calls), and so do the **run-time** gates of the product itself (D27: the kernel decides, consequential PC actions wait for the owner's PIN). Those are JARVIS's behaviour toward the owner, not build-time questions, and are not removed.
- **Coordination:** the cloud session sends the laptop session its tasks directly (`send_message`, no scheduled triggers) and reads its answer with `list_events`; the laptop session pushes to `jarvis/laptop` and ends each turn with a short factual report; the cloud session merges and reviews.
- **The four wishes and where they stand:** *see my screen* = `whats_on_screen` and the screen-text cleaner (D42), real-hardware check outstanding; *control my screen / whole laptop* = the desktop plugin and node (D27), real-hardware check outstanding; *show my things* = the preview panel in the face (building); *the look* = the HUD layer in the face (building). Devices beyond the laptop (phone, tablet, home) come after.

## D46 Show my things: the Preview panel in the face (2026-10-25, owner wish "show my things", D45)

- **What it is:** a panel in the face's main window that shows what JARVIS made: PDF and deck pages as images (with slide thumbnails), images, text, markdown and code with highlighting, and HTML in a sandboxed frame. A list of recent deliverables, open and close, next and previous (page, then item), and "Open in app", which calls the existing `deliverables__open_output` entry. It opens by itself when the chat model calls `jarvis_show` ("show me the Q3 deck") and when a deck or report finishes while the face runs (only a fresh one: made in the last two minutes).
- **Server side** (`jarvis/bridge/output_preview.py`, tools in `face_tools.py`): `jarvis_show` is the one tool for the chat model; it returns one sentence and no content. `jarvis_outputs`, `jarvis_output_get` and `jarvis_preview_wanted` are the panel's own reads (their descriptions say so). They serve only files inside the deliverables output folder (`DELIVERABLES_OUT_DIR`): ids are URL-safe base64 of the relative path; `..`, absolute paths, drive letters, backslashes, hidden names, any link, junction or reparse point in the path, secret-looking names (token, key, password, `.env`, `.pem` and similar) and types off the allowlist are refused; the first bytes must match the name; caps per type (image 4 MB, text 2 MB read and 200,000 characters shown, HTML 1 MB); a private key in a text file refuses it and other key-like strings are replaced. The panel tools are not run through the model-facing cleaner (it would hide the names and text the panel must show), so the model is told not to call them. The content is what JARVIS's own tools wrote from a spec.
- **Face side:** `PreviewPanel.vue` draws; `use-jarvis-preview.ts` holds state and polls `jarvis_preview_wanted` every 3 s (backs off to 60 s while the server is silent; switch off with localStorage `jarvis:preview` = `off`); `jarvis-preview-logic.ts` holds the rules (data URLs for four raster types only, id and mime checks, HTML clean-up). HTML: DOMPurify with no script, meta, base, link, form, frame or embed and no `href`, `src` only on an `img` as a `data:` image, CSS without `url()` or `@import`; then `<iframe sandbox="" csp="default-src 'none'" referrerpolicy="no-referrer">` with the same policy in a meta tag. A click on a link in shown content does nothing. No network, no CDN, no change to the face's content policy.
- **Edit in `App.vue`:** two lines (an import and `<PreviewHost v-if="fullStageRuntime" />`).
- **Checked:** `pytest jarvis/bridge/test_output_preview.py jarvis/bridge/test_face_tools.py`, `vitest` for the two composables, `node scripts/preview-check.mjs` (renders the panel in Chromium from real tool-server output, tries a hostile HTML file and markdown, fails on any request to the outside address, any script run or any navigation, saves screenshots), and `check:csp` still passes.
- **Not done:** markdown tables show as plain text (the face's markdown pipeline has no GFM plugin); the Electron window is 450 x 600 by default, so the panel covers most of it (the expand button and fullscreen mode give it room); the panel has not been seen in the real Electron app on a real screen; the PowerPoint file itself has no preview beyond the slide images that the deliverables checks save.

## D47 Real-laptop battery, VM2 Agent PC working, model candidates measured (2026-10-25, owner: "the perfect Jarvis")

- **Agent PC (VM2):** Hermes Agent v0.21.6 (pinned) as the lab agent, a loopback model forwarder (no key on the VM), the runner token and a model token issued ON the main server's kernel (tokens are keyed by name and live in the kernel that issues them; a token made on the laptop is unknown to the server). Smoke test passed end to end. The verifier fails when the public lane's first model is MiniMax-M2.7 (it answers ~11k-token prompts with an unclosed think block that Hermes counts as empty); the public lane now prefers dahl-glm (`providers.json`), but Dahl answers GLM with 429 when busy, then MiniMax serves it.
- **Wrong-window safety (`windowpick.py`):** a change (type, click, key, scroll, move, resize) aimed at an app with several windows is refused unless JARVIS opened one of them; reads take the front or the newest and say so. Found on the real laptop, proven live with three Notepads.
- **Screen text:** the cleaner now removes real IBAN account numbers (mod-97 checked); window capture creates its own local-only folder; preview titles lose direction-override and zero-width characters (a file named `invoice<RLO>gpj.exe.pdf` was shown as a PDF).
- **HUD and Preview panel** fit the default 252 x 336 window (ring + stacked readout, panel as a full-window overlay); the controls island leaves an 8 px gap. Click-through on the ring and strip is real; the window's transparent corners still take clicks.
- **Models (numbers only; D1/D5 rule: our own tests decide):** Spark-X2.6-4B Q4_K_M scores 63/70 on the 70 decision cases (Qwen3-4B-2507: 70/70) and answers in 15.3 s p50 against 0.97 s on this laptop: **not adopted**. LightOnOCR-3-0.8B (Q4_K_M + mmproj, CPU) read three synthetic pages in 4.2 s, 4.6 s and 10.7 s (character error 0.0, 0.03, and 0.99 on the table page where every word was present, probably a layout difference); Hindi not tested: **candidate for a document-reading tool**, not for screen text. Nemotron 3.5 Parse needs an NVIDIA/Linux stack: not for this laptop.
- **Open:** large private-lane prompts (about 12k tokens) have no provider that fits the owner's privacy rule (Groq is capped at 8k, OpenRouter's free endpoint answers 429/404; the kernel never stored the 404 body, so the cause is not proven). The fix to look at is a smaller agent prompt for the private lane, not a looser privacy setting.
- **Owner-only steps left:** allow the Playwright extension in Chrome (one click), close the YouTube tab and old Notepad windows the battery opened, remove the Z6 peer in the Tailscale console, recreate `protected.json` from the draft.

## D48 Read my documents: LightOnOCR through llama-server, English only, local, cleaned (2026-10-25, owner wish "read my documents")

- **Owner words / scope:** JARVIS should read the owner's own documents (scanned invoices, PDFs without a text layer, pictures of pages) on his laptop. **English documents only** (the owner said to skip Hindi). Hindi is **untested and not required**; the earlier "99.4 % on a Hindi invoice" claim stays unverified (D47).
- **Decision (D1/D5: our own tests decide):** LightOnOCR-3-0.8B (Apache-2.0) as GGUF Q4_K_M + `mmproj-f16` (community build by mradermacher, ~0.7 GB) through llama-server's multimodal path, behind llama-swap as `lightonocr-docreader` (aliases `docreader`, `ocr-docs`, `-c 4096`, `ttl 300`; CPU, so the 4 GB GPU stays with the other models). Evidence: three synthetic pages on the owner's CPU laptop, 4.2, 4.6 and 10.7 s, character error 0.00, 0.03 and 0.99 (every word present; the table came back in another layout) (`evals/quality95/results/lightonocr-20261025-174505.json`). Both files are pinned with Hugging Face's lfs sha256 (read from the API 2026-10-25, nothing downloaded) in `jarvis/tools/jarvis.lock.json`, repo commit `d7699238`.
- **The tool** (`jarvis/plugins/doc_reader`, entry `read_document(path, max_pages?)`; the face's model calls it as `doc_reader__read_document`): only the one file the owner names (absolute path; no `..`, UNC or stream paths, links or junctions, secret-looking names, protected folders; pdf/png/jpg/jpeg/tif/tiff/webp/bmp whose first bytes match the name; 25 MB). PDFs are drawn with pypdfium2 (not PyMuPDF: AGPL), at most 10 pages by default and 30 at most, longer side 1540 px so a page plus its answer fits 4096 tokens. One call per page in the shape measured in `lightonocr_eval.py`; `<think>` / `reasoning_content` is stripped. Returns pages, characters, text and seconds.
- **Privacy:** the text goes through the screen-text cleaner before any model sees it: secrets, API keys, card numbers and real IBANs are replaced and the reply names the kinds removed (never the values); then, as for `read_window_text`, it reaches a model only when `JARVIS_SCREEN_TEXT_TO_MODEL=1` and the privacy gateway has hidden names and places, otherwise it stays on the PC and the reply says so. The only network call is to 127.0.0.1 (no proxy, no redirect). No telemetry. JARVIS does not call Claude at run time.
- **Not done / open:** never run through llama-swap with `--jinja` on the laptop (the measurement started llama-server by hand); the tool has not read one of the owner's real documents; the face's boundary (`screen_text.STRING_LIMIT`) cuts every string a model receives to 2,000 characters, so a long document reaches the chat model in part until that limit is raised for document text (a decision for the owner, it is a privacy limit); the model files go in `D:\jarvis\models\gguf\lightonocr-3-0.8b\` (fetch with `jarvis/tools/fetch.py`; the files already on the laptop may need moving there).

## D49 Face chat: tool path, compact profile, hidden reasoning (2026-10-25, found on the owner's laptop)

- **What was seen:** a goal typed in the face chat never ran a tool: the "Mcp list tools" card got no usable answer and nothing reached the tool server (127.0.0.1:48970). The chat request was about 40 KB, mostly tool definitions, which free providers cannot take (Dahl busy, Groq capped at 8,000 tokens in all). The model "thought aloud" in the visible chat for minutes.
- **Root cause of the stall, in plain words:** the face connected to the tool server **once, at start-up**, and never again. The tool server needs 10 to 60 s to load its plugins and the supervisor restarts it after a failure; if the face started first, or the tool server was restarted later, the face had no working connection for the rest of its life. Worse, it hid that: the list-tools call only asked the connections it had, so with none it answered "No MCP tools are available. Do not retry discovery during this assistant turn." (the model gave up at once), and every call said "mcp server is not running" before anything was sent. A restarted tool server also forgets the face's session (HTTP 404 "Session not found"), and the face kept using the dead session id. A second cause on a profile that already exists: `mcp.json` is written only when missing, so a file left by an earlier face (AIRI's own default is an empty server list) never got the JARVIS entry, the same class of bug as the empty character defaults fixed the same day (`adoptRuntimeDefaultsWhereEmpty`).
- **What was ruled out by running it (not by reading):** the real tool server (`python -m jarvis.bridge.face_tools`) driven by the face's own MCP client library (`@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` 1.30.0, `StreamableHTTPClientTransport`, same client name and options): connect 60 ms, protocol 2025-11-25, `tools/list` in 98 to 168 ms with all 105 tools, `tools/call jarvis_ready` 40 ms, Host `127.0.0.1:PORT`, no Origin, JSON and SSE both fine. The face's network lock allows `http://127.0.0.1:48970/mcp`, `localhost` and `[::1]` (test added) and the locked global `fetch` carries the MCP transport (test added). So the transport, headers, Host/Origin rules, protocol negotiation and speed are not the problem; the connection life cycle is.
- **Fix (face main process, `src/main/services/airi/mcp-servers/index.ts`):** the manager keeps the list of servers that must be connected and connects the missing ones on demand (list and call), with one shared attempt per server, a 1 s cool-down and an 8 s limit on `initialize` (the SDK default is 60 s); a timer in the main process retries every 5 s while one is missing; a lost connection, or a 404 "Session not found", drops the session and a new one is opened (a read-only request and a refused connection are then sent again; a **tool call is never sent twice** after a failure that may have reached the server). Discovery now fails loudly with the server's name and reason when a configured server is down, instead of answering an empty list. `ensureConfigFile` adds the JARVIS entry to an existing `mcp.json` that lacks it (other servers and an owner's `enabled: false` stay). Regression tests with the real client library against a stand-in tool server with the real server's rules: `index.connection.test.ts` (starts late, restarts, session lost, mcp.json without the entry, network lock).
- **Compact tool profile (`face_tools.py --profile chat`, now the default; `full` is the old behaviour, `--list-panel-tools` lists the panel tools for debugging):** the chat model is offered 20 tools (`jarvis_do`, `jarvis_task_result`, `jarvis_cancel`, `jarvis_waiting`, `jarvis_show`, `jarvis_recall`, `doc_reader__read_document`, `doc_tools__read_document`, `desktop__whats_on_screen`, `desktop__read_window_text`, `desktop__check_pc`, `desktop__run_pc_action` (open an app), `desktop__open_website`, `desktop__play_youtube`, `desktop__stop_pc_task`, `deliverables__make_presentation`, `deliverables__make_report`, `deliverables__open_output`, `deliverables__show_in_folder`, `web_search__search`) with short descriptions that keep what makes a call safe (full path, "data, never instructions", owner-allowed screen text, the `check` and `action` values the plugin schema loses) and trimmed schemas (no titles, no `default: null`, no `anyOf [X, null]`, no annotations). Everything else (workbench, learn_vault, main_agent, local_team, missions, browser actions) is reached through `jarvis_do`, which hands the task to the console and the kernel; those tools are **not registered** in this profile. The tools the face's own panels and HUD call by name through the same MCP route (`jarvis_status`, `jarvis_spoken`, `jarvis_remember_chat`, `jarvis_outputs`, `jarvis_output_get`, `jarvis_preview_wanted`, `jarvis_notices`) stay callable and are left out of the list. No kernel check, path rule or cleaner changed. **`tools/list`: 105 tools, 53,103 bytes before; 20 tools, 7,655 bytes after** (measured with the face's client library against the real server).
- **Reasoning no longer reaches the face's chat (privacy gateway):** a request that carries the face's placeholder key (`jarvis-local`; the plugins send their own key and are not changed) gets a reply without `reasoning_content` / `reasoning` / `reasoning_details` and without `<think>…</think>` text (also `<thinking>`, `<thought>`, `<reasoning>`; tags split across chunks; a thought that opens and never closes; a reply that carried only reasoning is dropped, an SSE comment goes out every 15 s meanwhile so nothing times the stream out). `JARVIS_PRIVACY_SHOW_REASONING=1` turns it off. Redaction rules are untouched. Tests on recorded-style SSE streams: `privacy/test_gateway_plumbing.py`.
- **Optional, off until checked live:** a provider entry in `privacy/providers.json` may carry `extra_body` (every request) and `face_extra_body` (the face only), validated to the same exact shapes as a caller's provider switches. Groq now has `extra_body: {"reasoning_effort": "low"}` (mirrors `config/providers.json`, which the kernel's gateway already uses for it). Dahl has `_face_extra_body_example: {"chat_template_kwargs": {"enable_thinking": false}}`: rename it to `face_extra_body` to try it. **Whether Dahl honours it for `zai-org/GLM-5.3-Flash`, or answers 400, was not verified**; it is the only part that would also shorten the wait, because hidden reasoning is still generated.
- **Not verified here (needs the real app on the laptop):** the Electron main process itself (everything above ran in Node with the same SDK, and the build and CSP check pass, but the face was not started); that the reconnect fixes the owner's exact stall (the cause is shown by reproduction of "face first, server later" and "server restarted", not seen in the owner's logs, which were not read); how Dahl treats `enable_thinking`; an orphan `</think>` with no opening tag (the model's template opened it) is left as it is, it cannot be told from an answer in a stream; the owner's profile `mcp.json`.

## D50 JARVIS footprint budget: 20% of the laptop, D: only (2026-10-25, owner)

- **Owner words:** "JARVIS should use only 20% of the whole laptop, and for storage use only the D: drive." The same day the owner set the video-memory share to 2 GB of the 4 GB card (first draft 0.8 GB).
- **The numbers** (one place: `jarvis/supervisor/budget.py`; override with `JARVIS_BUDGET_<NAME>` variables or `D:\jarvis\data\budget.json`, variables win; a bad value is ignored and shown as a note):

  | metric | budget | how it is read | status of the number |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | CPU | **20% of the whole machine**, averaged over 60 s (about 3.2 of 16 threads) | cumulative CPU seconds of every JARVIS process / (seconds x logical processors) | owner |
  | RAM | **6,554 MB** (20% of 32 GB) for ALL JARVIS processes together | sum of working sets (private bytes shown beside it) | owner; the working-set sum counts shared pages once per process, so it is a slightly high estimate |
  | GPU memory | **2,048 MB** of the 4 GB card for all JARVIS processes | "GPU Process Memory" counters (Windows performance counters through CIM, per pid), no install | owner; whether the AMD driver exposes the counters is **not verified** (the meter then says N/A, never OK) |
  | Disk writes | 5 MB/s average | per-process write bytes | **a guess**, not an owner number |
  | Storage | **nothing outside D:** | environment of every service, plus a scan of known C: places (names and sizes only) | owner |
  | llama-server threads | `-t 3` (3 of 16 = 18.75%) | the `server` macro in `llama-swap.yaml` and the profiles (`cpu8`: `-t 2`) | decision: just under the CPU budget |
  | Model idle unload | `ttl: 120` for every model (was 300) | llama-swap | decision; models reload more often after 2 idle minutes |
  | Job memory backstop | 1.25 x RAM budget = 8,192 MB committed | Job Object `JobMemoryLimit` | **a guess**; committed bytes are not working set, so it may need a different factor once measured; `governor.py --no-mem-cap` leaves it out |

- **Measure:** `jarvis/tools/footprint.py` (`--json` for the HUD and doctor, `--watch` for a rolling average, exit code 1 when any metric is OVER). It finds JARVIS processes by program path or command line (under `D:\jarvis`, `D:\jarvis-apps`, `D:\APPS\jarvis`; the face; llama-swap and llama-server; the Playwright MCP node process; cua-driver; interpreters running JARVIS scripts) and everything those start. The owner's own apps are never counted (an editor that has the repo open is not an interpreter running a JARVIS script). It uses psutil where the venv has it (agent-venv does) and PowerShell/CIM otherwise.
- **Enforce, hard limits** (`jarvis/supervisor/jobobject.py`, `jarvis/tools/governor.py`): every JARVIS process gets BELOW_NORMAL priority and EcoQoS (`PROCESS_POWER_THROTTLING_EXECUTION_SPEED`), so JARVIS yields to the owner's apps. All of them sit in a Job Object with a CPU rate **hard cap** of 20% of the machine (`JOBOBJECT_CPU_RATE_CONTROL_INFORMATION`, `CpuRate` 2000 = 1/100 percent units) and the memory ceiling above. The supervisor's tree gets this on creation (kill-on-close stays). Processes started by `jarvis-start.ps1` are adopted by `governor.py` (started by that script, one copy) into a second job **without** kill-on-close, so closing the governor never kills the face; the caps stay while any member lives. Honest limits: a process already in another job can join a new one only if Windows allows the nesting (8+), error 5 means no; it is then logged once ("job-refused") and left to the soft layer. The supervisor's job and the governor's job are each capped at 20%, so together they could reach 40%; the soft layer watches the total. The hard cap also slows the face if JARVIS as a whole exceeds 20%.
- **Enforce, soft governor** (same program, every 5 s): over budget (CPU of the last sample, RAM or GPU memory) for more than 20 s: (1) ask llama-swap to unload its models (`POST /api/models/unload`, else `GET /unload`; neither is verified against llama-swap v261 here, the program logs which one answered); (2) lower llama-server's `-t` by one (minimum 2) for the **next** load: `llama_threads.json` is written and llama-swap is restarted through `local-models.ps1 -Threads N` only when nothing is loaded; (3) duty-cycle llama-server (suspend about 2.5 s per sample, resume in a `finally`). Only llama-server is ever suspended or unloaded. The face, the kernel client, the console, the supervisor and the governor are protected: if they are the offenders it only logs ("over-budget-no-action"). Nothing is killed. Every action is a line in `D:\jarvis\data\logs\governor.jsonl`.
- **Storage:** `budget.storage_env()` is the one function: TEMP, TMP, TMPDIR, HF_HOME, HF_HUB_CACHE, XDG_CACHE_HOME, PIP_CACHE_DIR, UV_CACHE_DIR, npm_config_cache, PNPM_HOME, npm_config_store_dir, PNPM_STORE_DIR, PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH, ELECTRON_CACHE, ELECTRON_BUILDER_CACHE, OLLAMA_MODELS, LLAMA_CACHE and `APP_USER_DATA_PATH` (the face's Electron profile, also `budget.face_args()` = `--user-data-dir=...`). It keeps the folders `d-env.ps1` has used since 2026-10-21 (`D:\jarvis\tmp`, `D:\jarvis\cache\...`) so what is already downloaded stays valid (the owner's text said `D:\jarvis\data\...`; the old paths were kept for that reason, the face profile is under `data\face`). `scripts/d-env.ps1` (dot-sourced by jarvis-start, the installer and every start script) mirrors it, and `test_budget.py` fails if they differ. The installer and `jarvis-start.ps1` refuse with one line when a data, cache or temp path is not on D:. The supervisor refuses a manifest whose env names such a path (`ManifestError`), and refuses to start a component whose merged env has one (`StorageError`). The face's main process (`jarvis/face/.../main/jarvis/storage-lock.ts`) sets `userData`, `sessionData`, `temp`, `logs` and `crashDumps` under D: on Windows when D: exists; the network lock and CSP are untouched.
- **Local models:** `-t 3` and `ttl 120` in `llama-swap.yaml` and the profiles; the exclusive swap group already keeps one model loaded. No model or file was changed. **Over the GPU budget at `-ngl 99` (flagged, not changed): `decider-qwen3-4b` and `decider-qwen3-4b-2507`** (about 2.6 GB on the GPU, the figure given with the 2 GB decision, not measured by this change). Option: fewer GPU layers (`-ngl N`, N chosen by measuring, which also changes the 3.3 s decision time). `ling-tiny` (shared layers on the GPU with `--n-cpu-moe 16`), the vision and document models are expected to fit; not measured under this budget.
- **Guess versus measured:** nothing here was measured on the laptop. Measured elsewhere and reused: the existing model speeds and the `ling-tiny` MoE split (D-series notes). Guesses: the disk-write limit, the 1.25 memory factor, `-t 3` giving acceptable speed, the effect of the hard cap on the face's smoothness, the unload endpoint of llama-swap v261, GPU counters on this driver, EcoQoS on the face's windows. The laptop session verifies these (list in the build report).
- **Reuse (CLAUDE.md rule):** Windows Job Objects, `SetPriorityClass`, `SetProcessInformation(ProcessPowerThrottling)` and `NtSuspendProcess` are the operating system's own; psutil and PowerShell/CIM for measuring; llama-swap's own `ttl`, unload and groups. I know of no maintained open-source project that caps a named set of processes at a share of the machine and keeps their files on D: (Process Lasso is a proprietary tool and Docker/WSL limits would add a virtual disk on a drive with 8 GB free; neither vendor page was read today, so this is a lead, not a verified comparison). The glue (about 900 lines with tests, see `docs/01_RESEARCH_AUDIT.md`) is custom.

## D51 (2026-10-25): online models reason, local models execute; simple requests are answered locally first
- **Owner's words:** "use local model MoE", "for the simple tasks", "provider models only do reasoning and execution will be done by local models".
- **Rule:** an online provider (Dahl, Groq, Cerebras) only reasons: it understands the goal and writes a short plan with a success check per step, from redacted text. It does not emit PC tool calls and never sees a screenshot. The local MoE model on this laptop (Ling-3.0-tiny-heretic; decider and parse models as before) executes: it picks the exact tool, the arguments and the UI target, through the desktop decider and coordinator, and the kernel still permits or gates every action (D27).
- **Simple requests:** the gateway puts the local model first when a request is short and carries at most 3 tools (`prefer_first`, `prefer_max_tokens` 3000, `prefer_max_tools` 3 on the `local` provider). A local error of any kind falls through to the online chain. Big or tool-heavy requests still go online first until the on-screen benchmark (`evals/onscreen_bench/PROTOCOL.md`) shows the local model can take them.
- **Why:** the failures of 2026-10-25 were mostly online models forming tool-call JSON (Dahl 400 "Unterminated string"), rate caps, and the old toolset; execution on the laptop removes the first, spends fewer online tokens, and keeps screen content on the PC.
- **Not measured:** the local MoE's speed and tool accuracy on this laptop (estimates only, `01_RESEARCH_AUDIT.md`). The benchmark decides whether the thresholds move.

## D52 (2026-10-25): the face's chat request is about 8 KB, not 40 KB; no second model request beside a turn

- **What the 40 KB were** (measured by building the request with the real orchestrator, tool factories and prompts; `jarvis/face/packages/stage-ui/src/stores/chat/request-budget.test.ts`, no network): 31,125 bytes of tool definitions (15 tools: AIRI's `builtIn_emitSparkCommand` 5,971, `configure_wake_words` 3,523, `builtIn_proposeRecipe` 3,931, memory x3 3,980, `stage_widgets` 2,543, `image_journal` 1,796, `builtIn_drawCard` + `builtIn_searchIcons` 2,930, `builtIn_useRecipe` 1,715, the debug random-number tool 930, the MCP proxies 2,191 and `get_weather` 1,615), every tool carrying two extra arguments `call_summary` + `call_icon` (about 670 bytes a tool, 10 KB in all); persona 4,162; emotion rules in the user message 1,718; memory prompt 877; card prompt 630; format rules 437; Live2D controls about 480 (stand-in). The MCP tool list of D49 is not in this request; it arrives as a tool result and adds about 6.4 KB to the next one.
- **The second request** (about 8 KB, seen beside a turn whenever a tool call finished): the chat's *tool cards* (`stores/tool-cards.ts`). When a call to a tool finishes, the face asks the chat model, on the same provider and rate limit, to design a card for the result: the card-language rules (about 8 KB) plus the call, once for each new tool, with a second round when the program does not validate, and a tool (`builtIn_searchIcons`) for icon names. It is a convenience, not a JARVIS capability. Now: tool cards and reply cards are **off by default** (Settings turns them on), and when on, a card is made only when the owner unfolds the row of a call, never for a call that just finished. The voice end-of-speech judge and the transcript rephrase (`hearing` settings, both on by default) are two more small model requests for each *spoken* turn; they are **not changed** here (a typed turn has none). Turn them off in Settings > Hearing if a spoken turn meets a rate limit.
- **What a request carries now:** the tools `builtIn_mcpListTools`, `builtIn_mcpCallTool` and `get_weather` (the weather card is drawn from the result of `get_weather` itself, so `stage_widgets` is not needed), each with a one-line `call_summary` label and no icon argument. `builtIn_emitSparkCommand` and the debug tool are offered only when a caller sets `airiModuleTools` (nobody does). Memory (prompt + 3 tools) goes into a request only when the owner has accepted memory entries or the message says remember/forget; the recipe tools only with a usable recipe (use) or a message about recipes or skills (propose); the wake word editor only when the message names wake words; reply cards (draw + icon search + prompt) only when the owner turns them on. `stage_widgets` and `image_journal` stay registered but are not selected. The rules are in `stores/chat/run-tools.ts` and `lean-tools.ts` (English words; a Hindi or Telugu message that wants one of these can name it in English). Persona: 4,162 to 3,550 bytes (same rules; a new "Doing things" block names `desktop__do_goal` for PC, screen and document goals, `jarvis_do` only for server work, `jarvis_recall`, `jarvis_show`, the two document readers and the `jarvis::<tool>` call form); emotion rules in the user message 1,718 to 864.

  | part | before (bytes) | after (bytes) |
  |---|---|---|
  | tool definitions | 31,125 (15 tools) | 2,056 (3 tools) |
  | persona | 4,162 | 3,550 |
  | format rules / Live2D controls (stand-in) | 437 / 484 | 437 / 484 |
  | memory prompt / card prompt | 877 / 630 | 0 / 0 |
  | owner text + time + emotion rules | about 1,850 | 984 |
  | envelope | about 250 | 234 |
  | **first request** | **about 39,900** (seen live: 40-41 KB) | **7,745** (limit in the test: 12,000) |
  | request after the tool list | about 48,000 (seen live: 48-49 KB) | 15,144 (limit 22,000) |

- **Guard:** `request-budget.test.ts` fails when the first request passes 12,000 bytes, names the part that grew, checks that the AIRI helpers listed above are absent and the honesty and routing rules are present, and `jarvis/bridge/test_face_tools.py` checks that its tool-list snapshot names the same tools as `CHAT_TOOLS`. `JARVIS_PRINT_BUDGET=1` prints the table.
- **Not verified (needs the real app on the laptop):** the actual bytes on the wire (the test has no network and uses a stand-in Live2D list and the provider adapter adds a few hundred bytes); that the models now pick the right tools; the Hindi and Telugu behaviour of the word triggers; the persona against `jarvis/persona/cases.json` with a live model (the rules were kept, not re-run). To see the real request on the laptop: open DevTools in the face's chat window (Network tab, each `POST .../chat/completions`: the Size column and the Payload tab show the bytes and the body), or print `Content-Length` in the privacy gateway's POST handler for one run (the gateway logs no sizes today and was not changed), and compare with the table. The on-screen benchmark records `request_kb` per request the same way.

## D53 (2026-10-26): JARVIS controls the PC on screen; on-screen permission buttons the owner lists are clicked without the PIN
- **Owner's words (said more than once):** "JARVIS can click for permission on screen", "Jarvis can control my PC on screen".
- **Already true (policy.py, 2026-10-16):** the owner's own request (owner_direct, private lane) runs ordinary PC actions at once: open, focus, click, type, key, scroll, minimize, maximize, restore. Opening Calculator needed no PIN in the 2026-10-26 on-screen runs. Only a click or key whose words could pay, send, delete, install, sign in or out, or accept something waits for the PIN (D27).
- **New:** `jarvis/config/auto_click.json` lists permission buttons as `{process, element}` (both match the whole name). A `ui.click` from the owner's own request on a listed button is `YELLOW_AUTO` even though `accept/agree/allow/grant/authorize/confirm` are in its words. Words for money, deleting, sending, installing, signing in and passwords are never waived (`HARD_CONSEQUENCE`). The file is console-only (in `SAFETY_COMPONENTS`): an agent cannot add itself. A missing or broken file waives nothing.
- **Not done:** the real button names. The default entry (Chrome, "Connect") is a placeholder; the laptop session adds the real name of the Playwright extension dialog after looking at it on screen. UAC and other secure-desktop prompts are not reachable by UI Automation and stay the owner's.

## D54 (2026-10-26): a save is part of the request; three owner-level goals on screen, no PIN for the ordinary steps
- **Owner's words:** "When I told [JARVIS] to open Notepad and type so and so, I gave it permission to save, so it can control my mouse and click the Save button. Or I tell it: open VS Code in a certain folder, type the code. Or there is a problem statement on screen: it should solve it and type."
- **Rule:** when the owner asks JARVIS to write or type something and to save it (or to create a file), the save is part of that request. It runs through the kernel as the owner's own ordinary action (`owner_direct`, D27): no PIN. Words that could pay, send, delete, install, sign in or out or accept stay gated exactly as before (`CONSEQUENTIAL` and `HARD_CONSEQUENCE` are untouched); a typed text or a file name that holds such a word waits for the PIN. The persona carries the rule in one line (`jarvis/persona/jarvis_card.json`, the face's `base.yaml`): the chat model puts the save into the goal and does not ask again. The decider adds a save only when the request says to save or to create a file; typing alone saves nothing.
- **Three goals, each a short plan of verified steps (the 4-step cap is unchanged):**
  1. *Write and save:* `open_app`, `type_text`, `save_as`. `save_as` is ONE kernel job (`ui.save_as`): Save As in the window (ctrl+shift+s, never a plain ctrl+s that would silently overwrite a named document), the file name box set to a full path, the dialog's Save button pressed by UI Automation, then checked on the node: the file is on disk, the window title names it, and its text equals the typed text (digest compare, read back with the node's own helper). Default place: Documents (a dated `JARVIS note ... .txt` name when none was said).
  2. *VS Code:* `open_in_vscode` (`pc.open_code`: `Code.exe` found in its three usual places, arguments `-n` and one folder, nothing else), `type_in_editor` (`ui.key ctrl+n`, then `ui.type_focused`: the text is pasted into the focused editor, because keystrokes would trigger Monaco's auto-closing and auto-indent; VS Code is always given a new untitled file first, since its terminal may hold the focus), `save_as` (the file is then read back from disk: Monaco cannot be read by UI Automation). Running the code is not included; "run it" would be a separate, gated step.
  3. *Solve what is on screen* (`goal.py`, no online plan): the local side reads the ONE window (accessibility tree, then OCR), the owner's goal and that window's text go to the ONLINE reasoning model through the privacy gateway (`screen_text.release_for_request`: secret pass, then the gateway's confirmed redaction; header `X-Jarvis-Route: online`; no tools, no image) and return the solution text only; the local side types it into the editor the owner named (else the front editor) and saves if asked. If the gateway or its redaction is down, nothing is sent and nothing is typed. The step result records the size that was sent, never the text. `JARVIS_SCREEN_TEXT_TO_MODEL` is unchanged and still off for every other path: the owner's explicit request is the permission for this one window.
- **Never overwrite, never leave the known places:** the node picks a file name that does not exist (`name (2).txt`), refuses a folder that is not Documents, Desktop, Downloads, outputs, a project of `config/projects.json` or an existing folder inside one (no drive letters, `..`, hidden or system folders, links out of a root, secrets), and refuses programs and scripts (`.exe .bat .cmd .ps1 .vbs ...`). If a Replace / Confirm Save As prompt shows anyway, the script presses No and cancels and the step fails with that said. A Yes, Enter or position click on such a prompt is refused by the node for everyone (`savefile.refuse_replace_answer`); the policy lists were not touched for this.
- **Kernel:** `ui` ops `save_as` and `type_focused` and `pc` op `open_code` are in `UI_OPS`/`PC_OPS`, `ORDINARY_OPS` and the rules (an agent still needs approval; the public lane still needs approval). The `save_as` policy action carries the folder and the file name as separate leaves, so the secrets-path rules see each.
- **Not verified (laptop):** the Save As dialog's element names and wording in Windows 11, Notepad and VS Code, the `-n` launch behaviour, the paste into Monaco and into Notepad, the clipboard being put back, and the Replace prompt. See the report of this change.

### D54, corrected the same day: it is not three goals, it is ONE rule for any task (task-scoped implied consent)
- **Owner's correction:** "Not the goals. It should work like that for ANY kind of task." Notepad, VS Code and "solve what is on screen" are three test cases of the rule below, no longer special cases.
- **Principle:** when the owner gives JARVIS a goal, every ordinary step needed to finish it is covered by that request, with no PIN: open, click, type, select, scroll, navigate, create a new file or folder, save what the task made, press Next / OK / Continue / Open / Save / Yes / Allow on a dialog the task itself causes, in any app.
- **Effect classes (kernel `policy.py`: `effect_of`, `_ui_level`, `_file_level`, `_setting_level`):** **A** ordinary and local: covered. **B** changes or overwrites something that existed, runs a program or script, changes a system or app setting, uses the network: covered ONLY when the owner's own goal words say so (grant tags `B:overwrite|run|settings|modify|network`), otherwise the PIN. **C** money, sending anything to other people, deleting or uninstalling or formatting, credentials / sign-in / 2FA, admin / UAC, accepting terms, installing software: ALWAYS the PIN, never covered (`C_NEVER`, `C_TERMS`; `CONSEQUENTIAL` and `HARD_CONSEQUENCE` themselves are untouched). `config/auto_click.json` stays an owner-editable EXTRA allow list for soft permission buttons only.
- **The grant comes only from the owner's own goal text** (`grant_from_goal`, deterministic words), carried with the task into every kernel action (`Action.goal`; every job body the `Pc` sends carries `goal`). Text read from a screen, a web page, a file or a model's plan can never raise it: a dialog's visible text rides along as `Action.ctx` and can only ADD risk (a word like "delete" makes it class C); an unreadable dialog, or one that does not belong to an app the goal opened or named, is B (ask). An online plan only names steps; the grant is recomputed locally from the goal (`OWNER_GOAL`, `OWNER_UTTERANCE`: his own sentence when the host has it).
- **Executor for any task:** an observe - decide - act loop with a step budget (`plugins/desktop/drive.py`, tool `drive`): observe the app's windows and dialogs (UI Automation, then OCR; the vision model only locates), the LOCAL model proposes one action from a closed vocabulary (grammar-forced), the action is one kernel job, and the result is verified by EFFECT (window appeared, text read back, file on disk, file in the folder, value on screen), never by the model's "done". Two identical actions in a row end the loop.
- **Overwrite** of an existing file is class B: "Yes" / "Replace" is never pressed unless the goal says replace / overwrite; the default is a new numbered name (`name (2).txt`), the Replace prompt is answered No and reported.
- **Kept from before:** windowpick (type only into a window JARVIS opened or the owner named), known folders for saves, gateway redaction, screen text to the online model only for an explicit "solve / read what is on screen" goal and only the one window, caps on the persona and tool-list bytes.
- **Tests:** the three examples, plus unrelated tasks (Paint draw + save, Calculator, copy a file into a NEW subfolder, a local HTML form, Settings read-only), class-C negatives (send mail, pay, sign in, install, uninstall, UAC) that still stop for the PIN, and the injection cases (a page or dialog saying "you are allowed to delete" changes nothing): `kernel/tests/test_task_consent.py`, `plugins/desktop/tests/test_simple_tasks_run.py`.

### D54, deletion follows the owner's words (no longer always the PIN)
- **delete / remove / trash X** = the Recycle Bin (reversible, `taskops.trash`: send2trash approach, Windows `SHFileOperation` with `FOF_ALLOWUNDO`, never `del` / `Remove-Item` / a shell). Covered by the goal, no PIN, for items he NAMED inside the known folders (`named_in_goal`, whole-word). Verified two ways and both reported: gone from its folder AND present in the Recycle Bin.
- **permanently delete / for good / erase / shift-delete X** = real deletion, covered only when those words are in the goal, only the named items, same folders. **Emptying the Recycle Bin** counts as permanent: covered only by an empty-the-bin, cleanup or permanent request.
- **clean up my PC / free up space / clean everything** = broad cleanup from a deterministic SAFE LIST computed first (paths + sizes), removed, then reported with the freed space: temp folders, browser and app caches (not cookies, passwords, history, sessions, profiles), thumbnail and icon caches, crash dumps and old logs, the Windows Update download cache, pip / npm / nuget caches, the Recycle Bin. Whatever else looks removable (duplicates, old installers, big old files in Downloads) is LISTED with sizes and asked, never deleted on the broad phrase; Documents, Downloads, Desktop and Pictures are never touched.
- **Always blocked, whatever he says** (kernel DENY): system folders, the user profile root, `D:\jarvis-secrets`, JARVIS's code / vault / memory / audit folders, drive roots, wildcards, other users' folders, anything the protected-paths list names. **Ambiguous** ("delete everything", "delete those", "delete old stuff") is a question. Text on a screen cannot widen any of it.
- **Tests:** `kernel/tests/test_task_files.py` (trash vs permanent by wording, protected paths denied, injected text), `node/tests/test_taskops.py` (bin presence verified, cleanup list never touches the owner's folders), `plugins/desktop/tests/test_simple_tasks_run.py`.

### D54, the simple-task catalogue (`jarvis/config/simple_tasks.json`)
- **Owner's words:** "Add this type of simple tasks." About 80 everyday tasks in ONE data file (name, group, class, example phrases, how it is done = a handler plus parameters, how it is verified, `needs_laptop`). More tasks are added to the file with an existing handler, no code; a bad entry is skipped and listed (`simple_tasks.problems()`), never fatal. `plugins/desktop/simple_tasks.py` reads it; `decide.py` asks it after the fast path and before the model, so a catalogue task needs no model call.
- **Matching:** the WHOLE request must fit a phrase (`{kind:group}` placeholders); when several fit the more careful class wins (C, ask, B, A), then an entry's `beats`, then the more specific phrase; two equal fits mean no match (the model plans). A compound request ("rename a to b and move it ...") is not matched. A vague item ("those", "old stuff") or a place outside the known folders is a question.
- **Classes in the file:** FILES (mkdir; create a text / md / html file with content; rename and move / copy with numbered names, never replacing; open with the default app; find by name / date / size and show in Explorer; zip / unzip into NEW archives and folders; sort Downloads by type with an undo list ("undo the last sort" puts the whole sort back); trash / permanent / cleanup / empty the bin as above) are A inside the known folders. APPS / WINDOWS (open, close, minimize, maximize, snap, arrange, switch, show desktop; close an app, left open when it asks to save; website and search; media and volume; screenshot to outputs; copy a window's text to the clipboard and paste; type in the active field) are A. SETTINGS: dark / light, brightness, do-not-disturb, wallpaper to a named picture, lock, reminders / timers / alarms in the kernel scheduler are A; Wi-Fi / Bluetooth / airplane are B (they can cut the connection JARVIS needs: his words or the PIN); security, firewall, updates, accounts, privacy, startup apps and power plan are explained and not done ("ask"). DAILY: a note into the vault inbox, read the clipboard (the text stays on the PC), time / battery / disk / network, today's folder (never guessed), a quick list, Calculator (the answer is checked against the window), unit conversion (fixed table, on the PC). Class C entries (send, post, pay, buy, install, uninstall, sign in, run a download) go to the loop, where the kernel holds the step for the PIN. Reading a window aloud and summarising a window are listed as not built.
- **Each entry** has a fake-based test of its class and its verification (`test_simple_tasks.py`, `test_simple_tasks_run.py`); `needs_laptop: true` marks the ones whose real behaviour only Windows can show.

## D54b (2026-10-26): the decision model is the ONE shared decision engine
- **Owner's words:** "Use the decision model also in everything where it can be used."
- **What:** `jarvis/plugins/_shared/decider.py`: `decide(kind, question, options, context) -> {choice, confidence, why, source, ms, ask}` for every small structured choice. Order: deterministic rules first (a sure rule answers alone, no model call), a 5-minute cache, then ONE short call to the local model (alias `ui_decide` = the 4B, per kind the 1.7B once measured; JSON-schema grammar so only an option can be returned; 3 s timeout), then the backstops. The model only PROPOSES. `dialog_class` is raise-only (A < B < C): the more careful of the rule's and the model's class, an unknown or unreadable dialog is B, so a model answer can never lower a class C / B step to A and never widens a grant (the grant is not an input). Below the kind's confidence threshold the answer is "ask" (choice None). A model that is down, slow, malformed or off-list ends in the kind's deterministic fallback, never an exception and never a blocked UI (`adecide` is cut off after the timeout). Logging is content-free (kind, label, confidence, ms, source; a candidate is logged by number). `config/decider.json` holds the thresholds and the per-kind model.
- **Used at:** (1) intent routing (`route()`: chat | pc_task | screen_read | doc_read | file_task | delete_cleanup | ask_ui | web_search | weather | simple_task, the catalogue id when it is one; the bridge's `jarvis_do` returns a hint naming the tool that answers a screen / document / weather / web question directly, rules only, tool list and descriptions unchanged); (2) the class of a dialog in the screen loop (`drive.py`: a class C, or a B only the model saw, asks the owner before the click; the kernel keeps `CONSEQUENTIAL` / `HARD_CONSEQUENCE` and the grant); (3) `specialist_for()` (task kind to the alias of `config/specialists.json`) and `ui_pattern_for()` (`config/ui_patterns.json` does not exist on this branch: it answers "ask" until it does); (4) a vague folder phrase in the catalogue (`resolve()`: candidates + confidence, the owner is asked below the threshold); file and app resolution are available the same way. (5) The privacy gateway's simple-vs-heavy routing is NOT moved here: it keeps its cheap size / tool-count rule and never calls a model.
- **Eval:** `jarvis/evals/pc_control/decider_cases.json` (68 labelled cases: intent incl. Hindi / Telugu mixed with English and injection attempts, dialog class, specialist, folder / file / app ambiguity; the catalogue's examples are added as simple_task cases) and `eval_decider.py` (`python jarvis\evals\pc_control\eval_decider.py --models decider,decider-qwen3-1.7b [--apply]`): accuracy per kind for the engine and for the model alone, p50 / p95 latency, sure-but-wrong answers, class-lowering violations (must be 0), suggested thresholds (smallest confidence with >= 95% right and >= 60% answered) and the cheapest model per kind within 2 points; `--apply` writes them into `config/decider.json`. Rules only (no model): intent 0.99, nothing sure-but-wrong. The 4B / 1.7B numbers are for the laptop session to produce.
- **Tests:** `plugins/_shared/tests/test_decider.py`, `test_decider_eval.py` (a fake model for the logic), `plugins/desktop/tests/test_decider_uses.py`, `bridge/test_route_hint.py`.

### D54, review fixes of 2026-10-26 (goal.py, specialists)
- A goal step that was not fully observed is reported as "Done, but not everything was observed" and the dropped parts as "Not done" (`goal.py` follows `coordinator.summary`). The goal runs as a registered operation, so Stop reaches it while it plans, between steps and inside a helper step. An online "cannot" (a well-formed plan with no steps) or question is told to the owner and is not overridden by the local model planning alone; only transport and format failures fall back. A path for a helper must be whole in the owner's own words (`written_by_owner`, his utterance when the host has it). A specialists file that lists only some kinds keeps the built-in rows of the others. `llm_result_fields` names `reason`, `provider` and `question`. A reply is read up to 1 MB. The plan timeout stays above the gateway's longest queue wait (`plan_timeout()`). Tests: `plugins/desktop/tests/test_goal_review.py`.

## D56 (2026-10-26): the face window is exempt from the footprint throttling
- **Owner's words:** "right now the Windows widget is very non responsive".
- **Cause (code reading, not yet measured on the laptop):** D50's governor put BELOW_NORMAL priority, EcoQoS (efficiency mode) and the 20% CPU hard-cap job on every JARVIS process including the face (Electron). Under EcoQoS Windows runs a window's threads at low clocks and on efficiency cores, and inside the capped job a model at `-t 3` takes the whole 20%, so the UI is starved exactly while JARVIS works.
- **Change:** `governor.INTERACTIVE = {"face"}`: those processes get none of the three (the Enforcer records `interactive`). The soft governor still unloads or suspends only llama-server. The face's own CPU still counts in the footprint report.
- **To verify on the laptop:** UI latency with a model loaded before and after; whether the face's GPU process (WebGL/Live2D render, backdrop blur) is itself heavy; frame rate with the HUD on.

## D57 (2026-10-26): the face window no longer freezes around the moment the avatar and the chat open
- **Owner's words:** "When the JARVIS avatar and chat box open, sometimes it is unresponsive." (and, earlier, "the Windows widget is very non responsive"; D56 already removed the governor's throttling from the face).
- **Method:** the start-up path was read statically (main process, injeca graph, windows, MCP manager, renderer stores and polls, Live2D/VRM stage) and what could be measured here was measured in headless Chromium. **Nothing was run in Electron on the laptop**, so every "cause" below is a reading of the code plus, where stated, a number from a stand-in; none is confirmed on the owner's machine. Paths are under `jarvis/face/apps/stage-tamagotchi/src` unless they start with `packages/`; line numbers are those of commit `c8cf222` (before this change).
- **Measured here (headless Chromium, ANGLE/SwiftShader, so a software GPU, not the Radeon Pro 5500M):** one `gl.readPixels(x, y, 1, 1)` on a 900x1200 canvas with `preserveDrawingBuffer` takes 0.4 ms when nothing is pending, and 164 ms right after a draw call (it waits for the GPU to finish the frame; the shader there was deliberately heavy, so only the mechanism counts, not the 164). `document.elementFromPoint` after a style change: 0.04 ms. A real GPU shared with a local model sits between the two numbers; the laptop trace (below) gives the real one. The poll and timeout logic was measured with the real MCP client library against a stand-in tool server (tests below).

| # | Cause | Evidence | Can freeze input? | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The avatar window is not created until the tool-server connection has been tried. injeca builds the main window only after `mcpManager`, and `setupMcpManager` awaited `applyAndRestart` | `main/index.ts:471` (main window depends on `mcpManager`), `services/airi/mcp-servers/index.ts:718`; connect limit 8 s (`:91`), a stale socket repeats the try, so up to about 16 s with a tool server that accepts and does not answer (it loads its plugins for 10 to 60 s, D49) | The whole app: no window at all, then "sometimes" because it depends on the tool server's state | Fixed: connect in the background; every list/call already waits for it (`applying`). Test: `index.connection.test.ts` "start-up does not wait..." (returns in under 1.5 s against a server that never answers) |
| 2 | The hidden Beat Sync window's page load is awaited, and the main window depends on it | `windows/beat-sync/index.ts:22` (`await load`), `main/index.ts:393,471` | Delays the avatar by one page load; a second copy of the renderer start-up runs beside the model load | Fixed: window object at once, page loads 20 s later (Beat Sync is off until turned on). Not measured |
| 3 | Four renderers boot inside the same few seconds: avatar, voice inlay (started right after the avatar's page load), Beat Sync, chat (cold, on the first click). Each loads the whole bundle and runs `App.vue`'s start-up | `windows/main/index.ts:258`, `windows/inlay/index.ts`, `windows/chat/floating.ts:507` | The avatar's model load and first clicks compete with three page loads and a local model | Fixed: stagger inlay +6 s, chat +12 s, Beat Sync +20 s after the avatar (`windows/shared/startup-stagger.ts`). Not measured |
| 4 | The first press of the chat button creates the window and loads the page inside the chat mutex; a second press (the owner presses again, nothing seemed to happen) queues, runs after the open and folds the chat again | `windows/chat/index.ts:376` (`toggle` = `modeSwitch.run`), `windows/chat/floating.ts:525,552` (`open` awaits `reusable.getWindow()` = new BrowserWindow + `await load`) | Yes, as seen by the owner: seconds with no reaction, then the chat flashes and goes | Fixed: the floating chat is created folded and hidden 12 s after the avatar (`JARVIS_FACE_PREWARM_CHAT=0` turns it off), and a press during a press joins it (`windows/chat/single-flight.ts`, test) |
| 5 | Click-through hit test reads the canvas back on every cursor move: `gl.readPixels` (synchronous GPU wait) on each of the up to 60 cursor messages a second, plus `document.elementFromPoint` | `renderer/pages/index.vue:104,160,171`, `packages/stage-ui/src/composables/canvas-alpha.ts` (`readPixels`), `packages/electron-vueuse/src/main/loop.ts:11` (60 Hz) and `main/services/electron/screen.ts` | Yes while the cursor moves over the avatar, which is when the owner reaches for the avatar or the chat button; size depends on how busy the GPU is | Fixed: the pixel tests see the cursor at most every 50 ms (`refThrottled`). The cost on the Radeon is not known |
| 6 | The stage draws every frame the display offers, forever (`max-fps` default 0), into a transparent, always-on-top window (`transparent: true`, 'screen-saver' level) at render scale 2 | `packages/stage-ui-live2d/src/composables/live2d/live2d.ts:35`, `packages/stage-ui-three/src/stores/model-store.ts:88`, `Canvas.vue:23,84`, `windows/shared/window.ts` (`transparentWindowConfig`, `setWindowAlwaysOnTop`) | Steady GPU and compositor load, which a local model on the same GPU turns into slow input | Fixed (the cap): 30 fps idle, 60 while the character speaks/thinks/listens or the owner used the window in the last 3 s (`utils/stage-frame-rate.ts`); the owner's own limit in Settings still wins. Not changed: render scale 2 and `preserveDrawingBuffer: true` (the hit test needs it), transparency, window level |
| 7 | Background polls had no limit of their own: the main process allows a tool call 180 s (D49's change for real tools), so a hung tool server held the HUD (4 s), spoken (8 s), preview (3 s) and memory (60 s) polls for up to 180 s each. Spoken, preview and memory did not skip a hidden window; the memory saver could overlap itself | `renderer/composables/use-jarvis-*.ts`, `mcp-servers/index.ts:79`. Already right and kept: each poller has a `running` flag (no pile-up), preview backs off 3 s to 60 s, HUD skips a hidden window | A stuck poll does not block the page, but it holds a slot of the tool server connection and shows stale state for minutes | Fixed: a poll asks for 8 s (`timeoutMs`, new, clamped 1 s to 180 s in the main process; real tool calls keep 180 s), stops waiting at 10 s, skips a hidden window; the HUD polls every 15 s when the window has no focus; the memory saver gets a `running` flag. Tests per poller |
| 8 | Reconnect when the tool server is down: one shared attempt, 1 s cool-down, a 5 s timer, three pollers | `mcp-servers/index.ts:91-104,246-269` | No storm found (at most one try a second). It did keep one refused connection a second for as long as the server was down | Fixed (small): the wait grows 1, 2, 4, 8, 10 s while tries keep failing, and resets on success |
| 9 | Speech: a microphone left on starts the voice activity model and the wake word spotter (ONNX/WASM Workers) with the window | `packages/stage-ui/src/composables/voice-input-mode.ts` (`useVoiceListening`), `stores/settings/audio-device.ts:26` (microphone off by default) | Contention with the model load, not a main-thread block (the models run in Workers; the audio windows still reach the page 31 times a second) | Fixed: a microphone that was left on starts listening 8 s after the window opens; turning it on later starts at once. Kokoro preload only runs for a configured `kokoro-local` and Whisper is never preloaded (checked in `use-inference-preload.ts`): nothing to change |
| 10 | Checked and **not** causes: IPC handlers on the UI path (only `electronMcpListTools`/`callTool`/`applyAndRestart`, and the chat mode switch, wait on something slow, and the first two now have limits); synchronous fs in the main process (the server-channel certificate work runs only with TLS on, which the JARVIS default is not; `persistence.ts` saves are throttled and async; no `execSync`/`spawnSync` anywhere in `src/main`; the only synchronous file calls are the small config reads and one `existsSync` on the D: drive at start (`main/index.ts:102`), which a sleeping or disconnected D: could stall); `disable-gpu` or other GPU switches (none are set); backdrop blur (macOS only); the network-lock callback (microseconds per request); the chat-open path (no tool list, recall, memory or card request is made when the chat opens; the tool list is read once by the main window at start-up, 105 tools); duplicate subscriptions after a reload (the renderer is torn down with the page; the main process guards its loop and its service registration) | as listed in the text | no | none needed |
| 11 | **Not fixed, needs the trace:** the serial awaits in `App.vue`'s `onMounted` (locale, chat store, auth, display models, character card, stage model, audio devices, server channel, in that order); every window running the whole renderer start-up; the `setIgnoreMouseEvents(true, { forward: true })` windows (inlay, floating chat) on Windows; the 60 Hz cursor messages to each renderer; `location.ts` `load()` calls `removeAllListeners('did-start-navigation')` after the first load, which removes the backdrop service's and the main window's own listeners (a bug, not a freeze); the HUD's endless CSS animations in a transparent window | `renderer/App.vue`, `windows/inlay/index.ts:57`, `windows/chat/floating.ts:453`, `libs/electron/location.ts` | possible | open |

- **Tests added (all pass; nothing existing was changed to make them pass):** `mcp-servers/index.connection.test.ts` (start-up returns without waiting for a silent server; a call with `timeoutMs` is cut and a call without one still waits; the limit and back-off functions), `composables/poll-guard.test.ts`, additions to `use-jarvis-spoken.test.ts`, `use-jarvis-memory.test.ts`, `use-jarvis-preview.test.ts` (hidden window skips; a stuck answer is given up and the next poll runs; no overlap), `composables/use-stage-frame-rate.test.ts` (30/60 rules and the 3 s hold), `windows/shared/startup-stagger.test.ts`, `windows/chat/single-flight.test.ts`, and in `packages/stage-ui` `composables/voice-input-mode.test.ts`. Existing suites run unchanged: `apps/stage-tamagotchi` `src/main` (all), the touched renderer composables and `components/hud`.
- **Not changed, as asked:** the chat request size (D52), policy, secrets, the governor (D56). The 180 s limit for real tool calls stays (D49). Live2D render scale and `preserveDrawingBuffer`, window transparency and level are untouched until the trace says they matter.
- **Not verified here:** all of it in Electron; the effect on the Radeon Pro 5500M; that closed-port connects on Windows take about 2 s (a general TCP behaviour I did not check against a source and did not rely on); that the chat prewarm costs no noticeable memory (one more renderer, about the size of the inlay's). Turn it off with `JARVIS_FACE_PREWARM_CHAT=0` if the footprint report (D50) shows it matters.
- **Capture a real start-up trace on the laptop (Windows; paths on D:):**
  1. Close the face. In PowerShell: `$env:APP_DEBUG='1'` (the main window opens its DevTools on creation), then start the face from the same shell. Also start it once with `--inspect=9230` added to the command line for the main-process profile.
  2. **Renderer (the avatar window):** in the DevTools of the main window open Performance, tick Screenshots and Web Vitals, press the record button with "Start profiling and reload page" (Ctrl+Shift+E), wait until the avatar has loaded and the chat button answers, then stop. Save the profile to `D:\jarvis\traces\renderer-startup.json`. Then record a second trace: press record, move the mouse over the avatar for 5 s, press the chat button, press it again, stop. Look at: the Main track for **Long Tasks** (red corners, over 50 ms) and what they hold (Bottom-Up, group by Activity); `readPixels` / `getContext` / `elementFromPoint` under "Event: message" (the cursor IPC); the Frames track (a flat 60 or 144 fps line means the cap is not applied); GPU track gaps; `Evaluate Script` and `Compile` at the start (bundle size); the timestamp of the first Paint and of "Loading..." leaving. Do the same in the DevTools of the chat window (right-click in the chat, or `APP_DEBUG=1` opens it) for the chat's own start-up.
  3. **Main process:** open `chrome://inspect` in Chrome, Configure `localhost:9230`, click "inspect" on the Electron main process, Profiler tab, "Start", launch/open chat, "Stop"; save as `.cpuprofile` to `D:\jarvis\traces\main.cpuprofile`. Look at Bottom-Up for synchronous work over 50 ms (fs, `execSync`, JSON, certificate code) and for the time between the first line of `main/index.ts` and the main window's `ready-to-show` (add `console.time` around `injeca.start()` if needed).
  4. **Whole app (GPU, utility and renderer processes together):** start the face with `--trace-startup --trace-startup-file=D:\jarvis\traces\startup.json --trace-startup-duration=40` (Chromium switches, so I expect Electron to honour them; if no file appears, use `chrome://tracing` > Record with the "Web developer" preset against `--remote-debugging-port` or Electron's `contentTracing.startRecording`, which I did not check here), open the file in `chrome://tracing` or ui.perfetto.dev. Look at `CrGpuMain`/`GPU Process` busy time per frame, `UpdateLayeredWindow` or `Present` time for the transparent windows, and the Browser process `CrBrowserMain` for blocking tasks.
  5. **Compare** with `JARVIS_FACE_PREWARM_CHAT=0` and with the model loaded vs `llama-server` stopped. What decides: (a) a long task above 100 ms in the Main track at the time the owner could not click, (b) frames at 144 or 60 where 30 is expected while idle, (c) `readPixels` over 5 ms, (d) the time from process start to the avatar window's first paint over 5 s, (e) the Windows Task Manager GPU engine graph of the face's GPU process against `llama-server`'s.

## D55 (2026-10-26): generated UI = declarative forms only; the ask panel, the pattern guide, and the PC cleaner as its first use
- **Owner's words (2026-10-26):** "for cleanup open a UI where I have options like a cleaner (CCleaner style) I can check, or I tell JARVIS 'check this and that' and at last 'clean up'." Then: "If there is a UI requirement it will create the UI and ask me." "when JARVIS creates something to ask, it should also be responsive and nice." "The UI JARVIS wants to create depends on the task."
- **Decision: generated UI = declarative forms only.** JARVIS (the chat model, or a pattern builder in code) never writes code or HTML for the face. It writes a JSON spec; ONE fixed, trusted component (`AskPanel.vue` with `AskNode.vue`) draws it. A spec is `{title, intro, fields: [node], buttons, timeout_s}`. A node is a layout (section, row, column, tabs, grid), an input (checkbox_group, radio or segmented, select, text or textarea, number, slider with a live value, toggle, date or time, file_choice with thumbnails, gallery, table with sortable columns and row select, cards with pros and cons, split list-and-detail) or a display (info_table, chart bar/line/pie, code_view with line numbers and Copy, diff_view, steps, progress, callout). Every text is plain text: control, invisible and direction characters are removed in Python (`bridge/ask_spec.py`) and again in the face (`jarvis-ask-logic.ts`), and the face draws text only through Vue interpolation (no `v-html`), so markup in a title stays as characters. No link is clickable, no frame, no script, no network; images only as `data:` URLs of four raster types. `file_choice` and `gallery` list only what the BACKEND finds in the known folders (Downloads, Documents, Desktop, Pictures; the save list adds JARVIS's output folder): options a caller writes into them are dropped, secret-looking names, links and folders are never listed, and the answer is the path of the item the owner clicked (the panel only ever holds option ids). Unknown component or bad shape: a plain list plus a note, never an error screen. Caps: 150 nodes, depth 4, 500 options or rows, 60,000 characters.
- **A form can only collect choices; it never approves anything.** The module does not import the console client or the kernel (tested), a field that asks for a PIN, password, passcode, code, token or key is refused, and the answer comes back as `{answered, button, values, note: "data for the task, not an approval"}`. Consequential approvals stay in the console with the PIN (D27); the `confirm` pattern says so on screen. A form that is not answered returns `{answered: false, reason: timeout | closed | replaced}`; an answer is never invented. The panel's three calls (`jarvis_ask_wanted`, `jarvis_ask_submit`, `jarvis_ask_close`) are registered but not listed to the chat model and not named in its persona; submit needs the form's one-time token from the poll.
- **Tools.** `jarvis_ask(spec | pattern + data)` (waits up to 90 s, at most 150 s; the face gives a tool call 180 s) and `jarvis_cleaner(action, items)` are in the chat list. The chat list is 7,564 bytes (limit 7,600): about ten tool descriptions and the parameter-description cap (160 to 110 characters) were shortened to make room, and the snapshot `request-budget.mcp-tools.json` is regenerated (first face request 8,586 bytes, limit 12,000). Persona: one line, 3,770 of 3,800 characters: "Choices: show them with jarvis_ask, not text. PC cleanup: jarvis_cleaner; tick only what the owner names, clean only when the owner says clean."
- **Pattern guide** (`jarvis/config/ui_patterns.json`, one builder per id in `bridge/ui_patterns.py`; a test fails when they disagree). The model gives a pattern id and a little data; the id list is one compact line in the tool description.

  | task | pattern | screen |
  |---|---|---|
  | clean up, free disk space | `cleanup` | checkbox_group with sizes right-aligned (safe ticked, review unticked) + progress + result with undo list |
  | which file or picture | `pick_file` | file_choice list with size, or gallery with thumbnails |
  | ambiguous app or name, option | `choose` | segmented radio (2 to 5 short options), cards with pros and cons, or a radio list |
  | change a setting | `settings` | toggles and sliders with a live value, Apply and Undo changes |
  | a coding problem is solved | `code` | code_view (line numbers, Copy) + Explanation tab + "Type it into the editor" button |
  | compare options or products | `compare` | sortable table with row select, or cards |
  | long running job | `job` | steps with status marks + progress + Cancel job; shown without waiting, a later call with the same pattern updates it in place and reports the owner's Cancel click |
  | numbers and statistics | `data_view` | chart + table of the same numbers |
  | review before a consequential step | `confirm` | info_table + warning + Go ahead and Cancel (the PIN is still asked in the console) |
  | reminder or schedule | `schedule` | date + time + repeat + note |
  | free input | `text` | one text box (or area) |
  | where to save | `save` | the known folders |
  | the decider's question | `clarify` | "Which one: A or B?" becomes a segmented choice, "Which file?" lists the newest files with sizes, anything else is a text box |
- **The PC cleaner** (`bridge/cleaner.py` scan and clean on an injected file system and Recycle Bin; `bridge/cleaner_ui.py` the form and the chat tool). SAFE rows, a fixed list, ticked by default: temp (user and Windows\Temp; files touched in the last hour are left alone), browser caches (cache folders of Chrome, Edge, Brave, Opera, Firefox: never cookies, passwords, history, sessions or profiles), app caches (Discord, Slack, VS Code, Teams), thumbnail and icon caches, crash dumps and logs older than 7 days, the Windows Update download cache, the Recycle Bin, pip/npm/NuGet caches. Their files are deleted for real. REVIEW rows, never ticked by themselves or by "all" or "everything", listed with the biggest items and their sizes (150 per row; only listed items can be ticked): duplicates in Downloads and Documents (same size and same content, newest copy stays), installers (.exe, .msi) in Downloads older than 30 days, files over 500 MB not opened for 180 days in the known folders, empty folders. They always go to the Recycle Bin (`send2trash`); the result lists what went there. Locked or vanished files are skipped and counted; the cleaner then re-scans and returns freed bytes, bytes moved to the bin, the skipped count and the undo list. A dry run says what would happen. Hard blocks, checked at listing and again before each removal on the resolved path: Windows, Program Files (both), ProgramData, the user profile folder and the owner's known folders themselves, the Users folder and other users' folders, drive roots, D:\jarvis and D:\jarvis-secrets with the JARVIS code, data, vault and audit folders, and the project folders in `config/projects.json`. The only places inside Windows a row may touch are the exact roots Windows\Temp, SoftwareDistribution\Download, Minidump, Logs and MEMORY.DMP. A link, junction or reparse point is never followed or removed (a file swapped for a link after listing is skipped).
- **Cleaner rules in the chat flow.** `clean` runs only what is ticked and only when the owner's words (passed in `items`) say clean, clean up or clean now and do not negate or postpone it; the panel's Clean selected button is the owner's click and counts. "Clean up my PC" with nothing ticked opens the panel and, because the same sentence says clean up, runs the SAFE list only. `check` and `uncheck` take plain names ("temp", "browser cache", "recycle bin", "old installers"; the longest matching phrase wins, so "windows temp" is one row and "temp" is two); a name that matches nothing is reported, not guessed. Honest limit: the tool cannot see the owner's words, so the model passes them and the check guards against a stray call; the click is the real confirmation. The chat model receives category ids, fixed labels, sizes and counts only: file names go to the owner's panel and nowhere else (tested on the scan, the clean result and every tool answer).
- **Responsive and nice (the owner's bar), measured with `scripts/ask-check.mjs`** (Chromium; forms built by the real Python backend on a fake PC; 62 forms at 360x640, 480x800 and 1280x800 in dark and light; screenshots in `jarvis/face/apps/stage-tamagotchi/docs/ask-panel/`). The panel is drawn within 100 ms of the server having the form (measured max 60 ms, median 25 ms, cold first form 30 to 40 ms) because `jarvis_ask_wanted` is a long poll: it returns within milliseconds of a change, never asks more than once a second, and backs off up to 30 s while the server is silent. Scan and clean run in background threads; the panel shows a skeleton or a progress bar with real numbers ("1.0 GB of 2.0 GB, 5 of 13") while the owner can still read or close it. A 450-row review list draws about 20 rows (virtual list, fixed row height) and scrolls with no long task; a collapsed section and the inactive tab draw nothing; checkboxes react in the same frame (optimistic, sent only with a button); no horizontal scroll at any size. Tab order is the page order, Space toggles, arrow keys move inside a choice, Enter presses the primary button (also from a choice), Escape presses Cancel or Close; focus moves into the panel on open and back on close; roles and labels for dialog, radiogroup, switch, progressbar, tabs and table; `prefers-reduced-motion` stops every animation; motion is CSS transitions of 150 to 180 ms. Style: the HUD tokens (cyan, mint, amber, red, glass, monospace numbers, corner ticks) in dark; the same hues darkened for light.
- **Files.** Backend (all new): `bridge/ask_spec.py`, `ask.py`, `ask_tool.py`, `ui_patterns.py`, `cleaner.py`, `cleaner_ui.py`, `config/ui_patterns.json`. Edits to shared files, kept small: `bridge/face_tools.py` (two CHAT_TOOLS entries, three PANEL_TOOLS names, shortened descriptions, the parameter-description cap, an `asker`/`cleaner` argument and 3 lines in `build_server`, a `--no-ask` flag and the start-up lines), `persona/jarvis_card.json` and the face's `base.yaml` (one line), `request-budget.mcp-tools.json` (regenerated), `App.vue` (two lines). Face (new): `components/ask/{AskPanel,AskNode,AskHost}.vue`, `ask.css`, `composables/jarvis-ask-logic.ts`, `use-jarvis-ask.ts`, `scripts/ask-check.mjs`, `scripts/ask-harness/`. Nothing in `plugins/desktop`, the kernel policy or `config/simple_tasks.json` was touched.
- **Tests.** pytest: `bridge/test_ask.py` (language, caps, fallbacks, escaping, known folders, broker, long poll, every pattern from sample data and from nonsense, the decider's question, which file, where to save, no approval path), `test_cleaner.py` (fake PC and fake bin: rows, sizes, defaults, cookies never touched, exact removal, rescan, dry run, locked files, review rows to the bin, stale item ids, every hard block, links, the listing-to-removal race, no file names in the model view), `test_cleaner_ui.py` (the owner's-words rules, ticking by plain words, all/everything never tick review, clean up my PC runs the safe list only, the panel's click, progress, through the real tool server). vitest (node project): `jarvis-ask-logic.test.ts`, `use-jarvis-ask.test.ts`, `components/ask/AskPanel.test.ts` (server-side render: escaping, no link or script, thumbnails, virtualization, totals, progress, labels).
- **Not done or not verified (the laptop session checks these on screen).** (1) Nothing was run on Windows: sizes against Windows Settings > System > Storage, the Recycle Bin size and emptying (`SHQueryRecycleBinW`, `SHEmptyRecycleBinW`), whether `send2trash` is installed (it is not pinned yet, see `01_RESEARCH_AUDIT.md`; without it review items are skipped and counted), locked files in use (Chrome running, Explorer holding thumbcache), Windows\Temp and SoftwareDistribution without admin rights (skipped and counted), OneDrive-redirected Documents or Desktop (not scanned), the real browser profile folders.
