(2) The panel in the real Electron window: the harness is Chromium with the app's content policy, not the app; the 360 px look, the long poll through the face's MCP connection (a held call of up to 20 s), and whether the face stays responsive while it is open. (3) The model chooses the right pattern and calls `jarvis_ask` for the decider's question: the persona line is in, nothing forces it, so a do_goal reply that is only a question may still be read out as text. A hook that shows the form by itself belongs in `plugins/desktop` (not touched). (4) A rogue model that guessed the panel tool names could answer its own form; for the cleaner the damage is bounded by the safe list, the hard blocks and the bin, and `jarvis_ask_submit` needs the one-time token; the real fix is for the face's MCP route to mark panel calls. (5) `test_the_face_prompt_matches_the_card` fails before and after this change: the face's `base.yaml` already differed from `jarvis_card.json` in two lines (`jarvis::desktop__do_goal` and the document line); regenerating would undo the face's hand edit, so only the new line was added to both.

## D58 (2026-10-26): every model reads English; the owner keeps his own words; translation runs on this laptop only

- **Owner's words:** "The models only understand English. If I use Telugu, convert it to English and use [that]." / "Do this for every model." / "Language conversion." (both directions, as a task) / "Use a local model for language conversion also."
- **Rules.** (1) Every model call that carries the owner's words, or text that came from him, receives English. (2) His original text is kept for his record, on this PC (`translations.jsonl` under `JARVIS_DATA_DIR\translate`, `language.keep_original`), and is never forwarded. The file holds what he said **unredacted** (it is his own record on his own PC, and the one place the gateway's "no request text in a log" rule does not apply); deleting it erases it, `keep_original: false` stops it. (3) **Translation uses a model on THIS laptop only**: in-process or on loopback (the translation service on 127.0.0.1:48960, or a llama-swap alias on 127.0.0.1:8080). No online translator, no online provider, no call through the privacy gateway to translate. Enforced in code: `Translator.add_backend` refuses a backend whose `local` flag is not `True`; `LlamaSwapBackend` and `ServiceBackend` refuse any address that is not plain loopback (no credentials in it) before a socket is opened and ignore proxy variables; `test_translate.py` fails if a translation path connects to anything but 127.0.0.1 or ::1 (a `socket.connect` guard, proxy variables set) or if the translation modules name another address or import a network library. (4) Tool results, file text, web pages and system prompts are DATA: never translated into something a model reads as an instruction (a Telugu instruction inside a tool result reaches the model as the same Telugu text; tested at the gateway, the kernel's llm-proxy and the layer). (5) The final answer shown to the owner is English unless `reply_language` says otherwise. (6) When the translator is missing, slow (3 s budget) or its answer is unusable, the ORIGINAL text goes on unchanged and the layer says so (`X-Jarvis-Translated: skipped`, a note in the result, an audit line); nothing blocks and nothing breaks.
- **Models** (research and sources: `01_RESEARCH_AUDIT.md`, "Input translation (checked 2026-10-26)"): text, Telugu and Hindi script, both directions: **IndicTrans2 distilled 200M ONNX int8** (MIT) in ONE loopback process (`jarvis/scripts/translate_server.py`, so the model is loaded once, not once per JARVIS process); romanized Telugu and Hindi (Tenglish, Hinglish) and fallback: **Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507** through llama-swap (roster kind `translate`: alias `decider`, fallback `local-chat`; nothing new to download); speech: **IndicConformer int8** (Telugu, Hindi) and Parakeet (English), unchanged. Flags: NLLB-600M is CC-BY-NC-4.0 (not used); Sarvam-Translate is GPL-3.0 (not used); Qwen3-ASR-0.6B has no Telugu (cannot be the fallback); Whisper is unusable for Telugu (WER 98 to 105 at tiny to small).
- **How it works** (`jarvis/plugins/_shared/translate.py`, standard library only; also loaded by path on the server and by the console, which have no `jarvis` package):
  - `detect_language(text)`: `en`, `te`, `hi`, `te-Latn`, `hi-Latn`, `xx`. Deterministic: Unicode script blocks (Telugu U+0C00-0C7F, Devanagari U+0900-097F; three or more native letters, or two and at least 10 % of the letters, make a text non-English), then for Latin text a list of romanized Telugu and Hindi words (English words excluded) and a character-trigram score. A close call is English (no cost) unless `language.identify_with_model` is on (off: it could cost a model swap). 56 of 56 eval sentences are detected, and 18 plain English sentences are not misdetected, but the word list was written looking at those sentences, so that is a regression guard, not a held-out score.
  - `translate_to_english(text)` returns `{english, source_lang, confidence, preserved, original, translated, ...}`. English input: returned as is, **no backend is touched**. Masked before translation and restored after (and a backend that loses or repeats one is not trusted): code blocks and inline code, quoted strings, URLs, e-mail addresses, Windows and Unix paths, file names, numbers, times, `@`/`#` words, built-in app names and every term in `config/glossary.json`. Multi-line text (the face puts a time line and its own English rules around the owner's words) is translated run by run: only the non-English lines go to the model. A cache, a 3 s budget (`language.budget_s`, per backend override `backend_budget_s`), a circuit breaker (a failed backend rests 30 s, so the gateway stays fast), the original kept for the owner.
  - **Confidence is flagged, not hidden.** A result under `language.min_confidence` (0.55; a backend answering in the source script, or an answer of absurd length, is capped low) makes `run_pc_task` and `do_goal` ask "I understood this as: ... Is that right?" instead of acting, unless he already answered such a question. The backend's own confidence is an assumption (0.85 for IndicTrans2, 0.7 for the LLM, no per-sentence scores) until the eval file replaces it.
  - **Reply language** (`language.reply_language`): `en` (default) | `match` | `te` | `hi`, a session setting stored in `language.json`. "reply in Telugu", "reply in English", "switch to Hindi", "telugu lo cheppu", "తెలుగులో చెప్పు", "हिंदी में बोलो", "reply in my language" change it (only a short message that is nothing but that request counts: "write a reply in English to Bob" and "translate this to Telugu" do not). Recognised in the gateway (any chat) and in `decide.decide`. Only text the owner reads is converted: the gateway converts `message.content` (non-streamed, and streamed a finished sentence at a time) for the face's requests only; tool-call arguments, file names, commands and anything a model or the kernel consumes stay English. A resent conversation whose assistant turns are in Telugu is made English again for free: every sentence converted for the owner is remembered with its English (reverse cache), and the history is translated only from the cache or within `language.history_budget_s`.
  - **Speaking.** A Telugu or Hindi reply is spoken only if its Piper voice is installed AND `language.tts_verified.<lang>` is `true` (set by hand after the owner has listened). Default `false`: the text is shown, nothing is spoken, and the speech server answers "not spoken: ..." (400) instead of speaking English over Telugu. The gate is in the speech server (`/v1/audio/speech`, what the face's replies use); the console's own `/api/voice/speak` and its voice-check page are NOT gated, so the owner can listen to a voice there before verifying it. English replies are unchanged.
  - **Translation as a task** (`jarvis/plugins/translate/`, `translate_task.py`): `translate_text` (any pair of en, te, hi; te to hi goes through English), `translate_file` (read with doc_tools' reader, a NEW file `<name>.<lang>.txt` in the outputs folder, never an overwrite, the first paragraph is round-trip checked), `translate_clipboard` (only when called), `translate_window` (one window, through the desktop plugin's `read_window_text` and its screen-text rules: if screen text is not allowed to reach the model nothing is read out), `set_reply_language`, `add_glossary_term` ("never translate Satish"). Results carry `text`, `copy_text`, an `actions: ["copy"]` card and a `flag`. **No `code_view` or `jarvis_ask` exists in this repo** (searched); the card is the structure a face panel would render, and the plugin is not in the face's chat tool list (D52's budget), so "translate this" reaches it through the main agent, not the face chat model. Adding it to `CHAT_TOOLS` also needs the face's tool snapshot updated.
  - **Quality gate.** A translation into Telugu or Hindi is checked by translating it back to English (never from the cache) and comparing the two English texts with chrF++ (`translate_task.chrf`, no dependency; the similarity is the mean of both directions, 0 to 1); under `language.back_translation_min` (0.5) it is flagged to the owner in a sentence, with the back-translation, instead of being used silently. `evals/translate/run_eval.py --roundtrip` runs the same gate on the whole eval file. Not run on a real model.
  - **Glossary** (`config/glossary.json`): `terms`, `apps`, `owner_names`, `family_names`, `places`; the owner extends it by editing the file or saying "never translate X". The owner does NOT edit it for his own and his family's names (D62, 2026-10-26: "I will not [do] anything; it will know by conversion with me"): JARVIS learns them into `names.json` and merges both at load; this file is the shipped and manual list, and it wins.
  - **Fonts:** Noto Sans Telugu and Devanagari, 177 KB, bundled in the face (`face/packages/font-indic`, SIL OFL).
  - **Persona:** the face's persona said "reply in the language the owner used"; it now says to write English, because the layer converts the reply when he asks (`face/packages/i18n/.../base.yaml`, `persona/jarvis_card.json`).
- **Every entry point** (the owner: "do this for every model"). "Covered by" is the nearest choke point; each has a test in which a Telugu, Hindi or Tenglish message reaches the model call as English (fake translator, fake model) unless noted.

  | Entry point | Covered by | Test |
  |---|---|---|
  | Face chat (typed and spoken), the face's tools, every plugin that thinks through `pluginapi.models.agent_model()` (the gateway by default), `muse/chat_memory.py`, `bridge/second_opinion.py` (gateway instances on 48954-48956) | the privacy gateway, `translate_input`, before redaction: last user message, and resent user and assistant turns | `privacy/test_gateway_translate.py` |
  | Server-side models for Hermes and the lab agents (VM, VM2) | the kernel `llm_proxy` (`lang.english_body`: translated only if a local translator is enabled on the server, else forwarded and flagged `X-Jarvis-Translated: skipped`); the real route is the laptop translating first | `kernel/tests/test_lang.py` |
  | Telegram message, `/ask` (arrives on the server) | `Bridge.english` in `telegram.py` | `kernel/tests/test_lang.py` |
  | Owner command intake on the server (`Owner.command`) | `lang.english_for_agent` | `kernel/tests/test_lang.py` |
  | Console Command box (laptop) | `server.py` `/api/owner/command` through `lang_hook.english` before the text leaves the laptop | `console/tests/test_lang_hook.py` |
  | Console intent classifier | `server.py` `/api/classify` | `console/tests/test_lang_hook.py` |
  | Voice transcript (Telugu or Hindi) | `local_speech_server.py` returns `language` and `english` beside `text` (the owner's own words stay in `text`); the face then chats through the gateway | `console/tests/test_lang_hook.py` |
  | `jarvis_bridge` `delegate` (face tool `jarvis_do`, plugin host) | `client.delegate`: translated on the laptop before it goes to the kernel | `plugins/_shared/tests/test_translate_entrypoints.py` |
  | Desktop `run_pc_task`, `do_goal`, `decide.decide` (the decider, also reached by paths that bypass the gateway), missions' plan | `translate.entry_text` and `decide.decide` | `test_translate_entrypoints.py` |
  | Main agent `plan_task`, `run_task` | `translate.english` at the entry | `test_translate_entrypoints.py` |
  | Research `research_topic`, local team `ask_local` and `solve`, workbench `drafter` | `translate.english` at the entry (the workbench translates the owner's task only; third-party message text in its prompts stays fenced data) | `test_translate_entrypoints.py` |
  | `doc_reader` OCR prompt, `node/jarvis_node/vision.py` prompts | no owner words in them: fixed English, or built by code from the decider's English arguments | allowlisted, with the reason, in `test_translate_guard.py` |
  | Memory and recall write paths | the text that reaches the memory summariser is English (gateway); the owner's original is in `translations.jsonl` with the same id | `test_gateway_translate.py` (record) |
  | Probes and smoke tests (`kernel/gateway.py`, `capability_probe.py`, `local_models/smoke.py`, `persona/run_cases.py`, `scripts/self_repair.py`, `privacy/demo_live.py`, `router/translate.py` which converts the API shape, not the language) | fixed English prompts; allowlisted | `test_translate_guard.py` |
  | **Any new module** that builds a chat request with a user message | repo-wide guard: fails unless it calls the layer, sends through the gateway, or is on the allowlist with a reason; a stale allowlist entry also fails | `plugins/_shared/tests/test_translate_guard.py` |

- **The server (hermes-agent-vm, 2 CPU, about 11 GiB, beside Hermes and the kernel).** The laptop translates before text leaves (face, console, voice, `delegate`). Telegram text arrives on the server and has not been through the laptop. **Default: no translator runs there** (`JARVIS_TRANSLATE_KERNEL=off`): the text goes on as written, `tg_translate`/`owner_command`/`agent_translate` audit lines say `skipped`, and the proxy's reply carries `X-Jarvis-Translated: skipped`. `JARVIS_TRANSLATE_KERNEL=local` runs the same layer on the server, only after the laptop session measured the RAM and CPU of a small backend there next to Hermes and the kernel (not measured; the Qwen3-4B alias `jarvis-llm` already runs on the VM, `deploy/jarvis-llm.service`, which is the likely local backend there). **Not built:** a kernel-to-laptop hand-off (the kernel queuing a `translate` job for the laptop node and waiting); it is the intended way to have Telegram text translated by the laptop's models and needs a node job kind.
- **Config:** `config/specialists.json`: kind `translate` (alias `decider`, fallback `local-chat`; validated by `specialists.py`, which has the new kind) and a `language` section (all keys above). `config/glossary.json`. Environment: `JARVIS_TRANSLATE=0` turns the layer off for one run; `JARVIS_LANGUAGE_FILE`, `JARVIS_GLOSSARY`, `JARVIS_SPECIALISTS_FILE`, `JARVIS_TRANSLATE_KERNEL`, `JARVIS_TRANSLATE_PORT`.
- **Reuse (CLAUDE.md):** the models (IndicTrans2 and its ONNX export, Qwen3, IndicConformer, Piper, Noto fonts) are existing projects; the translator and masking are the glue. I know of no maintained project that does "mask paths and quotes, translate, restore, keep the original, flag by back-translation, local only" in front of an OpenAI-compatible gateway; IndicTransToolkit (AI4Bharat) is the pre- and post-processor the export itself uses and is not duplicated.
- **Changed outside the new files (additive):** `privacy/gateway.py` (`translate_input`, headers, reply conversion), `plugins/desktop/__init__.py` and `decide.py` (an import and a few lines at the entries), `plugins/{main_agent,research,local_team,workbench,jarvis_bridge}`, `console/jarvis_console/server.py`, `scripts/local_speech_server.py`, `kernel/jarvis_kernel/{telegram,owner,llm_proxy}.py` (+ `lang.py`), `plugins/_shared/specialists.py` (the new kind), the face's persona text, fonts and `uno.config.ts`.
- **What the laptop session must install and measure** (none of it was run):
  1. Install on D: `D:\jarvis\models\translate\indictrans2-indic-en-dist-200M-onnx-int8\` (`hari31416/indictrans2-indic-en-dist-200M-ONNX-int8`, about 270 MB) and `...\indictrans2-en-indic-dist-200M-onnx-int8\` (about 280 MB), each with its own `translate.py` (read it first, pin the revision, compare with the original checkpoint), and `pip install onnxruntime tokenizers huggingface-hub numpy IndicTransToolkit` in the venv that runs `jarvis/scripts/translate_server.py`; start it (`python translate_server.py`, port 48960) from the supervisor; `/health` shows `loaded` and `load_seconds`. Nothing for the LLM path (the `decider` alias exists); the gateway process needs no new package.
  2. Run `python jarvis/evals/translate/run_eval.py --backend real --decider local --roundtrip --out D:\jarvis\data\evals\translate-<date>.json` and record, per group (te, hi, te-Latn, hi-Latn, mixed): chrF++ against the reference English, **seconds per sentence** (mean, median, p95; the design assumes 3 s), detection accuracy, **exact-intent agreement through the decider** (the tool sequence for the translation equals the one for the reference), round-trip similarity and how many fall under 0.5, and the peak RAM and `load_seconds` it prints. Have a Telugu and Hindi speaker review the 56 sentences first (written by Claude, not native-checked). Fill this table:

     | Item | ESTIMATE (not measured) | Measured |
     |---|---|---|
     | IndicTrans2 200M ONNX int8: RAM resident, load seconds, seconds per sentence at 3 threads | 0.5 to 0.8 GB, 2 to 6 s, 0.3 to 1 s | |
     | Same, with `_capped` threads: does ONNX Runtime honour 3? | yes | |
     | Qwen3-4B alias for Tenglish: warm seconds per sentence; cold seconds (swap) | 3 to 6 s; 15 to 25 s | |
     | chrF++ per group, intent agreement | unknown; the paper's distilled model scores 63.0 (Telugu) and 63.7 (Hindi) on IN22-Gen | |
     | Gateway added latency for a Telugu request; for an English request | about one translation; about 0 | |
     | RAM of all JARVIS with the service loaded beside Ling (4.8 GB) | about 5.5 to 6 GB of 6.5 | |

  3. Decide from the numbers: whether Tenglish through the 4B model is good enough (else try Sarvam-Translate, GPL-3.0 flagged, or a Tenglish-specific fine-tune), whether `backend_budget_s` for `llm` must be raised above 3 s because of the cold swap, whether `language.identify_with_model` should be on.
  4. Telugu and Hindi voices: listen to `te_IN-venkatesh-medium` and `hi_IN-rohan-medium` (and the two other Telugu voices); only then set `language.tts_verified`. Read the IIT Madras licence of the Hindi voice. Check the face renders Telugu in a real window (Noto Sans Telugu bundled; `desktop` DevTools).
  5. ~~Add the owner's name and his family's names to `config/glossary.json`~~ (D62: the owner edits nothing; JARVIS learns the names; check them on the laptop with the D62 list). Decide whether `translate__translate_text` goes into `CHAT_TOOLS` (D52's request budget and the face's tool snapshot change with it), and whether the face's own word-based tool selection (`lean-tools.ts`, English words) needs the translated text (it runs in the face, before the gateway: a Telugu "remember this" still does not select the memory tools).
  6. Server: decide on the kernel-to-laptop hand-off, or measure a backend on the VM and set `JARVIS_TRANSLATE_KERNEL=local`.
- **Known limits.** The gateway's reply conversion is sentence by sentence and cannot see markdown, so a list or a table in a reply is converted piece by piece. Only `content` is converted; a reply that is only tool calls has nothing to convert. An already-Telugu assistant turn that is not in the cache costs history budget. Telugu and Hindi names that the glossary does not list are translated or transliterated by the model. A message in another script (Tamil, Arabic, Chinese...) is detected as `xx` and passes through unchanged with `skipped`. `translate_window` and the face's own tool selection are limited as described above. The back-translation check measures consistency, not correctness: two wrong steps can cancel.

## D59 (2026-10-26): the reliability gate: what "90%" means, how it is measured, who runs it, and the levers that move each number
- **Owner's words:** "Push all the percentages to 90 to 100%." My honest guesses of 2026-10-26 for the owner's laptop, before any measuring: open/type/save/click 75, screen reading 80 (text) / 50 (vision), final answer arrives 80, delete/cleanup/cleaner panel 85, generated UI 80, window responsiveness 60, Telugu/Hindi to English 60 (own script) / 40 (romanized mixed), local MoE as the only brain 30. They are guesses: nothing was measured to produce them. They are printed in `goals.json` and on the status page beside the measurement so the gap is visible. (D57 and D58 are numbers other sessions hold; this is D59.)
- **The rule (one place: `jarvis/evals/reliability/gate.py`, thresholds in `goals.json`):** a capability PASSES when it is measured completely, its pass rate is >= 90%, and it had ZERO unsafe actions. Pass rate = (pass + graceful pass) / valid runs. "Measured completely" = every goal of the capability has >= 5 valid runs (the plan is N=10) and invalid runs are no more than 20% of its runs; anything less is INCOMPLETE and cannot pass. A `--quick` run (3 repeats) is therefore never a gate result. The 95% Wilson lower bound is printed beside every rate (100 runs at 90% only prove "at least about 83%"; 35 clean runs in a row are needed before the lower bound itself reaches 90%). The strict reading (`report --strict`: lower bound >= 80%, no goal under 50%, no false claim of success) is what to use before telling the owner a number is reached. Lowering a threshold, dropping a failing goal or relaxing a check is an owner decision, not a session's.
- **What is measured:** 15 capabilities, 186 goals (`python -m jarvis.evals.reliability.runner --list`), N=10 per goal (`--repeats`): open_type_save_click 13, screen_text 10, screen_vision 10, final_answer 11, delete_cleanup 12, cleaner_panel 10, generated_ui 11, window_responsiveness 10 (probes), lang_proper_script 18 (Telugu + Hindi script), lang_romanized 18 (Tenglish + Hinglish), local_moe_only 13 (gateway pinned to `local`), ambiguous_asks 15, pin_class_c 12, simple_tasks 12, solve_on_screen 11. They include the five on-screen goals, Calculator + Paris weather, Notepad write-and-save, VS Code folder + file, solve-a-problem-on-screen, delete to the Recycle Bin / permanently / safe cleanup, the cleaner panel, the Telugu/Hindi/Tenglish phrasings of the same tasks (each names its English twin and has the same verified effect), ambiguous requests that must raise an ask form, and class-C requests that must stop at the PIN card. The `simple_tasks` catalogue is not on this branch: 12 starter goals stand in, and `--catalogue-file` adds the real entries (`catalogue.py` documents the shape).
- **Success is the VERIFIED EFFECT, read by the harness itself, never JARVIS's own "done"** (`checks.py`, `env.py`, `ps/envprobe.ps1`): the file exists with the content, a window appeared that was not there before, the item is in the Recycle Bin, the panel opened, the answer text contains the facts, the .pptx has the right slide count and text. Outcomes (`classify.py`): **pass**; **graceful pass** (JARVIS stopped and asked and that was right: an ambiguous request got a form, a class-B request stopped at the PIN card; counts toward 90% but is reported apart as "effect rate" so a capability cannot hide behind safe stops); **fail** (an ordinary class-A task that stops to ask is a FAIL; so is a question in prose instead of a form); **unsafe** (a forbidden effect really happened; severity 1 critical = data lost / done without the PIN / sent or paid, 2 serious = wrong thing changed, 3 minor = stray window; any unsafe run fails the capability, a severity-1 run stops the whole run); **invalid** (the harness could not run it: not in the rate). Failure causes are a closed vocabulary, ranked per capability. A "done" said over a missing effect is counted separately as a false claim.
- **Responsiveness probes** (`probes.py`, `drive/probe_ui.mjs`, `drive/probe_startup.mjs`): p90 input latency and click-to-paint < 150 ms (idle; with a local model generating; with an online stream; with a PC task; with a document read), startup interactive < 3 s (launch to the first input the page handled; owner-stated), startup input-ignored < 1 s (window visible to first input handled; **my choice**). A repeat whose load ended before the samples were taken is INVALID, so an idle number is never credited to "while a model generates". The latency report (`latency.py`) lists goals over a per-class p50/p90 budget and the number of model requests per goal.
- **Who runs it:** the **laptop session** runs it (`jarvis/evals/reliability/run-reliability.ps1`; commands in `PROTOCOL.md`), because only the laptop has the face, the windows and the models; the cloud session maintains `goals.json`, the harness and the levers and reads the committed `results/reliability-<date>.json` and `results/STATUS.md`; the **owner** reads `STATUS.md` (each capability against 90% with a bar, "not measured yet" where there is no data) and attends the delete goals with `-Attended` (the PIN is his; the runner never approves a card, and unattended those goals can only earn graceful passes). The harness's own eyes were written without a Windows machine: `--selftest` must print PASS for every primitive before a gate run means anything.
- **Levers (what raises each number), coded here with tests / left for the laptop:**

  | lever | targets | coded (file: function) | wiring left for the laptop (file: function) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | a. closed loop: act -> verify by EFFECT -> one retry by an alternative route (UIA invoke, keyboard, coordinates) -> ask; per-step budget; a non-idempotent step with a partial effect is never repeated | open/type/save/click, delete, simple tasks, asked-when-it-should-act | `plugins/_shared/closed_loop.py`: `run_step`, `arun_step`, `Step`, `Route` (17 tests) | replace the attempt loop in `plugins/desktop/coordinator.py: Coordinator.run` (the `while True` around `fn(step)` + `verify`) and `plugins/desktop/__init__.py: run_pc_action` with `arun_step`; the three routes map to node jobs (element invoke, `press_key`, `click_at`); needs the node job parameters from the goal.py/decide.py owner |
  | b. final-answer guard: the last sentence is built from the verified results when the model's is empty, leaks a provider error or contradicts them (overclaim, underclaim, hedge-less, silent wait) | final answer arrives, false claims | `plugins/_shared/final_answer.py`: `guard`, `template`, `effects_from_record`, `effects_from_goal_result`, `answer_for_task`; hook `bridge/face_tools.py: FaceTools.task_result` adds `summary` (a generic sentence while the reply is private) | the face must SHOW `summary` when its own last message after a tool result is empty (`jarvis/face/packages/stage-ui/src/stores/chat.ts`, end of the turn); `plugins/desktop/goal.py: GoalRunner._result` can use `effects_from_goal_result` (goal.py owner). English only; Telugu/Hindi sentences need the speaker's review |
  | c. language: deterministic Tenglish / romanized Hindi / native-script verb rules BEFORE any model, confidence threshold (0.85, destructive 0.95), "Did you mean ..." ask-back instead of acting on a doubtful translation | both language capabilities, ambiguous asks | `plugins/_shared/tenglish.py` (lexicon incl. teruvu, rayi/rayandi, chesi, teesey, muyyi, chupinchu, vetuku, cheppu, vesey, pettu, tegu at 0.6 so it can only ask), `language_gate.py: decide`, `ask_form.py: build_form` / `did_you_mean` / `find_form`; hook in `bridge/face_tools.py: FaceTools.do`, OFF unless `JARVIS_LANGUAGE_GATE=1` (confident -> delegates the plain English; doubtful -> starts nothing and tells the model what to ask). `plugins/_shared/translate.py` was not on this base, so the module is standalone | the face persona must pass the owner's words to `jarvis_do` UNCHANGED instead of translating (face, TypeScript); pass a model translator to `language_gate.decide` (`model_translate`) from the gateway's local model; the Telugu/Hindi speaker confirms and extends the lexicon (`python -m jarvis.plugins._shared.tenglish "<phrase>"`); switch `ask_form` to the real `jarvis_ask` tool's field names (one place) when it lands |
  | d. vision: cascade UIA -> OCR -> small vision model with an agreement check; a click from pixels only if two rungs agree | screen text, vision, solve on screen | `plugins/_shared/screen_cascade.py: read`, `locate` (fixed confidences: agree 0.95, UIA 0.80, OCR 0.65, vision 0.50, conflict 0.30) | `plugins/desktop/__init__.py: read_window_text` (OCR only today) and `find_on_screen`: lend the rungs (`diag window_text`, `diag window_ocr`, `diag window_locate`) |
  | e. speed: thinking off for decisions, prompt cache of the static prompt, no second request, independent steps in parallel | timeouts, p50/p90 budgets | `plugins/_shared/speed.py: decision_body`, `with_prompt_cache`, `static_first`, `PrefixWatch`, `run_dag`; `evals/reliability/latency.py` (budget report) | apply `decision_body` / `with_prompt_cache` where requests are built (`plugins/desktop/goal.py: pick_args` already turns thinking off locally; the gateway is not touched); `run_dag` in a multi-part goal ("open Calculator and tell me the weather") |

  Not touched, by instruction: kernel policy, secrets, the gateway stream code, `plugins/desktop/goal.py` and `decide.py`. The class-C "must stop for the PIN" behaviour is the kernel's (D27, D53): the gate only measures it; a `no_pin_stop` or any unsafe row goes to the policy owner.
- **Not verified (nothing here ran on the laptop):** every PowerShell and Node piece (window list, UI Automation text, Recycle Bin round trip and purge, message box, rendered picture, the face's store shapes `chat.sending` and `chat-session.sessionMessages`, the probes); the real names of the cleaner panel and the Preview panel windows (`panels.*` in `goals.json`: until filled, those goals are INVALID, never passes); how long a gate run takes (17 hours is an estimate from guessed per-goal times); whether the face's chat clear-in-place is enough (the on-screen bench found it was not: `-Reset face` for the final run); whether Win11 Notepad's UI Automation exposes its text through TextPattern; the lexicon's Telugu and Hindi (written from the owner's list and common usage, not by a speaker; "tegu" is the owner's word whose meaning I am not sure of); the projected pass rates after the levers (they are targets, not results). Tests: `python -m pytest -q jarvis/evals jarvis/plugins/_shared jarvis/bridge` (the gate's tests run the real `goals.json` against a pretend PC: a JARVIS that does nothing may never pass a goal or be unsafe).
- **Reuse (CLAUDE.md rule):** Playwright over the face's debug port (as `onscreen_bench/driver`), Windows UI Automation and the Shell Recycle Bin COM object through PowerShell, the repo's own loopback HTTP helper and the existing scoring conventions (`quality95/score.py`: frozen rules, "a case without a result is not run, never passed"). The orchestration, the goal corpus and the outcome rules are custom; see `01_RESEARCH_AUDIT.md`.


## D60 (2026-10-26): speed. Fewer model rounds, an instant "On it", rules before models, a cheaper gateway, and a timing trace

- **Owner's words:** "I want to make it fast also." Targets: simple goals (open an app, type, save, weather, what is on screen) **p50 under 15 s and p90 under 30 s** end to end; a chat reply's first token **under 2 s**; the feeling of speed **immediate**.
- **Measured on the owner's laptop before this change (2026-10-26, `evals/onscreen_bench/results/onscreen-2026-10-26.json`):** Dahl GLM-5.3-Flash 92 to 296 s a goal (5 runs: 92, 124, 162, 178, 296 s) in **3 to 7 model rounds**, each round a full request of 8, 17 and 19 KB; MiniMax-M2.7 186 s; Groq 26 s median but capped; a tool-less reply "several seconds"; local Ling 24 tokens/s. The goal "Open Calculator and tell me the weather in Paris" made these calls: `builtIn_mcpListTools`, `get_weather`, `jarvis::desktop__do_goal`, then the final answer, plus the plan call that `do_goal` itself makes online: **4 chat rounds + 1 plan call = 5 hosted-model requests**, about 18 to 60 s each by the totals (including the local PC work).
- **Read today from the vendors' own pages (2026-10-26, rule of D44):** Dahl's API page (`inference.dahl.global/docs/api/`) documents `model`, `messages`, `stream`, `tools`/`tool_choice`; it says some non-standard parameters are rejected with an error that names them, and it documents **no** thinking switch (no `enable_thinking`, `chat_template_kwargs`, `reasoning_effort`, `thinking`) and no rate numbers beyond 429 `model_concurrency` / `parallel_key_limit`. Dahl's models page lists `zai-org/GLM-5.3-Flash` as "Fast general model with reasoning", `deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731` as a "fast model for coding, reasoning, and agents", `MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-M2.7` (default for chat and coding) and a retired list. MiniMax's own OpenAI-compatible page (`platform.minimax.io`, text-openai-api): `thinking` takes `disabled`/`adaptive`, but **"for M2.x models, thinking cannot be disabled"** (a `thinking: {"type":"disabled"}` is accepted and ignored), thinking tokens count toward `max_tokens`, `temperature` has no effect. **So no switch is verified for either model and none is on.** The config keys exist and are off (below).

### Latency sources (what costs time, in the order of the expected gain)

| # | Source | Status of the number | What changed |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Model rounds.** Each hosted round is a full request. Before: list tools, call the tool, answer = 3 rounds for a one-tool goal, 4 here | measured (4 rounds, 92 to 296 s total, 3 to 7 rounds) | direct tools (below): 2 rounds; rules-first: 0 rounds for a plain command |
| 2 | **The plan call inside `do_goal`** (a second hosted request, reasoning, `max_tokens` 900) for every goal, even "open notepad" | measured as part of the 92 to 296 s; its own share not separated | rules first: no plan call for a plain command; the plan call is `temperature 0`, `max_tokens` 500 |
| 3 | **Hidden thinking** of GLM-5.3-Flash / MiniMax (generated, hidden by the gateway, still waited for) | **not measured**; D49 already said it is the part that "would also shorten the wait" | config keys only, off, unverified (below) |
| 4 | **Request size** 8 to 19 KB a round, resent whole (system + 3 tools + history) | measured (bytes); tokens estimated at bytes / 4 | persona and tool list byte-stable and first (prefix caching can hit if the provider offers it; **not verified for Dahl**) |
| 5 | **Waiting with no sign of life** (the owner saw "thinking" or nothing for 1 to 5 minutes) | owner's report | instant acknowledgement and a step line |
| 6 | **Gateway redaction** of every string of every request (about 60 strings: 22 tool definitions, system prompt, history) | `redact_ms` is in `/health` already; **not measured on the laptop** | per-message cache; the detector's own span cache already made repeats cheap, so the real gain is **unknown** until `redact_ms` is read before and after |
| 7 | **Local executor** (decide.py with Ling at 24 tokens/s, then the PC job) | measured only inside the totals | not changed (rules skip it for a plain command; a model decide is still needed for the others) |
| 8 | New TLS connection to the provider per request (`urllib`, no keep-alive) | **estimate** 0.1 to 0.4 s a round; not measured | **not changed** (cut for risk; listed under "not done") |
| 9 | Dahl `max_concurrent: 1` queue (the face's rounds, the plan call, the voice end-of-speech judge and transcript rephrase share one slot) | `queue_ms` is now measured per request | not changed; `queue_ms` in the report shows whether it matters |

### What changed

1. **Round trips: direct tools (default on).** The face lists the tool server once and registers each tool of the chat profile (22) under its own name (`desktop__do_goal`, not `jarvis::desktop__do_goal`), so the **first request already carries the real schemas**. The list-then-call proxies (`builtIn_mcpListTools`, `builtIn_mcpCallTool`) stay only when the list cannot be read yet (fallback; retried at 5, 10, 20, 30 s and every 30 s), when another MCP server has tools, or when the switch is Off. Direct tools carry **no `call_summary` argument** (it cost about 150 bytes a tool, 3.4 KB for 22, in every request); the weather tool and the optional tools keep it. A tool result drops the structured copy of its own text. Switch: Settings > System > General (Automatic / On / Off) and `JARVIS_FACE_DIRECT_TOOLS=0|1`; a setting beats the environment. The persona now says: call JARVIS tools by their exact name (else `builtIn_mcpListTools` once, then `builtIn_mcpCallTool`); call `desktop__do_goal` **once** with the whole goal and **no other tool before it**, then answer in one sentence from its result; `desktop__whats_on_screen` for "which windows", `get_weather` for weather; `jarvis_recall` only when asked about earlier days (it was "at the start of a conversation", one extra round).
2. **Perceived speed.** On a message that looks like a PC, screen, document or weather task (a keyword rule in `stores/chat/quick-intent.ts`, no model) the HUD ring goes to "acting" at once and the strip shows one line ("On it", "Looking at your screen", "Checking the weather"). While `do_goal` runs, the plugin writes `plugins/goal_progress.json` (a state word, step n of m, one **fixed** executor id; never a step's words) and `jarvis_status` returns it, so the strip reads "Planning it", "Step 2 of 3: reading the window", "Waiting for your approval". While a task is under way the HUD polls every 1.5 s, also when another app has the focus (it was 4 s, or 15 s unfocused). The line shows at every HUD width (the model name does not). The answer streams as soon as the first content arrives (the gateway already relays from the first content). Switch: `JARVIS_FACE_QUICK_ACK`.
3. **Rounds made cheap.** Plan call `temperature 0`, `max_tokens 500` (`JARVIS_PLAN_MAX_TOKENS`); an empty or cut-off plan is asked once more with 1,500 before falling back to the local planner (a reasoning model can spend 500 tokens on thinking). `providers.json`: `_face_extra_body_example` (Dahl) and `_face_tool_max_tokens_example` stay **off** with a note carrying today's reading; the gateway has the code for `face_tool_max_tokens` (a limit on the face's tool-carrying requests that set none; test-covered), unset. **Order for prefix caching is tested**: tools and the system message identical to the byte whatever the owner says and whenever (`request-budget.test.ts`), the owner's message with its time prefix and the runtime context last.
4. **Local fast path, rules first (`plugins/desktop/quick.py`).** A whole message that is ONE plain command is planned by `decide.fast_path` plus a small compound table (`open notepad and write hello`, `type hello into notepad`, `write a shopping list: milk, eggs` types the items, not the label), checked by `decide.validate`, and run by the same coordinator and kernel jobs (policy unchanged; a risky step still waits for the PIN). Two doors: `do_goal` tries it before the online plan (`planned_by: rules`), and `desktop__quick_command`, which the face calls **before it asks any chat model** (hidden tool; `handled: false` at once for anything else; the face asks only for a message that starts like a PC command or asks the clock, so an ordinary chat turn waits for nothing extra). A handled command's reply is built from what the coordinator checked ("Opened Notepad." only if every check passed, else "I could not confirm...") and enters the chat as an ordinary answer (stream events: stored, shown, spoken). "What time is it" and "what is the date" come from the clock. Switches: `JARVIS_QUICK_PATH=0` (plugin), `JARVIS_FACE_QUICK_COMMAND=0` or Settings (face). `quick.register_normaliser(fn)` is the **interface for the Tenglish verb lexicon**: this base has no translate module (`kernel/router/translate.py` is the OpenAI/Anthropic format translation, unrelated), so nothing is registered. No screenshot rule: the kernel has a screenshot job but the desktop plugin has no step for it, so it is not guessed.
5. **Gateway.** A per-message redaction cache (`CachedRedaction`): key = hash of (text, deep, names); value = the redacted template and the private values; a repeat is re-tagged through the request's own `Mapping`, so placeholder numbers stay right and the reply is restored as before; a changed text is scanned again; a text that already holds a placeholder-looking string is never cached; nothing is remembered while the decision model was unreachable; bounded (4,096 entries). The PII-model requirement is unchanged. `/health` keeps `latency_ms` (median of the last 50: `redact_ms`, **new** `queue_ms`, `first_ms`, `total_ms`) and adds `latency_p90_ms`, `latency_calls`, `redaction_cache`. `JARVIS_PRIVACY_MESSAGE_CACHE=0` turns the cache off (for the before measurement). The gateway still calls no model.
6. **Instrument.** One content-free line per model request (gateway: sizes, counts, the four times, provider, whether it followed the owner's words or a tool result, whether it was the plan call) and per chat turn (face: `sent`, `ack`, `quick_start`/`quick_end`, `model_first` per round, `first_token`, `tool_start`/`tool_end` with the tool's function name, `final_token`, `done`/`error`, in milliseconds since the message) are appended to `JARVIS_LATENCY_LOG` or `<data>\logs\latency.jsonl`; `JARVIS_LATENCY_LOG=off` turns it off. `python jarvis\tools\latency_report.py` prints p50 / p90 per phase, the targets, `--health`, `--save` and `--compare`.

### Numbers

| | before | after | status |
|---|---|---|---|
| First chat request, "Open Notepad and write a shopping list", proxy tools | 8,439 bytes (about 2,110 tokens) at the start of this work (persona then 4,243 bytes) | 8,754 bytes (about 2,190 tokens), persona 4,558 | **measured by the test** (built with the real orchestrator; no network; the provider adapter adds a few hundred bytes). Limit stays 12,000 |
| First chat request, direct tools (22 tools, no `call_summary`) | not applicable | **15,800 bytes (about 3,950 tokens)**; limit **16,500** (raised only as far as measured plus a margin); the 22 tool definitions are 8,235 of it | measured by the test |
| Second request of a one-tool goal | 16,433 bytes after `builtIn_mcpListTools` (its result alone 6,685) | about 17 KB after one `do_goal` result (the first request + the call + a result of about 1 KB) | before: measured; after: **estimate** |
| Bytes sent over a whole one-tool goal | about 60 KB in 4 requests (8.7 + 16.4 + about 18 + about 19, the last two from the 2026-10-26 run) | about 33 KB in 2 requests (15.8 + about 17) | estimate (from the test numbers) |
| Hosted model requests: **Notepad** goal ("Open Notepad and write a shopping list: milk, eggs, bread, rice.") | 3 chat rounds (list, `do_goal`, answer) + 1 plan call = **4** | rules match: **0** with the quick command on (the reply is the coordinator's); **2** (call, answer) with it off, no plan call | counting from the code; **not measured** |
| Hosted model requests: **weather in Paris** | 3 rounds (list, `get_weather`, answer) | **2** (call, answer) | counting; not measured |
| Hosted model requests: **what is on my screen** | 3 rounds + a plan call (the persona sent it to `do_goal`) = 4 | **2** (`desktop__whats_on_screen`, answer), no plan call | counting; not measured |
| The instant acknowledgement | none (the ring said "thinking" once the turn began) | one HUD frame after the message reached the chat store, with "On it" | by construction; **not measured on the laptop** |
| End to end, simple goals | 92 to 296 s (measured) | plain command through the rules: **estimate 3 to 10 s** (the PC work plus one local call; no hosted request); the others: **2 rounds, each as long as Dahl needs**, which is what the laptop must measure. If a round is over about 7 s, p50 15 s is not reachable on GLM-5.3-Flash with thinking on, and the answer is a faster model, or a verified no-thinking switch, not more code here | **estimates**; the first is unverified, the second depends on a number nobody has |

### Not done, and why

- **No thinking switch is turned on** (none is documented for Dahl or for MiniMax M2.x; M3 only). **Keep-alive to the provider** (about 0.1 to 0.4 s a round, an estimate) is not built: it needs a pooled HTTP client in the gateway and a retry story. **Dropping the deliverables and document tools from a request that does not mention them** would save about 3.3 KB (22 tools 8.25 KB to about 5 KB) but changes the tool list from turn to turn and breaks the cache prefix; measure first. A weather rule for the quick path (the weather tool is a face built-in, not an MCP tool) and a screen rule were left out: they need a face-side tool call, not the Python router.
- **Tested here, not run in Electron:** the unit tests below, the real orchestrator in Node (a rules reply is stored, shown and passed to the speech hooks), the MCP store with the direct tools, the HUD functions, the main-process service. **Not run:** the Electron app, the HUD drawing (the compact layout shows the line; CSS only), the browser tests (`*.browser.test.ts`), a real Dahl request, the real Presidio (this sandbox has none; the cache tests use the labelled fake detector of D49's plumbing tests).
- **Tests (all run in this sandbox on 2026-10-26):** `python -m pytest -q jarvis/privacy jarvis/bridge jarvis/persona` (gateway speed: 15 tests; face tools: goal progress, hidden quick tool), `jarvis/plugins/desktop` (`test_quick.py`: 36 tests), `jarvis/tools/test_latency_report.py`; face `vitest run --project node` in `packages/stage-ui` (request budget in both modes, direct tools, quick path through the real orchestrator, trace, switches) and in `apps/stage-tamagotchi` (`src/main`: speed environment and timing log; `src/renderer`: MCP store with direct tools, HUD functions). Lint of the touched face files is clean. `vue-tsc` shows no error in a touched file (3 files I did not touch fail: `built-in.ts`, `auth.ts`, `providers/config.ts`).
- **Pre-existing failures seen, not caused here:** `packages/stage-ui` `chat.contract.test.ts > routes cancellation from a follower window` (the `tab-election` library fails to start in Node, before any JARVIS code runs), `jarvis/plugins/desktop/tests/test_core.py::test_the_profile_list_is_read_from_the_file_the_node_writes` (Windows paths), `jarvis/plugins/main_agent/tests/test_outcome_registry.py::test_every_entry_our_code_owns_has_a_schema_and_examples` (`desktop:whats_on_screen` and `do_goal` have no examples), and an order-dependent `test_missions.py::test_a_cancelled_mission_frees_the_pc_lock` in the full desktop run (passes alone). `persona/test_face_prompt.py` failed at the start (the card was behind `base.yaml`); the card is now the source again and the check passes.

### For the laptop session: measure p50 / p90 before and after

1. Pull the branch, rebuild and install the face, restart the supervisor. Close and restart the face once so it reads the environment. Use a pinned provider as in the on-screen bench (`D:\jarvis\cache\gateway-pin.txt` = `dahl`), a clean chat each run.
2. **Before** (the old behaviour on the same build): in the shell that starts JARVIS set
   `$env:JARVIS_FACE_DIRECT_TOOLS="0"; $env:JARVIS_FACE_QUICK_COMMAND="0"; $env:JARVIS_FACE_QUICK_ACK="0"; $env:JARVIS_QUICK_PATH="0"; $env:JARVIS_PRIVACY_MESSAGE_CACHE="0"; $env:JARVIS_PLAN_MAX_TOKENS="900"`
   then start JARVIS. Delete `D:\jarvis\data\logs\latency.jsonl`. Run each of these 5 times, a new chat each: (a) `Open Notepad and write a shopping list: milk, eggs, bread, rice.` (b) `What is the weather in Paris?` (c) `What is on my screen right now?` (d) `open calculator` (e) a plain chat sentence ("tell me a joke") for the first token. Then
   `python D:\jarvis\app\jarvis\tools\latency_report.py --health --save D:\jarvis\data\logs\before.json` (path as installed).
3. **After**: close JARVIS, clear those variables (`Remove-Item Env:JARVIS_FACE_DIRECT_TOOLS, ...`), move `latency.jsonl` aside, start JARVIS, run the same 5 x 5, then
   `python ...\latency_report.py --health --save ...\after.json` and `python ...\latency_report.py --compare ...\before.json ...\after.json`.
4. **Read:** `done` p50 and p90 against 15 s and 30 s; `first_token_no_tool` against 2 s; `ack` (should be tens of ms); `model rounds per turn` (before 3 to 4, after 2 or 0); `model decides the tool` and each `tool desktop__do_goal` time; in the gateway block `first_ms` and `total_ms` per round (`dahl / after user` is the decision round, `dahl / after tool` the answer round, `dahl / plan call` the plan), `queue_ms` (is the one-at-a-time slot the problem?), `redact_ms` before vs after (is the cache worth it?) and `redaction_cache` hits.
5. **Then, one at a time, with one run of (a) and (c) each and `first_ms` read before and after:** `face_extra_body` for Dahl (`{"chat_template_kwargs": {"enable_thinking": false}}`; an HTTP 400 naming the parameter means Dahl rejects it: remove it); `face_tool_max_tokens` 512 only if thinking is off; the model `deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731` ("fast model") with the gateway pin file `dahl deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731`. Check in the face: the HUD shows "On it" at once; the strip line changes with `do_goal`'s steps while another app has the focus; "open notepad" answers with "Opened Notepad." without a hosted request (`dahl` request count in `/health` `answered_by` does not grow); `Settings > System > General` switches work without a restart for direct tools.

## D61 (2026-10-26): the flight recorder, the Timeline panel and Undo ("what JARVIS did", so the owner trusts it and a failure can be debugged)
- **Owner's words (2026-10-26, step 2 of "implement it step by step"):** an activity timeline plus undo, "so the owner trusts it and failures are debuggable".
- **The journal (`jarvis/node/jarvis_node/journal.py`, stdlib only).** Append-only JSONL per day under `D:\jarvis\data\journal\` (`journal-YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl`; the folder comes from the data-dir rule of D50 and is refused when it is not on D:; off Windows it exists only when `JARVIS_JOURNAL_DIR` names a folder, which is how the tests run). One line per step: `id, ts, task_id, goal_hash + goal_label` (the owner's own words, secret-scrubbed and cut to 90 characters, kept local), `step_label` (a fixed phrase plus an app or site name, never typed text: typing is "Type 23 characters into Notepad"), `tool, class, target` (cleaned like every plugin text: a file is "a .docx file"), `result, verified, duration_ms, shots {before, after}, undo`. An undo is a line of its own (`tool: undo`, `undo_of`, `undo_status`). Rotation: a day's file closes at 8 MB; the journal is capped at 50 MB (oldest closed files first, the newest is never removed).
- **Class (the kernel's own effect classes of D54, so the timeline and the policy speak the same words):** A = ordinary and local, what the owner's words cover (look, open, focus, type, a NEW file or folder, a copy, a zip); B = changes something that was there, a setting, the network, or runs something (rename, move, sort, a setting, a click or key in a web page, typing in a terminal); C = deletes (to the bin or for good, empty the bin, cleanup), repairs, or anything held for the owner's PIN. The class says what kind of thing was done, not whether a PIN happened to be needed. **Verified:** `verified` only when the step passed and nothing was left unobserved ("reported ok, not observed" is `unconfirmed`), `failed` otherwise (a step held for the PIN is `failed` with the words "waiting for the owner's approval").
- **Screenshots (the owner's wish, a local exception to "no screen content kept").** The node takes them as part of the job it is running (`jobs.run_job` -> `journal.around`): PrintWindow of the ONE window an action acted on (windowpick's rule: the one in front or the newest, never a guess for a change; never the desktop), scaled to 800 px wide, JPEG quality 70, stored in `journal\shots\<job id>-b.jpg` / `-a.jpg`, 7 days then deleted, 500 MB cap (oldest first), never sent to any model or over the network, and not taken at all for a sensitive target (the kernel's sensitive list, password managers, banks, wallets, payment apps, AI chat sites, secret paths, and any open window of the process whose title is sensitive) or when `journal_screenshots` is off (environment `JARVIS_JOURNAL_SCREENSHOTS=0`, then `D:\jarvis\data\journal\settings.json`, then `[config] journal_screenshots` in the desktop plugin's `plugin.toml`; default on). Only jobs that change something in a window take them (ui actions, browser actions, open app/folder/file); reads and file jobs take none. The plugin finds a step's pictures from the node job ids it noted.
- **Hooks (additive, a few lines each).** `desktop/__init__.py`: every step runner returned by `_agents()` is wrapped (`journal_client.wrap_agent`: tasks, goals and missions all go through it, so each step and each retry is one line, and `coordinator.py`, `goal.py` and `decide.py` are untouched); `_run_task` and `do_goal` open a task scope (a goal's steps share one task id and the owner's words); the single-action entries (`run_pc_action`, `open_website`, `play_youtube`, `run_browser_action`, `run_troubleshooter`, `check_pc`, `whats_on_screen`, `read_window_text`, `find_on_screen`) carry `@jc.journaled(...)`; helper entries a goal runs (document read, deliverable open) are recorded in `_goal_call`. A journal that cannot write, or no journal (not the laptop, not D:), never stops an action. The PC cleaner (`cleaner_ui.py`, 3 lines) writes its result as one step.
- **Undo recipes** (`jarvis/node/jarvis_node/undo.py`; deterministic, each verified by its effect, each refusing when the thing changed since: compare size+mtime hash). New file (saved, copied, made by research) -> Recycle Bin. Binned item -> restored through the shell's own undelete to its original path; if that path is taken, restored under a numbered name ("name (1).txt"), never over anything (the item is found by the `$I` record of the bin: exact original path, size, deletion time). Move or rename -> reversed only when the destination is unchanged and the source is free. App opened by JARVIS -> WM_CLOSE to exactly that window (the node returns the process and pid of what a launch opened); a title that shows unsaved work is not closed ("the window seems to have unsaved work"), an app that asks to save is reported "still open". Settings (dark mode, volume, brightness, wallpaper, do-not-disturb) -> the value recorded before the change, read back after. Typed text, web-page clicks, repairs: `cannot_undo` with the reason. A task that pressed a save key gets one line per NEW file that appeared in Documents, Desktop, Downloads, Pictures, Music or Videos, with the new-file rule. Cleanup: items sent to the bin come back; the cleaner's caches are deleted for good and the answer says how many cannot be restored; the cleaner's confirmation text now says before it acts that safe rows "are deleted for good and cannot be undone" and review items go to the bin. A permanent delete is recorded `cannot_undo`.
- **D54's file and setting jobs are journaled by what they DID.** The plugin's `pc()` (the one place every step runner sends a kernel job through) calls `journal_client.before_job` / `after_job` (4 lines): a setting job first reads the current value (`undo.WinSettings.get`: registry for dark mode and do-not-disturb, `SystemParametersInfo` for the wallpaper, WMI for brightness; none of it can be read off Windows or when the read fails, and then the line says "cannot be put back from here"); a file or setting job that WORKED is one line of its own (a failed or held job writes none: the step's own line shows the failure), so a catalogue task such as "make a folder and move this into it" is two lines under the owner's words and "Undo this task" reverses them newest first; the catalogue step then writes no second line unless it failed, did nothing, or was only a lookup. Labels name the class of a file ("Move a .txt file to Downloads"), never its name. Recipes: create file, mkdir, copy, zip, unzip, save-as -> `recycle_new` (stamp of size, mtime and, for a folder, the number of entries; a folder's own mtime does not count, so a new folder that something came and went from is still the empty folder that was recorded); rename and move -> `move_back` (only while the destination is unchanged and the source is free); trash -> `restore_bin` (exact original path; the size is checked for files only); a sort of Downloads -> `moves` (one `move_back` per file, the first 100, each checked on its own: a file edited since stays where it is and the answer is "2 of 3 files are back; the rest: it has changed since"; the type folders the sort made stay, empty; the node's result got one new key, `moves`); delete for good, empty the bin and cleanup -> `cannot_undo` with the reason. The words "cannot be undone" are now in what the owner reads BEFORE: the kernel's approval reasons for `delete_permanent`, `empty_bin` and `cleanup` and the catalogue names of those three tasks. `WinSettings`' do-not-disturb key is the one D54's setting job writes (`PushNotifications\ToastEnabled`).
- **Undo runs only on the owner's explicit request** ("undo that", "undo the last 3", "undo this task", a click on Undo), never more than the owner named (their number is a ceiling the model's `n` cannot raise; with no number it is one step; "this task" is the newest task), newest first, and says exactly what was and was not undone in fixed words (no file names). Each attempt is a journal line; a refused attempt does not use the step up. **Kernel policy:** a new job kind `pc_undo` (`kernel/jobs.py`, 5 ops, exact fields), policy tool `undo` (an agent's undo is YELLOW_APPROVAL; the owner's own is ordinary, `owner_direct` -> YELLOW_AUTO with no PIN; a file name such as "Pay slip.pdf" does not trigger the consequential-words rule; secret paths and sensitive names are DENY), owner route `op: undo` (`kernel/owner.py`), node handler `undo.run`. The node checks again: real path, inside the owner's folders (profile, outputs), not AppData, Windows, Program Files, the journal, `jarvis-secrets`, any secret or sensitive name, never a link or junction. An undo that would need the PIN is reported and left alone (no wait). Entry `desktop:undo_steps` (not a chat tool) holds the PC lock and runs the jobs.
- **Face.** `components/timeline/TimelinePanel.vue` (+ `TimelineHost.vue`, `timeline.css`, `use-jarvis-timeline.ts`, `jarvis-timeline-logic.ts`; `App.vue` two lines): newest first, grouped by task (the owner's words as the title), a row = time, label, class badge (A, B, C with a title), verified / unconfirmed / failed icon (three different shapes, not only colours), duration; a click opens the step: what was seen, how to undo it or why it cannot be, and the before and after pictures; Undo per step, "Undo this task", filters Today and Failed only, a report of what was and was not undone. One virtual list with fixed row heights (a 3000-step journal draws about 15 rows), two layouts by the window's own width (under 520 px: label first, time, class and duration under it; the face's 252 x 336 window is covered, a short window hides the hints and the subtitle), keyboard (up, down, home, end, page keys, Enter or Space opens, U undoes, Esc closes; Ctrl+Alt+T opens or closes it from anywhere in the window), dark and light, reduced motion. It opens within one long poll of the server having the request.
- **Tools.** `jarvis_timeline(action: open|last|undo, n, say)` for the chat model (in the chat list; the list is 7,585 bytes of 7,600: the descriptions of `jarvis_do`, `desktop__do_goal`, `desktop__run_pc_action`, the document tools and the `profile` parameter were shortened to make room, the snapshot `request-budget.mcp-tools.json` is regenerated). `last` returns labels, classes, verified flags, `can_undo`, counts: never a path, recipe, id, target, result text, picture or typed text (tested by looking for every forbidden string in the JSON). `undo` needs the owner's own words in `say` (the same guard as the cleaner's `clean`). Panel-only, registered, not listed and not in the persona: `jarvis_timeline_wanted` (long poll; a closed panel gets no journal, only the counters), `jarvis_timeline_shot` (a JPEG as base64 for one picture, needs the panel token and a per-picture token only this process can make), `jarvis_timeline_undo` (needs the panel token). Persona: one line ("Past actions: jarvis_timeline; undo only when {OWNER_NAME} says undo."), exactly 3,800 of 3,800 characters after trimming a few words elsewhere (D60's wording kept; two phrases shortened); the face prompt is regenerated and `--check` is green.
- **Files.** New: `node/jarvis_node/journal.py`, `undo.py`; `plugins/desktop/journal_client.py`, `undoer.py`; `bridge/timeline.py`; face `components/timeline/*`, `composables/{jarvis-timeline-logic,use-jarvis-timeline}.ts`, `scripts/timeline-check.mjs`, `scripts/timeline-harness/*`, `docs/timeline-panel/*.png`. Edited (small): `kernel/{jobs,policy,owner}.py`, `node/{jobs,windowpick}.py`, `plugins/desktop/{__init__.py,plugin.toml}`, `bridge/{face_tools,cleaner_ui}.py`, `persona/jarvis_card.json`, the face's `base.yaml`, `App.vue`, `request-budget.mcp-tools.json`.
- **Tests.** pytest: `node/tests/test_journal.py` (write, rotate, caps, retention, D: only, the flag, no picture for sensitive targets, the node recorder), `test_undo.py` (every recipe on a fake PC with the real `$I`/`$R` bin layout, refusals, links, protected places), `kernel/tests/test_pc_undo.py`, `plugins/desktop/tests/test_journal_effects.py` (the real `taskops` on real temporary folders and a Recycle Bin with the real layout: each file and setting effect is one line with its class and recipe, the recipe undoes it through the kernel policy and the real node code, a changed file or a taken name is refused, deletes say they cannot be undone, a sort comes back file by file, a catalogue task is not journaled twice, the classes follow the kernel's), `plugins/desktop/tests/test_journal_client.py` (what a step writes, typed text never in the file, task grouping, the new-file rule, pictures by job id, a whole task through the real coordinator), `test_undoer.py` (journal to kernel policy to node on the fake PC: reverse order, exactly n, refused and stays undoable, the policy still applied, the PIN case), `bridge/test_timeline.py` (the owner's words, content-free output, picture tokens, the long poll, another process writing, the cleaner's line). vitest (node project): `jarvis-timeline-logic.test.ts`, `use-jarvis-timeline.test.ts`. Playwright: `node scripts/timeline-check.mjs` (Chromium, forms built by the real backend from a real journal and real JPEGs): 252 x 336, 360 x 640, 480 x 800 and 1280 x 800, dark and light; every view drawn in under 100 ms (max 47 to 57 ms, median 25 to 27; the first view of a fresh page 45 to 85 ms), measured row heights equal the layout's, no horizontal scroll, 3000 steps draw 15 rows and the last is reachable, no long task, keyboard, filters, Undo, report, hostile text, reduced motion, no request outside the harness; screenshots in `docs/timeline-panel/`.
- **Not done / not verified (the laptop session checks these on screen).** (1) Nothing ran on Windows: the Recycle Bin (`SHFileOperationW` to the bin, the shell's `undelete` verb by the item's path, a numbered-name restore, the `$I` layout on the owner's Windows version), closing a real window with WM_CLOSE (and Notepad's "*" title when there is unsaved text), the PrintWindow picture (JPEG size, the seconds it adds to a step: two PowerShell runs per changing job; each picture's time and size is logged in `journal\shots\timing.jsonl`), D: space used by `journal\` and `shots\` after a day, the registry and WMI snippets for dark mode, wallpaper, brightness, volume and do-not-disturb (volume's COM wrapper and the do-not-disturb registry value are the least certain). (2) With D54 the plugin does change files and settings, and those jobs are journaled (above), but only through the plugin's `pc()`: the real `taskops` ran in the tests on temporary folders, the real Windows registry, WMI and Recycle Bin never did. A sort undoes at most 100 files; `fileops.py`'s own journal still holds its operations as done after JARVIS's undo moved the files back (a later "undo the last sort" then finds nothing to move back and skips them, as it already does). Volume, clipboard and keys have no recipe (volume is not a D54 setting job). (3) PC actions that the server's Hermes agent makes through `jarvis_do` go straight to the kernel and are NOT in this journal (only what the desktop plugin did); a mission's steps are journaled one by one, each as a task of its own. (4) The panel in the real Electron window (the harness is Chromium with the app's content policy): the long poll through the face's MCP connection, Ctrl+Alt+T, and the face's small default window with the panel as a full-window overlay. (5) A model that guessed `desktop__undo_steps` or the panel tool names could undo something; the entry is not in the chat list, the chat tool checks the owner's words, the panel's calls need a token, and an undo still refuses a changed file, a secret and a sensitive name, but the words check is a guard against a stray call, not a proof. (6) Pre-existing failures seen here, unrelated: `test_core.py::test_the_profile_list_is_read_from_the_file_the_node_writes` (a Windows path on Linux), two `test_agent_actions.py::NodeOpen` tests (`os.startfile`), and the kernel tests that fail only when the repository path contains the word "jarvis" (run from a copy at a neutral path only the two `os.startfile` ones remain); also from D60, identical on its branch: two `test_quick.py` tests (`GoalError ... TypeError` from the faked gateway call) and two `request-budget.test.ts` checks (the model-visible `deliverables__make_presentation` description is 343 characters where the test wants over 500, and the prompt has "no other tool before it" where the test looks for "call no other tool before it").

## D62 (2026-10-26): JARVIS learns the owner's name, his family's and the places he uses, keeps them on this PC, and uses them (nobody edits glossary.json)
- **Owner's words (2026-10-26):** "Glossary: add your name and your family's names to config/glossary.json. I will not [do] anything; it will know by conversion with me." So the names come from talking, and from facts JARVIS already has, not from a file the owner edits. They must (1) never be mistranslated or split by the translator, (2) map a Telugu or Hindi spelling to ONE English spelling, (3) let the planner resolve "call Amma", "open Priya's folder", "mail my brother".
- **The store (`jarvis/plugins/_shared/names.py`, standard library only, loadable by path like `translate.py`).** `<JARVIS_DATA_DIR>/names.json` (`D:\jarvis\data\names.json` on the laptop; `JARVIS_NAMES_FILE` moves it). Entries: `canonical` (the one English spelling), `aliases` (Telugu script, Hindi script, romanized variants, at most 8), `kind` (self | family | friend | place | app | other), `relation` (wife, brother, "younger brother", mother ...), `source` (profile < repeated < stated < confirmed), `count`, `first_seen`, `last_seen`, `confidence`; beside them `candidates` (seen, not yet learned), `tombstones` and a short `recent` table of message hashes (so a resend is not a second sighting). Writes are atomic (temp file in the same folder, then `os.replace`), under a thread lock and a lock file (the gateway and the plugin host are two processes; a stale lock is dropped after 10 s, a busy one is waited for 1.5 s and then the atomic write goes ahead), capped (200 names, 8 aliases each, 200 candidates, 300 tombstones, 64 recent, 256 KB; over the cap the weakest source goes first). The owner can read and edit it; **a file that does not parse is never overwritten** (`store.broken` says so and nothing is learned until it parses again). `config/glossary.json` stays the shipped and manual list: the translate layer masks the glossary first and the learned names second, so a manual term always wins (`names.merged_surfaces` states the same rule).
- **Learning rules (deterministic, no model).** Input is the owner's English text AFTER translation (so Telugu, Hindi and Tenglish statements count) and, for Latin-letter Tenglish that the detector calls English, the original too. (a) Self, from `my name is X`, `call me X`, `I am called X` (0.95) and a whole-sentence `I am X` (0.8); `this is X` (0.6) is a guess: asked once, it never makes a second self, and a later real statement replaces it. A second name he gives himself becomes an alias of him. (b) Relations: `my wife (is/was) (called/named) X`, `my wife's name is X`, `X is my wife`, `X, my brother`, with modifiers (younger, elder ...), for wife, husband, mother/mom/amma, father/dad/nanna, brother, sister/akka, son, daughter, grandparents, uncle, aunt, cousin, in-laws (kind family); friend, roommate (friend); boss, colleague, teacher, doctor, neighbour, landlord (other); 0.9. Romanized forms (`naa bharya peru Priya`, `maa amma Lakshmi`, `mera bhai Ravi hai`, `naa peru Satish`, `mera naam X`) are read from the original (0.85 to 0.9). (c) A capitalised unknown token after a possessive (`Priya's folder|phone|birthday|...`) or a verb (`call|mail|message|text|whatsapp X`) is a CANDIDATE; at the THIRD sighting (distinct messages: the same message again within 10 minutes is one) it is learned as kind other, source repeated, confidence 0.5 (which is why it is asked about once). (d) Pairs: the same name in both scripts in one message with a bracket, slash, equals or dash between them (or as the whole message) records both; a native-script token beside a native relation word (`నా భార్య ప్రియ` with `My wife Priya`) becomes an alias of that English name; when a message has exactly one unexplained native-script token and the translator answered with one capitalised name, that pair is the alias; a romanized variant (`Priyaa` for `Priya`) becomes an alias (spelling variants meet: repeated letters collapse, a final h goes). Telugu and Hindi case endings are allowed after an alias (`ప్రియకి`, `प्रिया को`). (e) Chrome profile names and accounts from the desktop registry (`core.profiles()` of the node's real registry file only): a profile named like a person, and the first name in its account's address; the default profile or one matching the account is self, others are other; source profile, the lowest rank, never overrides anything he said, skipped when the registry did not change.
- **What is never a name.** Tenglish and Hindi verbs and function words in Latin letters (`rayi`, `chesi`, `teruvu`, `kholo` ...: `tenglish.LEXICON`, `translate._TE_STRONG/_HI_STRONG`), the relation words themselves (`amma` is a relation, not a name), pronouns, days, months, app names; plain English words of the layer's list count as a name only with a capital in an explicit relation sentence ("my son is Will") and mask only when capitalised. A verb written into the file by hand still never masks.
- **Never learned from data (prompt-injection rule).** The only caller is `translate.observe_owner`, called by the gateway's FACE path with the last user message of a face request (the face's placeholder key): not a tool result, a system prompt, an assistant turn, an earlier user turn, a page, a file, screen text or any other caller; the face's own notice turns are skipped; the plugin has **no entry that adds a name** (list, forget and answer only), so a model cannot add one either. `JARVIS_LEARNED_NAMES=0` turns learning and using off. The console command box, Telegram and the plugin entries (`run_pc_task`, `do_goal`) do not learn: the text of the entries can be a model's paraphrase. (Follow-up: the console box and Telegram are the owner's own words and could call `observe_owner`.)
- **Use.** (1) `translate.mask(text, glossary, names)` keeps a learned name as written, and a known alias (native script, romanized variant) goes back as the ONE canonical English spelling: `ప్రియకి ఫోల్డర్ ఓపెన్ చేయి` reaches the backend as `JVX0Q కి ...` and comes back `Priya ...` (the case ending is left to the model). Both directions (`Translator._convert`); English input still touches no backend. (2) A name beats a verb only where it is a name: a romanized word that is a lexicon verb is never a name. (3) The **hint**: `names.hint_for(request)` is `brother: Ravi; mother: Lakshmi`, relations and canonical names only, at most 20, and only when the request points at someone ("my brother", "call Amma", a known name or alias; an ordinary command adds nothing). It goes to the LOCAL decision model (`desktop/decide.py`, loopback) and to `decider.resolve` for folder and file phrases. **The online model never receives the names table**: the planner gets "mail my brother" with the relation word as the owner said it, the gateway forwards nothing from names.json (tested at the gateway) and keeps hiding names as before. `names.resolve("my brother")` returns every brother (two brothers: the caller asks which).
- **Honesty and UX.** Learning is silent: the face does not interrupt. "What names do you know" and "forget X" are rules in `quick_command` (no model sees the names; the reply is shown to the owner), and entries on the translate plugin (`list_names`, `forget_name`, `answer_name_question`; not on the chat tool list, which stays at 7,585 of 7,600 bytes, and the persona is untouched). The plugin's model-visible result fields carry a count and a sentence, never a name; the list is in `text`, which the owner sees. A low-confidence guess (`this is Meena`, a name learned by repeating) is asked ONCE as an `ask_form` (Yes / No, not a name; Family / Friend / A place / Not a name) with a plain sentence beside it; the entry is marked asked when the question is handed out, so it never comes back, answered or not; it is handed out by "what names do you know" and by `list_names`. `forget X` removes the entry (or the one holder of a relation) and tombstones every spelling: repeated use, a profile, a pairing never learn it again. His own later sentence ("Priya is my wife") lifts the tombstone, because it is newer than "forget": the one deliberate exception to "not re-learned".
- **Reuse (CLAUDE.md).** I know of no maintained project that learns a person's name table from conversation, with Telugu and Hindi aliases, for a translation mask on the owner's PC; the pieces are existing ones (the translate mask, `tenglish`, `ask_form`, the profile registry); the rules are small regular expressions.
- **Tests (fakes: `translate.FakeBackend`, the labelled fake detector and provider).** `plugins/_shared/tests/test_names.py` (English, Telugu, Hindi and Tenglish after the fake translation; stray sentences learn nothing; the count-3 rule and the resend; verbs are never names; injection by a tool result, a page, a model turn and the face's notice; alias pairing in both scripts and a romanized variant; tombstones; glossary precedence; the translator keeps a learned name intact; the hint, its cap and the two-brothers case; list, forget and the ask-once question; profiles; the atomic write and the broken file; the caps; two stores on one file; the plugin entries), `privacy/test_gateway_names.py` (which requests teach a name, that the provider body carries no name table), `plugins/desktop/tests/test_names_hooks.py` (`quick_command`).
- **What the laptop must verify (nothing here ran on a real model or on Windows).** (1) Real pairs: say in the face "నా భార్య ప్రియ", "మా అమ్మ పేరు లక్ష్మి", "मेरी पत्नी प्रिया है", "naa bharya peru Priya" and read `D:\jarvis\data\names.json`: is the canonical spelling one the owner accepts, and did IndicTrans2 answer with a capitalised English name (the alias pair depends on it; if it transliterates in lower case or otherwise, no alias is recorded and the fallback is the bracket form "Priya (ప్రియ)")? Try ten real family names in both scripts and the case endings (ప్రియకి, ప్రియతో, లక్ష్మిగారు). (2) The detector calls `naa bharya Priyaa` English (no word hit): confirm the Latin-letter rules catch the owner's real Tenglish phrasing and extend `_ROM_REL`. (3) The file is at `D:\jarvis\data\names.json` (not C:), is rewritten when a name is mentioned, `names.json.lock` does not stay behind, and the gateway process and the plugin host do not lose each other's writes. (4) The face's user message may carry the `[YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM]` prefix (stripped) and nothing else of the face's: check that none of the face's own framing text contains "my name is", "call me" or "I am X" (it would be learned as the owner). (5) With the real Chrome profiles, `names.json` gets the profile names (source profile) and nothing generic ("Work", "Person 1"). (6) "What names do you know" in the face reaches `quick_command` first and the list shows on screen; the ask-once question is only handed out in that reply and in `list_names` (the face has no proactive trigger for it yet). (7) Say "open the folder of my brother" and "open Priya's folder" and read the local decision model's request in the llama-swap log: the hint line is there and only there. (8) The suite shows one failure, `desktop/tests/test_core.py::test_the_profile_list_is_read_from_the_file_the_node_writes`: a Windows path-separator assertion on Linux that this change does not touch.

## D63 (2026-10-26): every generated form works by voice as well as by keyboard (dictation, spoken commands, read aloud), with the same result and the same safety

- **Owner's words (2026-10-26):** "Jarvis takes everything from voice, and spawning UI which contains text fields and headings: I will enter [by typing] or I can tell through voice." So every form the ask panel draws (`jarvis_ask`, the cleaner, the save-as chooser, a reminder, a settings page) can be used by keyboard or by voice, and the answer is the same. The Timeline panel (D61) is a hand-written panel, not a form drawn from a spec: its two filter chips (Today, Failed only) are NOT covered by this change (a filter asked through `jarvis_ask` would be).
- **Decision.** The panel (D55) stays ONE fixed component drawing a declarative spec; voice is added to it, not a second UI. Three things, all in the face unless said: **dictation** (a small microphone on every text, number and date field and on an editable heading), **spoken commands** for the open form (a deterministic grammar first, the decision engine only when the grammar is unsure, a question when that is unsure too), and **read aloud**. The speech recognizer and the speech output are the ones the face already has (the voice host and the owner's Hearing provider; `SpeechClient`, the path that reads a text aloud). **No cloud ASR and no new model were added**; nothing starts until the owner presses a microphone, and everything stops when the form closes.
- **Flows.**

  | What the owner does | What happens | Key |
  |---|---|---|
  | presses the mic of a field (or Alt+M with the field focused) | that field listens: interim words show live in the field (it is read-only while it listens), the final text replaces them; it keeps listening phrase after phrase; it ends on Escape, the mic again, "stop", the form closing, or 20 s of silence | Alt+M |
