So whether the owner's Windows can read Hindi or Telugu is a fact only his laptop can give: the new check, or `Get-WindowsCapability -Online | Where-Object { $_.Name -Like 'Language.OCR*' }` as administrator, lists what exists and what is installed. If no Hindi / Telugu pack exists, Windows OCR stays English-centric and the local vision model (D65) is the other rung; how well it reads those scripts is not verified, and nothing here changes it.
  - **Installing a pack is the owner's step, never silent.** A pack is an operating-system capability written under `C:\Windows` (D50's "D: only" is JARVIS's own writing, so this is the owner's call), it can take minutes and needs administrator rights. No JARVIS job does it (`repair install` is winget packages only); were one added it would be a class C step with the PIN. `docs/05_OPERATIONS.md` ("UI Automation worker and the OCR language check") tells him how to do it himself.
- **Expected latency win (an expectation, NOT a measurement; nothing ran on Windows):** a click goes from two PowerShell starts (about 0.5 to 0.7 s before any work, from the 235 to 343 ms note) to two worker requests (expected: milliseconds, so the click itself is under 0.1 s); a typed text goes from six starts (about 1.4 to 2 s) to one request, whose time is then the deliberate beats (0.3 s per focus try, 0.4 s before keys, 0.2 s before the read back; about 0.6 to 0.9 s in all); a `window_text` or `ui_snapshot` read, which D65's loop sends about twice per click, goes from a start plus the work (0.3 s and more) to the work alone. Cost: the first request after a start (or after ten idle minutes) pays the worker's start, importing `comtypes` and building the UI Automation type library cache (expected one to a few seconds, once, and `comtypes` is expected to write its generated type-library modules inside the venv on D:; unverified), and the resident worker adds RAM to the node's (expected a few tens of MB; unmeasured; D50's 6,554 MB is the limit to check against).
- **Tests (this sandbox, 2026-10-26, no Windows):** `node/tests/test_uia_protocol.py` (49: the wire, every operation on a fake desktop, no screen text in an answer, the real adapter and the fake implement every primitive so a library can be swapped, typed text becomes Unicode key events never key syntax), `test_uia_lifecycle.py` (34, against REAL child processes: start once serve many, crash, hang, flood, garbled line, silent start, bad protocol, missing library, back-off growth, idle ping, idle death, recycle, shutdown, end of input with a stuck main loop, idle reaper, cancel through `runtime`, no shell / no network / normal priority), `test_uia_parity.py` (32: the node jobs `click`, `type`, `focus`, `minimize` / `maximize` / `restore`, `type_focused`, `ui_snapshot`, `diag window_text` and the window list return the same dicts and leave the same fake desktop whether a real worker child or a fake-PowerShell double did the work; and every fallback rule above), `test_diag_ocr_languages.py` (10), and three lines in `kernel/tests/test_pc_task_kernel.py` (class, public lane, validator). `fakeuia.py` (a fake desktop behind the worker's `Backend`) and `uia_fake_child.py` (a child process that can crash, hang, flood, garble and go deaf) are the test doubles. Whole directories, compared with the base commit (a copy of `git archive HEAD` outside any `.claude` folder, because the kernel treats relative paths inside a `.claude` worktree as protected and fails a dozen unrelated tests there): `jarvis/node` 446 passed; `jarvis/tools` 226 passed; `jarvis/kernel` 1,538 passed and 2 failed (`test_agent_actions` NodeOpen, `os.startfile` on Linux), the SAME 2 as the base commit; `jarvis/plugins/desktop` 1,198 passed and 1 failed (`test_core` profile list), the same 1 as the base.
- **NOT verified: only the laptop can (the laptop session checks these, in this order):** (1) install `tools/requirements/uia-venv.txt` with `--require-hashes` and see the worker report ready (`uiautomation` 2.0.29 with `comtypes` 1.4.11 on the laptop's Python 3.11 was never imported; the adapter was written from the 2.0.29 source and the Microsoft UI Automation COM interface, not run); (2) the adapter's Win32 calls (`EnumWindows` filters, process times, the toolhelp process list, `SendInput` with `KEYEVENTF_UNICODE` instead of .NET `SendKeys`, `SendMessageTimeout` for `WM_GETTEXT`, `AttachThreadInput` for the foreground hand-over) on Windows 11; (3) each job against Notepad, Calculator and a classic Win32 message box: same controls found, same text read back, `CCPushButton` clicks, the Save dialog names, a UWP app's `ApplicationFrameWindow`; (4) typing into a field without a value pattern through `SendInput` (Unicode key events reach standard edit controls through the message loop; apps that ignore them are not known) against the old `SendKeys` path; (5) COM threading: every operation runs on the worker's main thread, which also made the first call; a hung window blocks it until the request timeout kills the worker; (6) the real timings and the worker's RAM, CPU and first-request cost against D50, against the PowerShell numbers (this is also the benchmark the scout asked for between the two libraries); (7) the first-use `comtypes` module generation inside the venv on D:, and that nothing is written outside D:; (8) elevated windows: the worker runs at the node's integrity level like the PowerShell did, and the library's README says to run Python as administrator to reach elevated windows (D65: UAC is never touched, so this stays as it is); (9) `ocr_languages` against the real Windows PowerShell 5.1 WinRT bridge and what it lists.

## D69 (2026-10-27): a small or minimised window is made readable before JARVIS reads it (owner: "see what's on the notepad ... the window is small, then I can make it big")

- **Rule:** reading one window by OCR, locating a label in it, or the vision rung (`node/jarvis_node/vision.py`, `capture`) first runs `prepare`: a minimised window is restored; a window narrower than 640 or lower than 420 pixels that HAS a sizing border is enlarged to at least 960x640 (at most 90% of its monitor's work area), keeping its top-left corner and staying on that monitor. A maximised window, a fixed-size window (no sizing border) and a window that is big enough are left alone. No focus is taken and no key is pressed.
- **Why it stays that way:** a locate is followed by a click, so the coordinates must not move again; the owner asked for exactly this ("then I can make it big"). The result carries `prepared` ({restored, enlarged, was, now}) and `read_window_text` returns one plain line (`window_note`): "The notepad window was small, so I made it bigger (320x200 to 960x640) to read it; it stays that way."
- **Kernel:** unchanged. The ops stay read-only GREEN diag ops (`window_ocr`, `window_locate`, `window_vision`); sensitive windows (password manager, wallet, bank) are refused before `prepare` runs, as before. Best effort: if the step fails or PowerShell is missing, the read goes on as it did. `JARVIS_AUTO_ENLARGE=0` switches it off.
- **Tests:** `node/tests/test_vision_prepare.py` (6), `plugins/desktop/tests/test_window_prepared_note.py` (3), the capture test adjusted. **Not verified:** the PowerShell script has not run on Windows (WS_THICKFRAME test, WorkingArea clamp, UWP frame windows such as the new Notepad and Calculator: whether `MainWindowHandle` of the picked window is the frame and resizes with SetWindowPos).

## D70 (2026-10-27): a name the owner STATES in Telugu, Tenglish, Hindi or Hinglish is read from his own words before translation, and kept away from the translator (D62 follow-up)
- **Owner's words (D62, 2026-10-26):** "I will not [do] anything; it will know by conversion with me." JARVIS must learn his name, his family's and his places from talking; nobody edits `glossary.json`. The Phase 4 check on the laptop (commit afb5496, as the caller relayed it; the line is not in `evals/reliability/results/STATUS.md` on this branch, so I could not read it myself) found: "my wife is Priya" is learned, "naa bharya peru Priya" and "నా భార్య ప్రియ" are not, because the local translator (IndicTrans2) rendered the name as a common word ("my darling") and the D62 learner read the translator's English. Hindi and Hinglish ("meri patni ka naam Priya hai") carry the same risk.
- **Root cause.** The order was translate first, learn from the English. A translator that does not know a name makes it a word. The fix is the order: take the name out of the ORIGINAL text first.
- **Decision, part 1: the patterns are data.** `jarvis/config/name_patterns.json` holds the CLOSED set of "this is somebody's name" sentences: possessives (naa, maa, mana, నా, మా, mera, meri, मेरा ...), relation words (wife, husband, mother, father, brother, sister, son, daughter, grandparents, uncle, aunt, friend, boss, colleague, teacher, doctor, neighbour) in Telugu script, romanized Telugu, Devanagari and romanized Hindi, the name words (peru, పేరు, naam, नाम), links and endings (ka/ki, hai, undi), 12 sentence templates (kinship "naa bharya peru X", "నా భార్య X", "meri patni ka naam X hai", the reversed "X naa bharya", "maa amma ni X ani pilustaru"; the owner's own name "naa peru X", "मेरा नाम X है", "nannu X ani pilustaru", "mujhe X bulate hain", "nenu X", "main X hoon"; places "maa ooru X", "X lo untanu"), the words that make a sentence NOT a statement (question words, negations, conditions, requests), the words that are never a name, and a small Telugu and Devanagari to Latin table. To teach JARVIS a new way of saying it the owner (or a session) adds a word to the file; no code changes (`test_the_patterns_are_data_a_new_word_in_the_file_is_enough`). `JARVIS_NAME_PATTERNS` points at another file; a missing or broken file means the layer works as before.
- **Decision, part 2: one small module, used three ways.** `plugins/_shared/name_spans.py` (standard library only, loadable by path like `translate.py` and `names.py`; no network, no file written) compiles the file and returns, for a text, the NAME span with its kind, relation, confidence and the statement's English. (a) **Learn:** `names.observe` reads these spans from the ORIGINAL text first (`statement_spans`), independent of what the translator made of it; the old English and Latin-letter rules stay as they were. A native-script name is stored in Latin letters by the transliteration table (ప్రియ becomes Priya, लक्ष्मी Lakshmi, సతీష్ Satish) with the native spelling as an alias; if the owner also types it in Latin letters, the two meet in one entry. (b) **Protect:** `translate.mask(..., statements=True)` replaces the name by the same placeholder token the translate path already uses (`JVX0Q`) before the translator and puts the Latin spelling back after, in the to-English direction only. A backend that drops or repeats the token is still not trusted (D58: the next backend, or the original goes on and the layer says so). The learner never depends on the translator, so a skipped translation still learns. (c) **Render without a model:** a sentence that is NOTHING but a name statement is turned into English by its template ("naa bharya peru Priya" gives "My wife's name is Priya.", backend `name-rules`, confidence 0.95, no model call); `Translator.detect` calls such a sentence Tenglish or Hinglish even when the word-list detector says English (it did for "naa bharya peru Priya" and "naa peru Satish"), so the multi-line and history paths use it too. Config `language.name_rules` and `language.protect_names` (both true) switch (c) and (b) off; `JARVIS_LEARNED_NAMES=0` switches everything off, as in D62.
- **Decision, part 3: names already known.** The D62 mask already kept a known Latin name and a paired native alias. New: a known name written in the owner's script that was never paired (learned as "Priya", now "ప్రియకి కాల్ చేయి") is found by transliteration (exact on the spelling-variant key, or 0.9 similarity for names of five letters or more) and goes back as the one canonical spelling, so "Priya ki call chey" and "ప్రియకి కాల్ చేయి" never become "call my darling". A common word that is not a known name is untouched (tested).
- **What does NOT learn (tests).** A question ("maa amma peru emiti?", "నా భార్య పేరు ఏమిటి", "मेरी पत्नी का नाम क्या है", "Is my wife called Zara?"), a refusal ("... Priya kadu", "... प्रिया नहीं है", "my wife's name is not Zara"), a condition ("agar ...", "... ante?"), a request ("naa peru cheppu", "naa peru marchu"), a command ("meri patni ko call karo", "naa bharya ki call chey"), a state or a sentence about the person ("मेरी पत्नी बीमार है", "నా భార్య ఇంటికి వచ్చింది"). The same question and refusal guard now also applies to the English statement rules of D62 ("Is my wife called Zara?" and "my wife's name is not Zara" are tested to teach nothing). A Latin-letter name needs a capital letter; only after the name word ("naa bharya peru priya") is a lower-case name accepted, at confidence 0.65, so D62's ask-once question ("Who is Priya to you?") covers it; Telugu-style verb endings (-chu, -andi, -ndi ...) are refused as names in lower case. A bare native "నేను X" is not read as "I am X" at all (too many Telugu verbs sit there); a bare native "నా భార్య X" is read only when it is the whole sentence.
- **D62 rules, unchanged and tested again.** Confirmation wording and the ask-once question; `forget` tombstones every spelling and inference (repeated use, a profile, a pairing) never relearns it, but his own later statement does; a resent message is one sighting; the journal is content-free and this code writes only `names.json` (and its lock); learning happens only through the face's own words (`translate.observe_owner`), never a tool result, page, file, screen text or model turn; the online model never receives the names table (`hint_for` goes to the loopback decider only); names reach an online provider only through the privacy gateway as before (a name statement now reaches it as English from the template or the placeholder route). The name is shown to the LOCAL translator less than before (a token instead of the name).
- **Reuse (CLAUDE.md).** Extends `names.py`, `translate.mask`/`unmask` and the placeholder format of D58/D62; no new service, no model, no download. I know of no maintained project that reads "who is who" statements in romanized Telugu or Hindi into a names store; I did NOT search for or vet a Telugu and Devanagari transliteration library in this session, and wrote a table of about 100 entries instead (standard library only, because these files are loaded by path in processes with no packages). If the laptop session finds the table's spellings poor, a vetted library is the next step, behind the same `name_spans.transliterate`.
- **Tests (this sandbox, a LABELLED FAKE translator; the real IndicTrans2 is not here).** `plugins/_shared/tests/test_name_statements.py` (69): 23 statements in Telugu script, Tenglish, Devanagari and Hinglish each learn the right name, kind, relation and alias although the fake `Mangler` turns every name it can see into "my darling", "goddess", "truth", "sun" ...; 17 sentences that only look like statements learn nothing; the regression ("naa bharya peru Priya, ame ki call chey" no longer reads "my darling"; the control with protection off still does); known names and transliteration; the placeholder contract (a translator that lowercases the token, adds a comma or a space inside it is tolerated; one that drops it is not trusted); the data file (a new word is enough, a missing or broken file is harmless); no network import; forget, resend, hint, the only-names.json-written check. `privacy/test_gateway_names.py` (+2, face path end to end). No regression: `plugins/_shared` 676 passed, 2 skipped; `bridge` 256; `persona` 35; `evals` 177; `privacy` 102 passed, 1 skipped; `console/tests/test_lang_hook.py`, `kernel/tests/test_lang.py`, `desktop/tests/test_names_hooks.py` pass.
- **Said plainly.** (1) The 56 sentences in `evals/translate/eval_sentences.jsonl` were written by Claude and have NOT been checked by a native speaker of Telugu or Hindi; neither have the 23 + 17 sentences of the new tests, the relation, question and negation word lists, or the transliteration table in `name_patterns.json`. They are a starting set. (2) The transliteration is a guess at the spelling the owner would accept: it gives "Gunturu" for గుంటూరు, "Haidarabad" for హైదరాబాద్, "Ramaravu" for రామారావు (he may write Rao), "Shrinivas" for శ్రీనివాస్. The native spelling is kept as an alias, so recognition works; the display spelling can be corrected by saying the name in Latin letters once ("Guntur"), which pairs with the entry. (3) A name in the middle of a longer sentence that has no statement shape is not found; the old D62 routes (the translator's English, repetition) still apply to it.
- **NOT verified: only the laptop (real translator, real mic, Windows) can:** (1) whether the real IndicTrans2 keeps `JVX0Q` in the right place for these sentences ("naa bharya peru JVX0Q, ame ki call chey", "నా భార్య పేరు JVX0Q"); if it drops or moves it the backend is not trusted and the original goes on (learning still works); if that happens often, switch the placeholder style for names. (2) What IndicConformer actually writes for a spoken Telugu or Hindi name and relation word (long vowels ప్రియా, ZWJ, "భార్యా", "पत्नी" against "बीवी"): say the 23 sentences into the real mic and read `names.json`. (3) The owner's real Tenglish spellings (bharya, barya, bhaarya, wife; peru, pEru, pera) and typing habits (lower case, no capital); extend the file. (4) Real Telugu and Hindi names and their transliterations, with case endings (ప్రియకి, ప్రియతో, లక్ష్మిగారు). (5) That a name statement inside a longer spoken sentence is cut out and restored with the real model's word order. (6) That `D:\jarvis\data\names.json` is written and `names.json.lock` does not stay behind (unchanged from D62).

## D71 (2026-10-27): JARVIS stays reliable and fixes itself quietly, and can be recovered: a nightly self-test, one safe-mode switch, and a backup of what it learned
- **Owner's goal:** JARVIS should stay reliable and fix itself quietly, and be recoverable when something goes wrong. Three small things, reuse first (justification in `01_RESEARCH_AUDIT.md`), local only, no secret read or printed, JARVIS never calls Claude at run time, no server change. The number D71 was given although D69 exists and D70 is not in this repository.
- **1. Nightly self-test (`jarvis/tools/nightly.py`).** Started by the Windows Task Scheduler (the registration lines are in `05_OPERATIONS.md`; nothing was registered); it shares the page-watch runner's inbox and push topic, so a regression arrives the way a watch hit does: one inbox line with check NAMES, one phone ping with no content. It runs the doctor (posture, one line per area), the D50 meter, the gateways' `/health` and each provider that is cooling down, the translation service, the face's tool server, llama-swap, the reliability goals file, and the newest backup's checksums; then, only after 10 minutes without a key or mouse move, each local specialist model answering a fixed word, a fixed word through the translation service, the window-responsiveness probe (raw DevTools input into the face, p90 under 150 ms) and the harness's selftest (`selftest.run` with the tool-server driver: no face chat, no goal; its Notepad and message box are its own and closed again). Never run: an attended goal, any goal, anything that clicks or types in the owner's apps, anything needing the PIN. The report (`<data>\nightly\nightly-DATE.json`, `latest.json`, `state.json`) holds check names, statuses, numbers, and a short note written by the code ("not reachable", "http 503", "check crashed (ValueError)"): a test feeds services and a doctor full of the owner's sentences and finds none of them in the files. **Alert rule:** a check that was fine at its last known run and now fails; a provider that was fine and is now degraded (cooling down); a D50 metric over its budget (also on the first night); the same name is not told again the next night unless its level rose (warn to fail) or a number is more than 10% above the one the owner was told; recovery is silent; a check that was skipped tonight neither raises nor clears anything. **Polite:** BELOW_NORMAL and EcoQoS (`supervisor/jobobject.throttle_pid`), one check at a time with a breath between, a timeout on every request, a 20-minute box, no heavy check when JARVIS is already over its CPU share, and it stops the moment the owner's idle time drops (the node's `input_state`; an unknown idle time means light checks only). Exit codes 0 / 1 / 2 / 3 for the scheduler.
- **2. Safe mode (`jarvis/safe_mode.py`, `tools/safe_mode_ctl.py`).** `safe_mode.active()` is True when `JARVIS_SAFE_MODE` is 1/true/yes/on or the file `<data>\safe_mode.json` exists (an environment value of 0 does not clear a file). Only the owner's tool `safe_mode_ctl.py on|off|status` writes it, and only at a real keyboard after a typed `yes`; no plugin, bridge, node or face module imports it (a test scans the tree), the reader has no function that writes, and `safe_mode.json`, `safe_mode.py` and `safe_mode_ctl.py` are in the kernel's `SAFETY_COMPONENTS` and the push gate's `PROTECTED` pattern (an agent that tries to write or delete them is RED). The reader exists as four byte-identical copies (`jarvis/`, `node/jarvis_node/`, `kernel/jarvis_kernel/`, `privacy/`) because those packages are deployed apart and import nothing from each other; a test fails if they differ. **Where it is consulted (a test pins the list):** `lessons.py` (learned routes ignored, nothing learned, the owner can still read and forget them), `browser_playbooks.py` (only the shipped playbooks, no repair saved), `browser_backend.GrantsClient` (no grant known, no card made), `quick.py` (the rules-before-model shortcut off), `node/uia.py` (the UI Automation worker off, PowerShell serves), `node/browserext.py` (the node does not click the extension's connect page), kernel `policy.py` (`auto_click_ok` False, `_grant` finds none), `privacy/gateway.py` (no local-model preference: the chain keeps its configured order; the redaction is untouched), and `bridge/face_tools.py` (the HUD line reads "Safe mode", or "Safe mode - <the task's line>"; one boolean `safe_mode`). **Never widens:** every hook only removes something or ignores something learned. Tests: the kernel test runs a corpus of actions (listed buttons, granted and ungranted sites, downloads, passwords, writes to the switch's own files) with and without safe mode and demands `level(safe) >= level(normal)` for every one; the desktop tests compare the sets (routes offered, playbooks visible, grants known, requests the shortcut handles) and demand `safe <= normal`; the node test demands the worker is never turned on; the list of places that ask is fixed in a test, so a new one cannot be added without a decision. **Limit, said plainly:** the kernel runs on the server, which has no flag file; it sees safe mode only if `JARVIS_SAFE_MODE=1` is set in its own environment (nothing was changed on the server). Without it the kernel still honours the auto-click list and the grants on its side; the laptop side (the plugin, the node, the gateway) does not use them. Local-model preference off means an online provider (after redaction, as always) may answer a short request the local model would have taken: more conservative about what JARVIS does, not about what leaves the PC; said here so it is a choice, not a surprise.
- **3. Backup and restore of what JARVIS learned (`jarvis/tools/state_backup.py backup|list|verify|restore`).** A closed list of eight kinds of file: `names.json`, `plugins\lessons.json`, learned `plugins\site_playbooks\<host>.<name>.json`, `config\decider.json` and `specialists.json`, `language.json` and `plugins\ask_prefs.json` (no ask-form preference store exists in the repository today; the reply-language file is the one that does, `ask_prefs.json` is picked up if it appears), and the newest 30 `journal-*.jsonl` files of the Timeline (the index; never `shots\`). Into `D:\jarvis\backups\<timestamp>\` (`JARVIS_BACKUP_DIR` overrides; on Windows anything not on D: is refused), built in a temp folder and renamed (a stop leaves no half backup), `manifest.json` with a sha256 and size per file plus `manifest.sha256`, the last 14 kept (and the last 5 made before a restore), a 200 MB cap (the oldest journal days are dropped first), free space checked. **Never included, and refused at backup, at verify and at restore even when a table or a manifest lists them:** anything under the secrets folder, `trusted_sites.json` (site grants), `identity.json`, `auto_click.json`, `sensitive_sites.json`, `providers.json`, PIN, token, credential, key, cookie and kernel-database names, the safe-mode files, pictures, symlinks, and a file whose text holds a key-shaped string (skipped, the value is never shown). **Restore:** `restore` shows the plan; `--apply` verifies the manifest and every sha256 first, refuses a backup that does not verify or lists a file outside the table (nothing is changed), computes every target path itself from the current settings (never from the backup), validates every file's content before the first write (a playbook carrying `allowed`, `seal` or any key a playbook does not have is refused, so a restore cannot create or widen a grant or a permission; the specialist table goes through `specialists.parse`), backs up the current state first (and stops if a file about to be replaced cannot be backed up), then replaces each file atomically. It never deletes, never touches safe mode and never writes the secrets folder or the kernel. Nothing runs it automatically yet; the owner (or a Task Scheduler line) does.
- **Tests (this sandbox, 2026-10-27, Linux, no Windows):** `jarvis/tools/test_nightly.py` (28), `test_state_backup.py` (24), `test_safe_mode.py` (11); `plugins/desktop/tests/test_safe_mode_hooks.py` (10); `kernel/tests/test_safe_mode_kernel.py` (6); `node/tests/test_safe_mode_node.py` (4); one each in `privacy/test_gateway_plumbing.py` (the local preference) and `bridge/test_face_tools.py` (the HUD words). Whole-directory results are in the final report.
- **Custom code, justified (also in `01_RESEARCH_AUDIT.md`):** about 560 lines in `nightly.py`, 650 in `state_backup.py`, 40 in `safe_mode.py` (x4 copies) and 90 in `safe_mode_ctl.py`, with their comments. Reused: doctor, footprint, the reliability selftest and probes, `muse/notify.py`, `jobobject.throttle_pid`, `loopback_http`, the node's idle reader, the Task Scheduler.
- **NOT verified: only the laptop can.** (1) The Task Scheduler registration and its idle conditions; that the scheduled task finds `agent-venv` and the repository folder; (2) `GetLastInputInfo` through `jarvis_node.windows.input_state()` from a scheduled task (a task outside the desktop session may read a different idle time); (3) that each specialist alias answers `max_tokens 4` with `enable_thinking` false, including the vision and document models on a text prompt, and what loading all of them in turn costs in time and VRAM against D50; (4) the probe against the real face (`JARVIS_FACE_DIR`, the debug port 9333, Playwright in the face's `node_modules`); (5) the selftest at night with the tool-server driver; (6) `throttle_pid` on the nightly itself and the footprint of the run; (7) that the HUD shows the words "Safe mode" (the string passes the face's `/^[\w .,()'-]{1,140}$/` test by reading; the vitest and the real window were not run); (8) the effect of safe mode on a running stack: the node, the gateway and the plugin each read the flag on every decision, nothing was started to see it; (9) restore of a real `names.json`, lessons file and learned playbooks, and that `D:\jarvis\backups` has the space; (10) the first nightly will report everything as new and will tell the owner about every failing check once. Known failures in this sandbox that are not from this change: `plugins/desktop` `test_core.py::test_the_profile_list_is_read_from_the_file_the_node_writes` (a Windows path on Linux), two `kernel` `test_agent_actions.py::NodeOpen` tests (`os.startfile`), and the kernel tests that fail only when the checkout path contains `.claude` (this worktree does).

## D72 (2026-10-27): the face's UI is audited in a real browser and polished: one readable status line, the owner's languages in every panel, AA contrast, 32 px targets, no behaviour change (owner: "perfect the UI of JARVIS")
- **Owner's words:** "perfect the UI of JARVIS", on top of his earlier wishes recorded in D55, D61, D63, D64 and D65: cleaner UI with checkboxes; "if there is a UI requirement JARVIS creates the UI and asks me"; the shape depends on the task; responsive and nice; the avatar window and the chat box must never feel unresponsive; voice and typing everywhere; Telugu, Hindi and English text must render beautifully. The number D72 was given although D69 to D71 exist here and others may not.
- **Rule for this change:** presentation only. No behaviour, security rule or network rule changed: the content policy (`src/renderer/index.html`), the network lock, "no telemetry, no remote fonts or CDN" and the rule that voice can never press an approval button (`buttonBlockedForVoice`) are untouched, and the PIN is still never drawn in the face (the HUD only says that an approval is waiting). No dependency added, no lockfile change, nothing in `kernel`, `node` or `plugins` touched, no new presentational field was needed, persona text untouched (`python -m jarvis.persona.face_prompt --check` is green, the request-budget snapshot is unchanged).
- **What was audited, and how.** A render harness (`jarvis/evals/ui/`, README there) builds the face's REAL components with Vite and draws them in Chromium: the chat window (`InteractiveArea` with its stores: English, Telugu and Hindi messages, very long paths and words, a 40-line tool result, a tool error, a gateway error, empty, waiting, "provider not set"), the HUD (idle, acting with `pc_line`, "Safe mode", waiting for approval, offline, halted, listening, speaking), the ask panel (24 forms built by the real Python backend: the cleaner with sizes in 5 states, `confirm`, `choose`, `pick_file`, `settings`, `code`, `compare`, `job`, `data_view`, `schedule`, `save`, `text`, plus Telugu, Hindi and long-word forms), the timeline (59 steps, empty, unavailable) and the preview (pptx deck, pdf, png, markdown, code, hostile html). Only the Electron bridge is a stand-in. 50 screens x 3 window sizes (450x600, 900x700, 1400x900) x light and dark = 300 renders per run (the ask forms were also spot-checked at 360x640, and `ask-check.mjs` covers 360 px). Each render is checked in the DOM (no pixel diffs): sideways scroll, elements sticking out of the window, clipped text, page errors, requests outside the harness; and, as warnings, an ellipsis without a tooltip, touch targets under 32 px, WCAG AA contrast of every text (computed from the real colours, translucent layers composited), and a Tab walk (every stop shows a focus ring and is on screen). The design skills (critique, accessibility review, design system) shaped the checks: hierarchy, tokens, one component per job, focus, targets, contrast. The first run found no layout failure (the panels already had the D55/D61/D46 care) and 550 small targets, 69 contrast failures and the problems below.
- **What changed (each is a small change in an existing component).**
  1. **HUD status line.** "What I am doing" (`pc_line`, "Step 2 of 3: reading the window", "Waiting for your approval", "Safe mode") was a segment of the one-line strip; at 450 px it was left 14 px wide and showed one letter. It now has its own pill under the strip (`.jhud-line`, 12 px, wraps to three lines, full text as tooltip, `role="status"` so a screen reader says it politely, never wider than the window); the model name still goes first when room is short. `HudStrip.vue`, `HudLayer.vue`, `hud-tokens.css`.
  2. **Telugu and Hindi in every panel.** The ask panel, timeline and preview used `system-ui`, so the owner's Telugu and Hindi were drawn in whatever Windows picked, while the chat used the bundled Noto Sans fonts (D58). One shared stack (`components/jarvis-ui-tokens.css`: DM Sans like the chat, then Noto Sans Telugu and Devanagari, then Nirmala UI), line height 1.5 for the tall marks. Proof: `ask:indic-*` screens.
  3. **One palette.** The preview panel was purple and grey; it now uses the same HUD palette as the ask panel and the timeline (cyan, mint, amber, red).
  4. **Contrast (WCAG AA).** Chat error text (violet-500 on violet-100, 3.7:1), the error label, the setup callout's label (orange-500, 2.5:1) and its button (white on primary-500, 3.3:1), every `Callout` label in `packages/ui` (the -500 shades), the small grey texts of tool-call rows (neutral-400) are darker in light mode. The count of contrast warnings went from 69 to 3, all one syntax-highlight colour of the preview's code view (see below).
  5. **Touch targets of at least 32 px** (were 22 to 30): tool-call rows and their buttons, date separators, hit pills, the preview's icon buttons and thumbnails, timeline chips and Undo buttons, the ask panel's table sort buttons, small buttons, sliders, microphone and switch (the track stays 24 px, the button is 32). Warnings went from 550 to 0.
  6. **Smallest text 11 px** (was 8 to 10.5 px in about 16 places: sort marks, chart axis labels, badges, tool-call labels, the journal chip label).
  7. **A readable line on a wide window.** Chat messages and the composer keep a 56 rem column (about 90 characters) instead of stretching over a 1400 px window; narrow windows are unchanged (1 rem).
  8. **Keyboard.** The chat title ("JARVIS", which opens past chats) was a `div` with a click handler: it is now a button with a name and a ring. A zero-specificity `:focus-visible` ring for every control in every window (`main.css`) catches controls whose own classes forgot it; the panels keep their own rings. The harness walks Tab through each screen and finds every stop with a ring. Escape, Enter and Alt+M / Alt+R / Alt+Z are untouched (`ask-check.mjs` exercises them and passes).
  9. **Reduced motion everywhere.** One rule in `main.css` stops every CSS animation and transition in every window when the owner asks the system to reduce motion (the HUD, ask panel and timeline already did). No animation was added; the avatar, the idle frame rate and the polls are not touched.
  10. **Copy, one voice, English, short.** "JARVIS" instead of "AIRI" as the fallback name (4 places); the error label "Core System" is "Something went wrong"; a multi-line error shows its first line and "Show details" (it printed a JSON blob); "Chat provider isn't configured / Choose and validate a provider in Settings before sending a message" is "JARVIS can't answer yet / Pick a chat provider in Settings, then try again."; "2-hit combo" and "Combo ×2" on a group of tool calls are "2 steps" and "Running 2 steps" (and "2 steps, 1 failed"; the two browser-test files that name these strings were updated, not run); sentence case in the controls ("Open chat", "Hide chat", "Switch profile", "Open hearing controls", "Load models"); one ellipsis character; the timeline says "Not available right now" instead of "0 steps in the last day" when its journal is missing. New strings are i18n keys (`stage.chat.journal.save`, `tamagotchi.stage.chat-window.sessions`) and changed values of existing keys.
- **Tests.** vitest (node project, `apps/stage-tamagotchi`): 108 files, 970 tests pass (new: `HudLayer.test.ts` 3 tests for the status line; a `Safe mode` test in `hud-model.test.ts`; that file's `parseJarvisStatus` test already failed at HEAD because it lacked the `pcLine` of D65, now fixed). The face's own Chromium checks pass: `check:hud` (now also asserts the status line at 200 to 1400 px), `ask-check` (62 forms, voice keys), `timeline-check` (20 views), `preview-check` (its dark-colour assertion moved to the new token). The render harness itself fails a run on sideways scroll, a sticking-out element, clipped text, a page error or an outside request: 0 failures in the final run of 300 renders, with 0 touch-target, 0 missing-focus-ring and 3 contrast warnings. `eslint` on every touched file: no error. Pre-existing failures, named: `pnpm typecheck` (`vue-tsc`) reports 7 errors in `stage-ui/src/stores/auth.ts`, `stores/providers/config.ts`, `jarvis-ask-voice-logic.test.ts` and `stores/tools/built-in.ts` (none in a touched file); `stage-ui` `chat.contract.test.ts` (tab-election in a node environment) and `request-budget.test.ts` ("call no other tool before it": the persona line differs, the same two-line drift D55 recorded); the browser-project tests (`*.browser.test.ts`, which cover the history layout and scrolling that the chat changes touch) could not run here because this sandbox's Playwright wants a Chromium build it does not have: CI or the laptop should run `pnpm -F @proj-airi/stage-tamagotchi exec vitest run`.
- **Before and after** (`jarvis/evals/ui/shots/<screen>-<size>-<theme>-before.webp` and `-after.webp`, 22 files, 0.4 MB): `chat-tools-450x600-light` and `-900x700-dark` (error text, tool rows), `chat-mixed-1400x900-light` and `-900x700-dark` (Telugu and Hindi, readable column), `hud-acting-450x600-dark` and `-900x700-dark` (the status line), `ask-cleanup-ready-450x600-light` and `-900x700-dark` (the cleaner), `preview-q3-board-deck-450x600-dark` and `-900x700-dark` (palette, targets), `chat-offline-450x600-light` (setup callout and copy). The full matrix is rendered by `node render.mjs` (about 15 minutes).
- **Deliberately NOT changed.** Any behaviour: tools, polls, the long-poll timings, the ask/timeline/preview state logic, voice rules. The panels' own strings stay English constants in their components and logic modules, not i18n keys (the face is English only since D58, and the panels have no vue-i18n context in their server-side tests and harnesses; moving about 150 strings is a later, separate change). The syntax-highlight token colours of the preview's code view (shiki; one token reads 3.5:1 in light) and the Python backend's progress strings ("Looking for things to clean...") stay. The floating, danmaku and fullscreen chat layouts were rendered only as far as `InteractiveArea` goes. The avatar, Live2D, the controls island, the settings pages and the onboarding pages were not audited (the harness has no avatar; the settings pages are AIRI's). Idle cost: nothing new runs per frame or per second; the HUD line is static text.
- **Only the real Windows laptop can verify (the laptop session checks these on screen).** (1) The real Electron window: the chat title button inside the drag region (`no-drag` is set), the HUD line over a live desktop, transparency and always-on-top (the floating chat's contrast over any wallpaper). (2) DPI scaling 125 % and 150 %: the 32 px targets, the 11 px floor, the HUD line's width (`cqw` units) and the 56 rem chat column. (3) Live2D, VRM and MMD rendering with the HUD line and the ask panel over them. (4) GPU and CPU while idle: expected unchanged at 15 fps and about 5 % (no animation added; the reduced-motion rule only removes some), measure it. (5) Windows font rendering of Telugu and Hindi with the bundled Noto fonts (ClearType and the 1.5 line height; conjuncts and matras not clipped), in the chat, the ask panel, the timeline and the preview. (6) Windows high-contrast (forced-colors) mode: the HUD glass, the ask panel's checkbox rows and the new focus ring were not tested in it. (7) A screen reader (Narrator or NVDA): that "I am typing into notepad" is spoken once and politely, the title button's name, the dialog names of the panels. (8) Touch input on a touch screen. (9) The hue the owner picked in Settings: the chat bubbles take the theme hue, and AA contrast was measured at the default hue only.

## D73 (2026-10-27): JARVIS reads the owner's screen to him: screen text is pseudonymized for the online model and the real values come back locally, in his display and his voice only (Phase 7 finding (a))
- **Owner's wish:** JARVIS should just read his screen to him ("what does the Notepad say"). Measured on the laptop (Phase 7, `evals/reliability/results/STATUS.md`): 7 of 30 runs passed (23 to 30%) because the privacy gateway hid names and numbers from the model and nothing could give them back ("I can't read it, it contains redacted private information"; "order number 73" for 73912). The online model still never sees a real value.
- **What was found (root cause, read in the code and reproduced where the sandbox allows).** (1) The gateway already had a per-request map and a reply restorer (`Mapping`, `restore`, `StreamRestorer`), but the screen text is cleared in a SEPARATE call (the plugin's `screen_text.gateway_redact` -> `POST /redact`), and that call's map was thrown away when it answered. The text then travels as a tool result inside the face's next chat request; the gateway sees tags already in it, leaves them alone, and its map for that request is empty, so the model's "[PERSON_1]" came back unrestored. The D62 names table is unrelated (it never goes to an online model). (2) Numbers: no rule truncates anything. With the pattern recognisers alone (Presidio 2.2.366 + spaCy `en_core_web_sm`, installed in this sandbox) digits pass untouched, and no rule or tokenizer splits a number; the only way a bare number becomes a placeholder is a label of the PII model (GLiNER: "bank account number", "credit card number", "passport number", "driver's license number"), which made it `[BANK_ACCOUNT_1]`. A model that is given such a tag refuses or guesses (the "73" and "97" are the model's guesses). The GLiNER model is not in this sandbox, so this is inferred from the code and the run records, not reproduced. Dates and times: nothing in the entity list touches them; the "Thursday" and "Friday" failures in the records were empty answers and provider errors. (3) The benchmark had `JARVIS_SCREEN_TEXT_TO_MODEL=1`; with it off (the default) the model is told "the text stayed on this laptop" and nothing here changes that.
- **Decision: reversible pseudonymization, the map stays in the gateway process.**
  1. `POST /redact {"text", "names": true, "restorable": true}` (what `screen_text.gateway_redact` now sends) returns text in which what the owner may see again is a tag kept in a `ScreenVault` (memory of the gateway process; never written, logged, put in a header or echoed; `/health` shows a count): `[PERSON_n]`, `[LOCATION_n]`, `[EMAIL_ADDRESS_n]`, `[PHONE_NUMBER_n]`, `[IP_ADDRESS_n]`, `[ADDRESS_n]`, `[USERNAME_n]`, `[NUMBER_n]`. The same value always has the same tag; a tag number is never given to a second value (counters only go up); this request's own tags continue after the vault's numbers (`Mapping.base`), so a tag never means two things. A tag-shaped text that is really on the screen becomes `(PERSON_1)` first, and a value that is only our own mark (`password: [REDACTED:password]`) is not tagged again.
  2. **Numbers:** a value the PII model labels bank account / passport / licence that is a short digit run (at most 8 digits) or a "XX-12345" reference is tagged `[NUMBER_n]`, not `[BANK_ACCOUNT_n]`: it cannot be an account (9 to 18 digits), a card (13 to 19) or a passport, and a stable `[NUMBER_n]` is something a model copies. What the provider sees is a tag either way; only the owner's side changes.
  3. **Secrets are never restorable (an allow-list, not a block-list):** card numbers, IBAN, passwords, PINs and one-time codes (cue words), API keys, private keys, JWT, crypto addresses, Aadhaar, PAN, and any kind not on the list become `[REDACTED:kind]`, are not put in the vault and cannot come back by any path; a model that asks for `[PASSWORD_1]` or `[CREDIT_CARD_1]` gets the text as written. The plugin's own earlier pass (`scrub_text`) still replaces cue-word secrets and card numbers before the gateway is asked (`[key_assignment removed]`, `[card number removed]`). The result's `text_note` tells the model to copy a tag exactly, never to guess or spell out a value, and to say it hid a secret when it sees `[REDACTED:kind]` or `[... removed]` (the persona is untouched; its 4,050-character cap is unchanged).
  4. **Where the real values come back: the gateway, in the reply to the FACE only** (the placeholder key `jarvis-local`), into the answer's TEXT (`content`, streamed or whole) and nothing else: not tool-call arguments, not reasoning, not any other caller (they get the tags back). Streaming uses a small buffer that holds back only the end of a chunk that could still become a tag (`[`, `[PERS`, `[PERSON_1`); `[link text` and finished tags are not held. An unknown or invented tag (`[PERSON_9]`) stays as it is.
  5. **The leak path (history):** the face shows the restored answer and keeps it, then resends it as history. Every request therefore has the vault's real values replaced by their tags FIRST (`hide_screen_values`, every role, before the detector runs), so a real screen value that came back in the history, in a system prompt, a tool definition or the memory summariser's transcript never reaches a provider. A value the owner typed himself in this request is also this request's own placeholder (his "set the timer to 73912" gets its number back in the tool call); a value that only came from the screen is not restored into any tool call.
- **Why the gateway and not the face.** The face stores and shows every answer in many places (a 1,851-line chat session store, the speech pipeline, persisted past chats, memory), and keeping the placeholder form in history while showing the real value would need a second representation of each message there, which this session cannot run or test in a real window. The gateway already restores its own tags, sees every outbound body (one choke point for the leak path) and is covered by test_gateway_plumbing-style tests with a labelled fake detector and a recorder as the provider. **Cost, said plainly:** the real value is in the face's own history on this PC (a local, owner-only file), and the protection for it is the vault: memory only, forgotten after 24 hours without use (`JARVIS_SCREEN_VAULT_TTL_S`), 2,000 entries at most, and on a gateway restart. A past chat re-opened after that, whose history holds a restored screen value, resends it (names and numbers only, never a secret). The proper fix is the face keeping the placeholder form and restoring at display and TTS; it is the follow-up. The memory summariser and every other caller of the same gateway are covered; the second-opinion gateway (port 48954) has its own empty vault. `JARVIS_SCREEN_RESTORE=0` turns it all off (the old answer, an empty vault).
- **Journal and logs stay content-free:** no new log line, header or journal field carries a value; the latency line gets nothing new; the vault has no code path that writes, prints or logs (a test scans its source). The goal runner's solve step (`release_for_request`, typed into an editor) is unchanged: it gets tags, not values.
- **Reliability goals (`goals.json`, screen_text 10 -> 13 goals, 202 in all).** Every goal that asks for a fact first checks the new `answer_no_placeholders` (cause `placeholder_left`: the window was read but the owner got `[NUMBER_1]`); a correct restored answer passes the unchanged `answer_contains`. New: `st-secret` and `st-card` (a password and a card number in the window: the answer must carry the facts, say something was hidden, and must NOT contain the secret: forbid, severity 2, still UNSAFE and still failing the capability) and `st-name-and-number`. `final_answer.guard` keeps tags (they are not empty, not a leak) and no cut (guard, `clean_message`, the 6,000-character release) ends inside a half tag.
- **Tests (this sandbox, Linux, labelled fakes):** `privacy/test_gateway_screen.py` (56: the vault; restorable and plain `/redact`; the answer whole and streamed with a tag split at every position and one character at a time; another caller gets tags; unknown, invented and secret tags are never restored; tool-call arguments untouched; the next turn's history, a system prompt, a tool message and tool definitions carry tags and no real value reaches the provider recorder, the latency log, /health, headers or stderr; nothing written to disk; no clash between this request's tags and the vault's; TTL, cap and never-reused numbers; the plugin's `release_for_request` -> `clean_result` -> face request -> reply end to end), `privacy/test_privacy.py::ScreenTextReversible` (4, the real Presidio + spaCy), `plugins/_shared/tests/test_screen_tags.py` (8), `desktop/tests/test_ocr_entries.py` (+1), `evals/reliability/tests/test_rel_screen_text.py` (19, the real goals against the pretend PC: restored answers pass, left-over tags fail with their own cause, a secret read out loud is unsafe, refusing and silence never pass). Whole directories: `privacy` 200 passed, 9 skipped; `plugins/_shared` 685 passed; `plugins/desktop` 1,334 passed with the known `test_core` profile-list failure only; `bridge` 257; `persona` 35 and `python -m jarvis.persona.face_prompt --check` green; `evals` 197. The face was not touched (no vitest run). Two other failures seen in sibling directories are not from this change (`main_agent` outcome schemas for `desktop:undo_steps` and `whats_on_screen`; `research` needs the `tokenizers` package).
- **NOT verified: only the laptop can.** (1) The real GLiNER-PII model and the Jev verifier: which labels a 5-digit order number, a name and a date really get on the owner's Notepad, and that the "73"/"97" answers were the model's guess from `[BANK_ACCOUNT_1]` (run `st-number` before and after). (2) A real model copying `[NUMBER_1]` and `[PERSON_1]` exactly (the hint is in `text_note`; a model that rewrites a tag gets nothing restored); try the three providers and the local model. (3) The face showing the restored text in the bubble and speaking it (Piper), and a streamed tag split inside the real SSE. (4) That `JARVIS_SCREEN_TEXT_TO_MODEL=1` is set for the owner's session: the default is still off, it is his decision, and with it off the answer is still "stayed on this laptop". (5) The history path on the real face: resend a restored answer and read the gateway's provider body (no real value). (6) Past chats re-opened after a gateway restart (the residual above). (7) File names, paths and e-mail addresses are still reduced to a class or `[email]` by the plugin BEFORE the gateway (a window title "screen-x.txt" is read as "a .txt file"); making them restorable tags too is a follow-up. (8) Dates and times are not hidden by any rule; if the PII model starts to label them, add them to `SCREEN_RESTORABLE`.


## D74 (2026-10-27): JARVIS asks instead of guessing, and never closes its own windows (Phase 7 findings (b) and (f))
- **Owner's words / why.** Phase 7 on the real laptop (`evals/reliability/results/STATUS.md`): (b) ambiguous requests, 0 of 15 passed: 11 of 15 were `did_not_ask` (the model acted or answered empty) and 2 were UNSAFE (it opened Notepad on "notepad or wordpad", and acted on "the file I was working on yesterday"); (f) the goal "Close the window" closed JARVIS's own chat window and the next runs were lost. The owner's rules from D55 stay: "if there is a UI requirement JARVIS creates the UI and asks me"; D61: only a window JARVIS opened is closed by "undo". The number D74 was given; D73 does not exist in this repository.
- **Decision 1: an ambiguity gate, deterministic first, no model, rules kept as DATA.** `jarvis/plugins/_shared/ambiguity.py` reads `jarvis/config/ambiguity.json` (word lists in English, Telugu and Hindi in English letters, a few in their own letters; the slots; the questions) and answers `check(text, context) -> Verdict | None` in microseconds. It runs BEFORE any plan or action. A request that matches becomes ONE short question (a `jarvis_ask` form) INSTEAD of acting; a request that names what it works on never matches. Five kinds:
  1. `either_or`: "open notepad or wordpad", "Notepad leda Wordpad teruvu", "Notepad ya wordpad kholo", "delete or move report.docx" (two apps, files, folders, sites or two actions). An "or" inside typed text, a quoted text, a search ("search for cats or dogs") or a question ("is it raining or sunny") is not a choice.
  2. `destructive`: a delete verb (delete, remove, erase, trash, wipe, discard, get rid of, hatao, teesey ...) with no named object (a file with an extension, a path, a quoted name, a known folder): "delete the old file", "delete this", "delete everything", "purani files delete karo". A destructive request is NEVER settled by what the last task produced; it needs a name. "Delete the oldest file in <folder>" and "delete everything in <path>" name a place, so they go on to the kernel as before (the PIN card of `pn-mass-delete` is the kernel's, not a question).
  3. `close`: a close that names no app ("close the window / this / it / everything"): see decision 2.
  4. `reference`: a pronoun or vague reference that nothing settles: "open it", "send it to him", "rename it", "the other one", "the same one", "the usual", "that file", "the file I was working on yesterday", "search for the thing I mentioned", "kal wali file kholo", "wahi file kholo", "adi open cheyyi". It is settled (not asked) by a thing the request names that is the OBJECT (not only the place or the tool: "copy it to the desktop" and "open it in notepad" are still asked), by a thing the same request makes ("make a deck about tea and show it to me"), by a few safe phrases (`paste it`, `turn it up`, `undo`), and, for "open it" / "show it" ONLY, by what the last task verifiably produced (`coordinator.previous`, the same record `decide.follow_up` uses).
  5. `slot`: a required part is missing: to WHOM (send, email, share, message), which FOLDER (move, copy), what ABOUT (make a pdf / report / deck), WHEN (remind me), WHAT to play ("play something", "kuch bajao"), the new NAME (rename). "Research solar panels and make slides" is not a missing topic (the other clause holds it).
- **The question.** One title, one short question, the candidates JARVIS can really find as buttons, plus a "Something else" text box, Continue and Cancel; voice and typing both work (D63) because it is the same panel form. Candidates: the two apps the owner named; the owner's open windows (title on his panel only); the newest files by name and size (the panel lists them itself from the known folders, never an option a caller wrote; `ask_spec.file_choice` got a backend-only `filter`: name words, a day, extensions, oldest first; nothing matching = the newest files and a note); the known folders; the people JARVIS knows (the names store, D62). The form that goes to the CHAT MODEL (`ambiguity.form`, in the result's `ask`) holds the question and generic labels only: file names, window titles and people's names stay on the owner's panel (D55: "file names go to the owner's panel and nowhere else"). A form never carries a PIN or approves anything.
- **The answer continues the ORIGINAL goal.** `resolve()` puts the choice into the request where the ambiguous part was ("Open notepad or wordpad." + WordPad = "Open WordPad."; "Send the report" + Ravi = "Send the report to Ravi"; "Remind me to drink water" + "in 20 minutes" = "Remind me in 20 minutes to drink water"; a chosen file's full path, quoted when it has a space) and the request goes through the gate again (at most 3 questions, `max_rounds`; then it stops and says so). The resolved text is the owner's own word for the rest of the run (`OWNER_UTTERANCE`, so the helper rule "a path must be one HE wrote" and the kernel's grant read what he chose). "Close everything" is a tick list and runs one close per ticked window. No answer (timeout 90 s, Cancel, closed) = nothing is done and the result says so.
- **Where it runs (`plugins/desktop/gate.py` is the glue).** (1) `do_goal` before the translation and the plan (and again on the English text after a Telugu / Hindi sentence in its own letters was translated); (2) `quick_command`, which the face asks BEFORE any chat model for every command-looking message (the face's `mightBeQuickCommand` now also sends messages that start with delete, send, email, move, copy, rename, make, create, search, find, close, remind ... or hold a Telugu / Hindi verb in English letters; vitest cases added, not run here); (3) `run_pc_task` (the console's door, with the pending-input contract: a re-run with `answers.clarification` puts the answer into the same verdict); (4) the face tool server's `jarvis_do` (`FaceTools.do`, BEFORE the language gate: with `JARVIS_LANGUAGE_GATE=1` that gate turned "Notepad leda Wordpad teruvu" into "Open Notepad." and "ise delete karo" into "Delete ise." and dropped the doubt). When the plugin runs INSIDE the face's tool server (where `owner_ask` is installed, D64) it shows the form on the panel and waits; otherwise the result is `needs_clarification` with the form in `ask` (a field the chat model now sees: `llm_result_fields`) and a summary that says what to do (show it with `jarvis_ask`, then call `desktop__do_goal` again with the original goal and the answer): no persona change was needed (the card is 4,015 characters, `--check` is green). `JARVIS_AMBIGUITY_GATE=0` and `"enabled": false` in the file turn it off.
- **The decider's `ask` outcome ends in the same form.** A planner clarification ("Which one: A or B?", "Which file?", "What should the deck be about?", a folder / file / app below its threshold) used to be a sentence the chat model might leave empty. `gate.finish` attaches a form to it (`ambiguity.clarify_verdict`: "Which one: A or B?" is a two-way choice, "Which file?" lists the newest files, else a text box); with the panel here and NOTHING done yet it is asked at once and the goal runs once more with the answer added as the "(The owner's clarification: ...)" line the planner already reads. A question from the MIDDLE of a task (steps already done: the control loop's own form from `pcagent`) is attached and never re-run, because that would repeat the steps. The control loop's `ask` is also lifted into `do_goal`'s and the quick path's result now (it was dropped).
- **Decision 2: JARVIS never closes its own windows, in four layers.** "Close the window / this / it / everything" must mean the app the owner is working in, never the face, the avatar, the chat or the console, and never a window JARVIS did not open unless he names it. (a) The gate: with no app named it asks which of HIS windows (own windows are removed from the candidates by process and title; a browser tab titled "JARVIS ..." is the console); if the only window is JARVIS's own the question is a text box; a window JARVIS opened (the journal's `close_window` recipes of the last 30 minutes) is what a lone "close it" means, nothing else is guessed, and "close everything" is never decided for him. (b) The kernel: `policy.own_window` (built-in floor `jarvis`, `jarvisface`, `jarvis-face`, dev `electron`, a browser tab titled JARVIS, plus `config/own_windows.json`, which can only ADD) and a rule in `policy.decide`: a `ui` `close`, or the chords alt+f4, ctrl+w, ctrl+q, aimed at an own process is DENY (an owner goal that names JARVIS turns it into the PIN); an ordinary "close notepad" is unchanged. (c) The node: `jarvis_node/ownwin.py`, called by `jobs.ui_action` before the backend, knows each window's process AND title and refuses a close that would land on the face, or on the console's tab when it is the browser's only window (the kernel's list as given plus its own floor if the package cannot be imported, like `sensitive.py`); with several browser windows `windowpick` already refuses a guess. (d) The catalogue handler `close_app`: a generic word ("window", "this", "it", "everything", "app", "tab") is never an app and an own process is refused.
- **Console-protected, like `auto_click.json`.** `ambiguity.json` and `own_windows.json` are in `policy.SAFETY_COMPONENTS` (an agent's write is RED) and in `push_gate.PROTECTED`. A missing or broken `ambiguity.json` falls back to a small built-in floor in `ambiguity.py`, key by key, never to "guess everything"; a broken `own_windows.json` removes nothing.
- **Decision 3: the reliability checks say "asked, did not act".** The `ambiguous_asks` goals already judged the effect from the PC (the `forbid` checks: no window opened, no file changed, no window closed). New: `am-close-window` also forbids that JARVIS's own window closed (severity 2); every ask goal has `verify: ask_form` (the notepad-or-wordpad goal wants the two candidates as choices) and the runner now reports those checks for `ask` goals too (classification unchanged). The face driver now polls the tool server's `jarvis_ask_wanted` while a goal runs and counts a form that was really on the owner's panel (a form already open before the goal does not count), and a `jarvis_ask` call in the chat counts: before, only the string `"kind":"jarvis_ask"` in the chat store did, which the real `jarvis_ask` tool never writes, so a correct question could have been scored `did_not_ask`. Unsafe detection is unchanged (only an extra forbid was added).
- **Numbers (no model, no PC: `python -m jarvis.evals.pc_control.eval_ambiguity`).** FALSE ASKS: 0 of 453 clear requests (156 written for this, 129 of the 130 examples of the simple-task catalogue, 168 act / act_or_ask / pin goals of the reliability gate). The one catalogue example excluded is "post this on twitter": "this" stands for nothing, so it is asked (it is class C and would wait for the PIN anyway). TRUE ASKS: 100 of 100 ambiguous requests asked with the right kind of question (English 66, Telugu in English letters 15, Hindi in English letters 19), including all 15 `ambiguous_asks` goal texts. Honest limit: the rules were tuned on that corpus; a hold-out of 40 ambiguous and 41 clear phrases written afterwards found 3 misses ("write to him", "trash it", "pause or play the music") and 1 false ask ("show me the files in documents"); the false ask and two of the misses were fixed and added to the corpus, "write to him" is still NOT asked; real phrasing will find more, and a miss or a false ask is fixed by adding a line to `ambiguity.json` and a case to `ambiguity_cases.json`, not by code.
- **Tests (this sandbox, 2026-10-27).** New: `plugins/_shared/tests/test_ambiguity.py` (32: the two numbers, every rule, context, forms, rules-as-data, the floor), `plugins/desktop/tests/test_gate.py` (29: the three doors with a fake panel, one round trip on the REAL `AskBroker` (draw, click, id and file path mapped, Cancel), the question budget, the decider's ask, the Telugu-script second pass, the safety rule at the catalogue handler), `kernel/tests/test_own_windows.py` (9), `node/tests/test_ownwin.py` (7), `bridge/test_ambiguity_door.py` (6), six in `evals/reliability/tests/test_rel_drivers.py`; `python -m jarvis.evals.pc_control.eval_ambiguity` prints the numbers. Suites: `plugins/desktop` 1362 passed (+1 deselected: the known `test_core` profile-list test), `plugins/_shared` + `bridge` + `evals` + `node` + `persona` + `tools` all pass except `bridge/test_route_hint.py::...[search the web for pasta recipes]`, which fails after the `_shared` tests in one session at the base commit too (order-dependent, not from this change) and passes alone; `kernel` run from a path without `.claude`: 1599 passed, 3 failed: the 2 known `NodeOpen` tests and `test_lab_report::RunnerContract` (fails only inside the full run, passes alone, also at the base). `python -m jarvis.persona.face_prompt --check` is green (the card is unchanged, 4,015 characters).
- **NOT verified (only the laptop can).** (1) The real panel: the form drawn in the Electron window, voice answers to it, the 90 s wait inside a tool call and the face's 120 s limit for `quick_command`, and that the owner's Stop reaches a goal that is waiting for an answer (the gate runs before the goal is a registered operation). (2) That the chat model sends the owner's WORDS to `desktop__do_goal` (a reworded goal such as "Open Notepad" hides the "or"; the face's quick hook sees his raw words first for command-looking messages, but a message that does not start like a command reaches only the chat model). (3) `OWNER_UTTERANCE` is not set by any host in this repository (it reads the goal given); nothing here depends on it. (4) The real window lists: the face's process name (`jarvis`, from `electron-builder` `executableName`), the process name of Calculator (`CalculatorApp` behind `ApplicationFrameHost`) and how `windowpick` treats a browser with several windows. (5) The reliability harness's panel watcher against the real tool server, and a re-run of `ambiguous_asks` (15 goals x 10): nothing was re-measured. (6) vitest for the two face files (no `node_modules` here; the regexes were checked with plain node). (7) A rogue chat model that guessed the unlisted panel tools could answer its own form (D55 limit 4): for a delete the form's answer becomes the owner's word in the goal, so the real fix remains marking panel calls in the face's MCP route.

## D75 (2026-10-27): a plain question is ANSWERED, not turned into a task: maths, conversions, dates and money are worked out on the PC by exact rules, before any app, model or kernel job; the Calculator opens only when the owner names it
- **Owner's words (2026-10-27):** "it should do complex tasks, not simple ones like open calculator and do 2+3: can't we just do that by talking to JARVIS? What is the need of Calculator, be practical." Rule that follows: what a person can simply ASK is answered directly and at once, with no app window, no model round trip and no kernel job, unless the owner explicitly says to use the app ("in Calculator", "open calculator", "show me on screen").
- **What was wrong.** D54's catalogue entry `calculate` was the main example of a simple task: the chat model called `do_goal`, an online plan was asked for, then the Calculator was opened, the numbers typed, the window read and the answer compared (a drive step; the D60 table shows 4 chat rounds and a plan call for a goal that included Calculator). `convert_units` and `time_now` were catalogue entries that still needed `do_goal`. The reliability corpus used "Open Calculator" as its model of an ordinary task.
- **Decision 1: a direct-answer module, `plugins/_shared/direct_answer.py`** (standard library plus `tenglish.skel`; JARVIS never calls a model for it; no network, no subprocess: tests make `socket` and `subprocess` fail and every answer still comes). `answer(text, now=None) -> Answer | None`: one short sentence with the number and the working in one clause; None means "not mine, the normal path runs". Covers: arithmetic (+ - times divided, powers, brackets, mod, factorial up to 1,000, square and cube roots, `x`, `into`, symbols and words), percentages (X% of Y, up / down by X%, X is what percent of Y, percentage change), discount, GST / VAT, tip, split a bill, EMI (the month-by-month formula is shown), simple and compound interest, sum / average / min / max / gcd / lcm, half / double / twice, number words ("twenty three", "two lakh fifty thousand"), Indian grouping on input and output (1,00,000 and "(7 lakh)"), "in words", commas / Indian commas / rounding, lakh / crore / million conversions, unit and temperature conversions (length, mass, volume, speed, data, time, area, temperature), and from the PC clock: the time with the PC's time-zone name, the date, tomorrow / yesterday, the date n days / weeks / months / years from now or ago, days (or weeks) until / since / between dates, the weekday of a date, leap years, days in a month.
  - **Exact.** Every number is a `fractions.Fraction`; a root or power that is not exact is a `Decimal` at 40 digits. "about" is said only when the printed number is rounded, and the sentence says how ("rounded to 4 decimal places", "to 2 decimal places" for money, "to 6 significant digits"); a non-terminating division also shows the exact fraction ("exactly 1200/7"). Money is exact to the paisa or says it is rounded. Rounding is half-up (2.675 to 2 places is 2.68, which a float cannot say). `0.1 + 0.2` is `0.3`.
  - **No eval, no exec, no `ast` of the owner's text.** A regular expression cuts the text into number / operator / bracket / function tokens and a small recursive-descent parser evaluates them; any other word (a name, `__import__`, a unit inside a sum, a letter variable), an implicit multiplication (`2(3)`), two numbers side by side, a bare `%`, a trailing operator is refused. Caps: 200 characters, 30 digits per number, 120 tokens, 24 brackets deep, an exponent of 1,000 and a result of 60,000 bits, 30 numbers in a list, 50 years of loan.
  - **Never guesses (returns None):** `-2^2` (people disagree), `5 % 3`, `0^0`, `1,5` / `12,34` / `1.000,50` (locale), `03/04/2026` (day first or month first), a bare `555-1234` or `3/4` with nothing that asks a question (a phone number, a date or a fraction), "how many days in a year", a day in kilograms, "5 feet 3 inches", a currency conversion (needs the network), another city's time, "diwali", units mixed with arithmetic, digits of two scripts in one number, invisible characters, letters that only look English (Cyrillic or Greek), a square root of a negative number, anything about an app. A division by zero is the one thing said instead: "Dividing by zero has no answer."
  - **Tenglish / Hinglish.** The operator words (guna, bhaag / bhaginchu, jodo / kalupu, taggindi / tagginchu / ghatao, "ko ... se", "ni ... tho", "mein se ... kam", "aur ... jodo", and the "entha / kitna hota hai / batao / cheppu / cheyyi / karo" endings) are read with `tenglish.skel` (spelling-insensitive: gunaa = guna, bhaag = bhag) and the lexicon's own aux and "tell" words; "2400 ka 15 percent kitna" and "2400 lo 15 percent entha" are 15% of 2,400. Telugu and Hindi in their own letters reach the module as English from the translation layer (D58): tested with a fake translator on the `do_goal` door.
- **Decision 2: where it runs.** `quick.direct()` is asked first by `quick.match()` (before the rules for opening apps), by `quick_command` BEFORE the ambiguity gate (a sum is never a question to ask back), by `do_goal` before the gate and the plan (so the chat model's one tool call finishes with no plan and no kernel job), and by the bridge's `jarvis_do` (a plain question is answered instead of becoming a console task; the message tells the chat model to say it). Switches: `JARVIS_DIRECT_ANSWERS=0` (only this), `JARVIS_QUICK_PATH=0` and safe mode (D71) turn it off with the other speed shortcuts. The Calculator is used only when the owner NAMES it or the screen: `explicit_app()` (calculator, calc, notepad, excel, screen, window, app, open / launch / type / save ...) returns None and the catalogue's `calculate` entry runs as before. That entry now also matches "open calculator and do 2+3", "use the calculator to work out ...", "show me 23 times 47 on screen"; its class (A), its handler (`drive`), its check against the Calculator window and `needs_laptop` are unchanged; a bare "calculate 12 times 7" no longer matches it. `convert_units` and `time_now` stay as the fallback when the quick path is off. No new tool, no new persona line: `CHAT_TOOLS` and the persona are byte for byte what they were (bridge snapshot test and `face_prompt --check` green).
- **Decision 3: the face asks the hook for these messages.** `mightBeQuickCommand` (`quick-intent.ts`) gained one clause: a number or number word next to a maths / unit / money word, or a message that starts like a date question, is sent to `desktop__quick_command` before any chat model. The hook answers `handled: false` at once when it is not maths ("I have 3 apples" costs one fast local call).
- **Decision 4: the reliability corpus.** New capability `direct_answers` (64 goals, face driver only): 54 goals where the final answer must hold the right number (English 43, Tenglish 5, Hinglish 6, dates and the clock through new harness fields `{plus45}` and `{xmas_days}`), a Calculator window or a PC-task tool call is a severity 3 violation, and the new check `model_requests` (max 0, cause `model_called`) fails a goal that needed a model round trip; and 10 goals (tag `explicit_app`) where the owner names the Calculator or the screen and the window must open. The expected numbers are re-derived in `tests/test_rel_direct_answers.py` with `Fraction` / `Decimal` (an EMI from a month-by-month loan, a mile from the exact 1,609.344 metres), the goal regexes must accept them in plain, western-comma and Indian-comma notation and reject their neighbours, and no expected number may already stand in the question. The 90% rule, the zero-unsafe rule and the other 16 capabilities are untouched. The two catalogue tests that count capabilities (17) and goals (266) were updated.
- **Numbers (this sandbox, 2026-10-27, no model, no PC).** (1) The 54 direct goals run through the REAL quick path and the harness' own runner on the pretend PC: **54 of 54 pass (100%)**, 0 model requests, 0 windows, 0 task tools. (2) 5,000 random expressions with + - times / and brackets, compared with plain Python `Fraction`: **0 wrong, 0 unanswered** (3,123 of them non-terminating, each checked against an independent half-up rounding); 300 of the same kind are in the unit tests. (3) 428 unit cases for the evaluator (`plugins/_shared/tests/test_direct_answer.py`: correctness across arithmetic, Indian numbers, percentages, money, EMI against a month-by-month loan, conversions, temperatures, dates and the clock, Tenglish and Hinglish; 140 refused questions (126 plain, 14 spoofed) including `__import__`, `2**99999`, `10**10**10`, `9^9^9`, 5,000-character input, 3,000 nested brackets, Arabic-Indic / Devanagari / fullwidth digits, mixed-script numbers, Cyrillic and Greek lookalikes, zero-width and bidi characters, locale separators; 2,000 random garbage strings that must never raise; no network, no subprocess, no eval / exec / import in the module's syntax tree); 43 for the wiring (`test_direct_answer_wiring.py`: the router, the doors, the switches, safe mode, the Tenglish hook, the translated Telugu / Hindi path, an explicit "in Calculator" still reaching the `calculate` entry, no plan and no job); 10 for `jarvis_do`; 108 for the corpus; 25 added cases in `quick-reply.test.ts` (21 asked, 4 not). Time: p50 0.3 ms, p99 0.8 ms per question (302 different questions).
- **Tests run (per directory):** `plugins/desktop` 1408 passed, 1 skipped, 1 failed (the known `test_core.py` profile-list test, a Windows path); `plugins/_shared` all green; `bridge` 273; `persona` 35 and `python -m jarvis.persona.face_prompt --check` green; `evals` all; `tools` 294. The face's vitest could not be run (no `node_modules` in this sandbox): the "when the router is worth asking" block of `quick-reply.test.ts` (57 cases, 25 new) was run against the real `quick-intent.ts` with vitest 3.2.4 installed in a scratch folder: 57 passed. `simple_tasks.json` is in `push_gate.PROTECTED` and stays there (it is not in `policy.SAFETY_COMPONENTS` and was not before); a push of this range needs the owner's RED approval for that file and for the test files.
- **NOT verified (only the laptop can).** (1) The real face: that the message reaches `desktop__quick_command` through the tool server, that the reply is shown and spoken, and how a text-to-speech voice reads "°C", "x 10^60" and "P x r x (1+r)^n / ((1+r)^n - 1)" (words were used for the operators; the EMI formula line may be better left unspoken). (2) That the face driver counts `requests` as 0 when the hook answers; if it counts the hook call, `model_requests` fails all 54 goals with `model_called` (change the driver, not the goals). (3) The time-zone name on Windows (`datetime.now().astimezone().tzname()` gives the Windows name; the sentence shows it in brackets). (4) The ten Calculator goals, and `needs_laptop: true` for the `calculate` entry. (5) Telugu and Hindi in their own letters with the real translation models; Tenglish phrasing beyond the lexicon (the Telugu and Hindi speaker should add the forms he really says to `_OPWORDS`). (6) Data sizes count 1,024 per step as Windows does and say so; if the owner wants 1,000 (disk makers), it is one table. (7) Not done: a currency conversion (needs a network and a source), another city's time (no zone database is assumed on Windows), holidays that move (Diwali), a compound unit such as "5 feet 3 inches", and a question that reaches the server's Hermes agent by any door other than `jarvis_do` or the face.

## D80 (2026-10-27): speed, round two — a bridge-side tool-list cache, a widened redaction cache, today's re-check of the no-thinking switch (a better guess, still unconfirmed through Dahl), and two leads investigated and left alone with the reason why

- **Owner's words (2026-10-27, after clarifying "make JARVIS past" meant "faster"):** make JARVIS faster, investigate and fix what is safely fixable without touching the files other agents are editing (`plugins/desktop/{goal,composite,missions,pcagent}.py`, `config/simple_tasks.json`, `plugins/_shared/direct_answer.py`, `face/apps/stage-tamagotchi/src`, `lab/`, the vm2 modules).
- **What is "measured" below and what is not.** This session had no laptop, no real model and no real Presidio/GLiNER service; `presidio_analyzer` + `spacy` + `en_core_web_sm` ARE installed here, so a few findings are checked against the REAL detector (named below), but GLiNER is not. The one genuinely real speed measurement used throughout is the laptop's own, already committed to this repo: `jarvis/evals/reliability/results/latency-{before,after,compare}-2026-10-26.*`, `latency-deepseek-v4-flash-2026-10-26.txt` and `STATUS.md`'s Phase 2 (D60's own before/after run, Dahl GLM-5.3-Flash pinned, fresh face per run, 5 goals x 5). From it: `done` p50 22.9 -> 13.2 s (met, target 15), p90 61.7 -> 28.6 s (met, target 30); model rounds per turn p50 2 -> 1; `first_token_no_tool`/`model_first` p50 1.2 -> 6.1 s / 1.5 -> 5.5 s (NOT met, target 2 s, and WORSE after D60's own change, "a fresh face per run"; 2.1/3.4 s turn 1/2 of one running session); `done_with_tools` p50 22.7 s; gateway `first_ms` p50 1.2 s but `total_ms` p50 8.0 s / p90 22.8 s ("GLM's hidden thinking"); gateway `redact_ms` p50 335 -> 275 ms (close to the 351 quoted to this session; a different sampling moment), 1,696 hits / 87 misses (about 95%) in the real `/health` snapshot. A pinned `deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731` run (3 goals) is also already in the repo: `first_ms` p50 2.3 s (GLM 1.2 s), `total_ms` p50 7.3 s (8.0 s), `done` p50 10.1 s but p90 82 s and "model decides the tool" p50 26.2 s; STATUS.md's own conclusion stands: "not better, NOT adopted; GLM stays first." Nothing here re-ran that protocol; it was read and reused, cited by file name throughout.
- **1. Cold-face first token: measured, not guessed, that the cost is the MODEL reading more bytes, not local assembly.** The committed Phase 2 `compare` file shows the regression precisely: D60's own "direct tools" change (22 named tools instead of 2 proxy tools) made the FIRST chat request about twice as big (`req_bytes` 8.5 -> 16.0 KB, matching D60's own 8,754 / 15,800-byte table), and `first_token_no_tool` grew by about the same shape (1.2 -> 6.1 s) as a reasoning model reading roughly double the prompt would predict. That is model prompt-processing time on the wire, which no local cache can shrink.
